# Design And Performance Ledger

Updated: 2026-09-20. This is the maintained decision record. Detailed historical evidence remains in [VALIDATION.md](VALIDATION.md); raw experiment reports are retained unchanged. A design label describes intent, while source/challenge hashes identify what actually ran. Never infer improvement from a passing test suite, a lower training loss, or a single successful demonstration.

## Current Answer

### Measured passage history — `passage-memory-v1` (2026-09-20)

Hypothesis: the maze's repeated travel is partly caused by short model-visible history and destination ranking that does not retain the identity of previously travelled passages. This candidate adds a bounded graph of observed places actually reached, directed passages actually travelled, and directional branch attempts. Stable place IDs survive revisits and saved-map/checkpoint reloads; frontier grid IDs may change without discarding nearby branch attempt history. This first stage uses measured waypoints, not semantic claims that every waypoint is a junction or a dead end.

History is updated from localized odometry and accumulated sensor-map free space in the worker. Stationary scans/turns do not increment visit counts. Unobserved links, wall crossings, discontinuous motion and changed map frames cannot manufacture connections. Graph size is bounded to 512 places / 2,048 directed passages / 4,096 directional branch records, with saturation reported rather than silently evicting old visits. Saved history never restores an active route or pending offer; a fresh map starts empty, and skipping reuse retains earlier saved maps.

Before a measured loop, the existing exploration ranking is preserved. After a cycle or repeated directed passage, exploration considers the existing sensor-derived information-gain candidates, with soft penalties for revisited destinations and repeated local no-progress attempts. Candidates still belong to the currently connected inflated free-space component, and selected paths still pass normal worker revalidation. Penalties never ban return routes; if search candidates are unavailable, ordinary frontiers remain a fallback. Region-scoped requests retain their region boundary. The graph never directly executes movement, changes budgets/leases or reads maze geometry/scoring.

Qwen receives a compact current-place/loop/branch summary through AgentObservation, plus candidate visit/attempt metadata. The complete map history stays in the backend. A visit is explicitly distinguished from finishing exploration; blocked/no-progress attempts do not establish a dead end. All previous JSON recovery and Stop/reset/disconnect controls remain in place.

Initial software checks: 15 memory tests and four neighboring frontier tests passed. A broader map integration slice passed 35 tests. Worker authority/clearance regressions passed 27 tests. A real-physics test with scripted inference passed in 58.80 seconds and verified that physically travelled passages reach the model's paired feedback and remain available after stopping. Final map/frame/checkpoint checks and a complete fixed baseline series are being recorded; real-model maze effects are not yet measured.

### Malformed local mission response recovery — `mission-json-recovery-v1` (2026-09-20)

The complex-maze diagnostic below ended on a JSON parsing exception with approximately 119 seconds of mission time remaining. The adapter threw before returning usage or recording the response, so the exact malformed content/finish reason was lost and the executive's existing decision-correction loop was bypassed. Hypothesis: retaining a non-executable response with usage and bounded diagnostics permits safe recovery from an isolated malformed or truncated reply; it does not solve maze exploration quality.

The Ollama unified-mission adapter now returns no function calls for malformed/non-object/incomplete decisions. It retains at most 2,000 characters of visible response text, parse location, content length and provider finish reason, excluding thinking. The executive charges the ordinary request/token budgets, closes any active mapped objective, waits stationary, and captures fresh paired evidence for the next decision. It stops after three consecutive rejected responses, at the existing request/turn/token/deadline limits, or on Stop/authority loss. There is no text repair, hidden adapter retry, budget increase, model fallback, route change or access to evaluator geometry. Missing usage and transport/protocol failures remain fatal. Existing schema/action validation still applies to every corrected decision.

[Focused scripted checks](../.runtime/mission-json-recovery-v1/focused-final.xml): **19 passed in 148.17 s**, covering invalid/empty/fenced/non-object/oversized/truncated output, usage and thinking privacy, successful correction from fresh evidence, stopping an active physical route before retry, three-attempt termination, missing usage, request/token boundaries, and a late provider reply after Stop. Initial test-harness failures (empty synthetic model input and parsing the combined image-role text as a single JSON document) are retained in [first focused results](../.runtime/mission-json-recovery-v1/focused.xml); fixtures were corrected. A later assertion expected a diagnostic code in the compact model payload, which deliberately retains the explanatory reason instead; the assertion was corrected.

The [complete fixed household series](../.runtime/performance/mission-json-recovery-v1-20260920/summary.md), using `hybrid-default-v1-20260920` as the existing baseline, reports **18 passed / 0 failed / 3 unchanged missing-room prerequisites**, all 21 attempts retained. Original map and frozen source remained unchanged; the [comparison](../.runtime/performance/mission-json-recovery-v1-20260920/comparison.json) preserves per-task outcomes. This is scripted executive evidence without model calls. The remaining [Ollama regression checks](../.runtime/mission-json-recovery-v1/ollama-regression.xml) passed **101 tests in 560.24 s**, with the five pipeline cases already included in the focused set deselected; existing dependency/JUnit warnings remain. This gives 120 distinct targeted software checks, not a full backend/frontend-suite result.

The [real complex-maze rerun report](../.runtime/mission-json-recovery-v1/REPORT.md) confirms recovery from an actual Qwen truncation: a live API read at turn 24 showed `done_reason=length`, 4,795 characters and an unfinished JSON string, followed by normal acting with no controller error. The rolling 200-event trace later evicted that early response; the report preserves the observed diagnostic and explicitly distinguishes it from retained raw-trace evidence. The run ended safely on its unchanged 300-second mission deadline: **63 Qwen requests / 64 entered turns, 16.405 m, 306.329 recorded wall seconds, zero contacts, 3,123 complete unassisted samples, zero drops**. Usage remained known: Qwen 402,472 input + 1,625 output tokens, Luna 1,991 + 230 across two requests. All 46 live-manifest source hashes still matched. **The exit was not reached.** Recorded positions visited six maze cells and still show revisiting; the formatting crash is repaired, while reliable exploration/completion remains unproven. No route, scoring, budget or model changes were made during this diagnostic, and no causal performance improvement is claimed.

### Complex maze scenario — `maze-complex-v1` (2026-09-20)

- **Hypothesis and scope:** a separate fixed 6 × 6 maze adds loop recognition and more branch choices to the original exploration task. It contains 36 cells, 37 internal passages, two independent loops, ten junctions and eight dead ends. The shortest route along corridor centres from the start to the outside bay is 27 m. The 1.8 m cell spacing, wall dimensions and full-footprint/grounded/stationary one-second completion rule remain the same. The original `maze` preset and existing controller, navigation guards and fixed benchmark inputs are unchanged.
- **Implementation:** additive `maze_complex` preset and frontend selection/persistence/overview support. New selections default to no saved-map reuse without deleting existing maps. Fixed geometry remains evaluator-only; models receive ordinary sensor observations and the task goal. Tests derive connectivity from physical ray intersections and drive a privileged test-only route through a dead end, backtrack and exit. This scripted evidence cannot establish autonomous success.
- **Scripted validation:** **8 passed in 74.28 s**, covering both mazes' topology and completion scoring, collision-free wheel-driven exit/backtracking feasibility, preset contracts and complex-maze model-input isolation: [physics results](../.runtime/maze-complex-v1/physics.xml). The frontend production build passed with its existing large-chunk warning. The isolated port-8001 browser test **passed in 1.4 minutes**, covering loading, goal propagation, map defaults, reset, persistence, unchanged fixed regression selection and desktop/mobile rendering: [browser log](../.runtime/maze-complex-v1/browser.txt), [desktop](../.runtime/maze-exploration-v1/maze_complex-scene-1440.png), [mobile](../.runtime/maze-exploration-v1/maze_complex-scene-390.png), [preview](../frontend/public/scenario-previews/maze_complex.webp). This is targeted validation, not a full-suite pass.
- **Real-model protocol:** the requested live test uses Qwen/none plus Luna/low, an empty map, 160 decisions/requests, 1.2M Qwen tokens and the existing 300 s mission limit. It is a separate interactive diagnostic, not a matched comparison or fixed household benchmark. An isolated updated app on port 8008 preserves the earlier instance after automatic approval review rejected its restart command. Results and artifacts will be recorded below.
- **Measured real-model outcome:** **failed**, ending at 36 controller turns with `Ollama returned invalid mission JSON; no motion executed.` The independent scorer remained incomplete. Recorded travel **8.457317 m**, duration **172.5 s**, **zero contacts**, complete/unassisted **1,865 samples with zero drops**. The trajectory entered four cells near the start, then returned toward the entrance corridor. A larger maze and extra travel do not establish success. The invalid raw response is not present as a response event, so its exact formatting defect is unverified. [Full result and replays](../.runtime/maze-complex-v1/REPORT.md), [native report](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260920T184020Z-6c38b87c/maze_complex/report.json), [manifest](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260920T184020Z-6c38b87c/experiment.json). Source SHA-256 `f6969d2b9a1b79a69fa4a36e527508d822bf2e8d2792a30eb8ffe20cec04f763`; challenge SHA-256 `91cf1bb6a3135394e66cbee3021fb83fe560c0cdc3fdc584b0b70fa53d51ddf8`. All 46 recorded source hashes matched after the attempt. One earlier server exited during scene loading before inference; a replacement with captured logs completed this attempt. No code, goal, scorer, budget or safety validation was changed in response to the failure.

### Maze exploration scenario — `maze-exploration-v1` (2026-09-20)

- **Hypothesis:** a fixed maze with branches, dead ends and an initially occluded exit provides a repeatable challenge for sensor-based exploration and backtracking.
- **Scope:** new standalone `maze` preset, challenge selection/persistence, spectator framing and preview. Existing navigation/control logic and fixed benchmark suites are unchanged. The 4 x 4 layout has one physical exit; scene topology and exit coordinates remain evaluator-only. Selecting Maze defaults to no saved map without deleting any existing maps.
- **Completion:** the whole base and both wheels must be grounded and stationary in the exterior green bay for one uninterrupted simulated second. Seeing the exit, reaching its threshold or merely claiming completion does not pass the scorer.
- **Evidence:** **95 passed** across `tests/test_challenges.py`, `tests/test_maze.py` and `tests/test_preferences.py` (466.13 s; two dependency deprecation warnings). This includes maze topology, scoring, real-wheel backtracking/exit feasibility, model-input isolation and existing scenario/persistence regressions. [Physics log](../.runtime/maze-exploration-v1/pytest-scenarios.txt). The frontend production build passed. The final Playwright maze test **passed** (1.7 minutes including fixture startup), covering loading, goal propagation, no-map default, reset, saved selection, thumbnail loading and desktop/mobile rendering: [browser log](../.runtime/maze-exploration-v1/playwright-maze-serial.txt), [test](../frontend/tests/lab.spec.ts), [desktop view](../.runtime/maze-exploration-v1/scene-1440.png), [mobile view](../.runtime/maze-exploration-v1/scene-390.png), [scenario thumbnail](../frontend/public/scenario-previews/maze.webp).
- **Validation limits:** the broad Python run was interrupted after 144 passing checks and replaced by the completed 95-test scenario/persistence suite. The full browser suite could not start because another session owned port 8001; its server was preserved. A temporary test-only override of the existing scripted fixture used port 18001. Its first rerun hit the 90 s test deadline at reset during concurrent physics work; the final successful rerun used a 120 s deadline after physics finished. Neither partial run is a full-suite pass. No production port or controller settings were changed.
- **Real-model diagnostic — `maze-hybrid-diagnostic-v1`:** two fresh, isolated no-map trials used Qwen/none plus Luna/low task supervision, the original maze goal/scorer and unchanged control code. Each had a 300 s wall-time allowance, 30 Qwen decisions/requests and 200k Qwen tokens; Luna had a separate cap of four requests/100k tokens. Both profiles responded; neither was substituted. Stationary vision preparation preceded each mission. Source SHA-256 `d9ab515fb36195a79a5b0ad2da8cb2a659e745f02c41dbc2245a6f22f71ab075`; challenge SHA-256 `f39d6abffe87d15e0cd3a22857f99f33591a8bb23fcf5094c9650a6f35f471f5`; neither trial detected source drift. [Runner](../scripts/evaluate_maze.py), [frozen manifest](../.runtime/performance/maze-hybrid-20260920-tests/experiment.json), [results and replay links](../.runtime/performance/maze-hybrid-20260920-tests/REPORT.md).
- **Measured outcome: 0/2 reached the exit.** Trial 1 failed after **137.968 s / 6.367217 m**, exhausting 30 decisions at the first corridor's dead end. Its decisions were seven frontier selections, 14 exploration requests, seven looks and two observation reports; a claimed exit sighting did not pass independent physics. Trial 2 failed after **109.265 s / 4.871378 m**, with 24 decisions and the controller error `UNREACHABLE: current footprint is not in mapped free space` after three rejections. Both recordings are complete and unassisted, with **zero contacts and dropped records**, and neither physically completed the goal. Luna needed two requests per trial to correct an object plan to the required place/parking plan. These are retained task failures, not provider-availability failures or successful navigation.
- **Follow-up checks and limits:** the three dedicated maze physics tests passed again in **20.58 s** before autonomous testing. Scripted feasibility uses privileged test-only waypoints and is not autonomous navigation evidence. Two same-layout bounded diagnostics do not establish reliability, exploration efficiency, a comparative improvement or a fixed household benchmark. The configured Luna deployment name does not verify its underlying model identity. Real autonomous exit-finding remains unproven. The first trial ended on the decision cap before its time cap; the second ended on a planning rejection. No goal, scorer, production budget or motion guard was relaxed to obtain a pass.
- **Budget/progress analysis:** the diagnostic's **30-decision cap was lower than Maze's suggested 160 turns**; success with the suggested allowance has not been tested. Trial 1 still had about 162 s left; trial 2 about 191 s. Qwen replies took approximately 1.1–1.8 s. A decision-cap increase alone would encounter the 200k-token cap (173,268 Qwen tokens used in trial 1). Both trajectories remained in entrance-row cells (0,0) through (3,0), without entering the next corridor. Straight-line distance to the bay centre fell from 8.999 m to 5.327 m / 6.281 m, but it crosses walls and is not a navigable remaining distance. The second trial's observed-map footprint-clearance rejection is a separate failure; its underlying mapping cause remains undiagnosed.
- **User-observed run launched at 18:04 UTC — `maze-observed-160-v1`:** started the live app and a fresh no-map Maze mission using Qwen/none with Luna/low supervision, 160 decisions/requests, 1.2M Qwen tokens and the unchanged 300 s mission ceiling. Hypothesis: permit observation beyond the earlier 30-decision cap; mapping recovery is unchanged. The browser visibly confirmed an active mission and recording. [Launch record](../.runtime/performance/maze-observed-20260920-200433-2beb4a/launch.json) and [exact request](../.runtime/performance/maze-observed-20260920-200433-2beb4a/request.json). This entry records launch only: no completion result or improvement claim is available at handoff. This is an interactive real-model run with different budgets, not a matched comparison or fixed benchmark; any subsequent operator intervention must remain labelled in its automatic session recording.

**Exploration request remains unmet; autonomous retries paused (2026-09-20).** Twelve retained real development attempts across six iterative designs have not produced clean controller-plus-physics completion for both requested challenges. All ten original-budget attempts failed. One of two separately labelled extended-budget diagnostics passed cube physics, but its controller kept waiting until its request cap; kitchen also failed with the extended allowance. All twelve recordings are complete/unassisted with zero contacts or dropped records. These are heterogeneous diagnostics, not a pooled benchmark or reliability estimate. No default budget, goal, scene, scorer or safety gate was relaxed. The latest original-budget pair is detailed below. Full fixed-series qualification and reliable real-model success remain unverified; unrelated regression cases stayed deferred.

**Two exploration challenges, active development (2026-09-20), `exploration-inspection-v1`:** user explicitly requested retesting the yellow-cube approach and Find the Kitchen until both succeed. The next candidate addresses the reproduced empty `look` loop: both provider schemas require explicit yaw/pitch, and production-model stopped looks within 0.05 rad of the current head pose are rejected with corrective feedback. Fresh current images already accompany every review. Safe wait, moving stop-and-reobserve deferral, legacy refresh calls, goal/geometry/scoring and all worker motion guards remain unchanged. [Thirteen focused inspection/arrival tests](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/inspection-tests.xml) and [one moving-inspection worker test](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/moving-inspection.xml) passed, model-free.

The [existing diagnostic runner](../.runtime/kitchen-focus-v1/run.py) now accepts either `apartment` or `flat_kitchen`, records exact settings/source hashes and every failure, and distinguishes independent physical status from `clean_success` (physical pass plus controller completion). Each attempt uses fresh isolated knowledge, the complete original goal, Qwen/none plus one Luna/low task review, two images, 8192-token history and frozen code. Initial limits: cube **180 s / 12 requests / 80k tokens**, kitchen **180 s / 60 requests / 400k tokens**. No automatic model fallback or evaluator geometry is available to control. These are labelled development diagnostics, not full-suite benchmark or reliability claims; unrelated regression cases remain deferred. Isolated trial previews leave the user's port-8008 failed scene and unsent draft intact.

First frozen candidate pair **both failed**, complete/unassisted/zero contacts and drops. [Cube](../.runtime/performance/exploration-inspection-v1-cube-20260920/report.json): **2.923853 m / 90.438 s**, 1,108 samples, all twelve decisions were exploration renewals, no target selection; decision cap reached. [Kitchen](../.runtime/performance/exploration-inspection-v1-kitchen-20260920/report.json): **11.845197 m / 185.843 s recording-inclusive**, 1,883 samples, original mission deadline reached, no kitchen receipt. Kitchen produced an explicit `look` at yaw 0.5 / pitch 0.3 then selected another frontier; that mechanism was exercised, but the final route was unreachable in the observed map. Neither final image established its target. More travel is not a pass.

Candidate `exploration-cadence-v2` extends the existing room-only 10 s minimum periodic review to object search, retaining the 0.75 m travel trigger, measured-latency deadline reserve, feedback-rate changes and all worker safety monitoring. Hypothesis: avoid exhausting object decisions on frequent unchanged exploration renewals before target handling. Other task pacing, budgets and pass criteria remain unchanged. [Ten focused cadence checks](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/search-cadence.xml) and [23 combined contract checks](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/candidate-v2.xml) passed. The [real cube trial](../.runtime/performance/exploration-cadence-v2-cube-20260920/report.json) **failed**: **2.623321 m / 86.344 s**, 1,085 complete/unassisted samples, zero contacts/drops. It reached selection after three explores and two directed looks, but three boxes were invalid (out-of-range, reversed or zero-area); correction limit exhausted. No approach was executed. Kitchen was not rerun on this intermediate candidate because its behavior was unchanged by the object-only cadence edit.

Candidate `exploration-grounding-v3` reuses the existing bounded current-depth region IDs for `select_object` and `verify_object`, not just circle. Both require current visual evidence; resolution keeps the existing frame/run/age checks and forbids a replacement box. Model identity judgments remain unverified; regions can merge nearby surfaces. Legacy valid-box support remains. [Six candidate/authority/arrival contract checks](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/object-candidates.xml) passed. [One enhanced-physics integration](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/candidate-approach-physics.xml) passed: a scripted visual responder selected a current observed region, approached through the existing worker, verified against a new region ID, and returned Home. This is model-free workflow evidence, not real Qwen recognition.

The v3 frozen pair **both failed**, with original settings, complete unassisted recordings, zero contacts/drops and no source drift. [Cube](../.runtime/performance/exploration-grounding-v3-cube-20260920/report.json): **4.815165 m / 121.328 s**, 1,588 samples; eight explores, three looks and one frontier selection exhausted twelve decisions, no object selection. The unchanged-look guard rejected repeated directions and Qwen eventually chose a frontier, but the last decision left no budget for approach. [Kitchen](../.runtime/performance/exploration-grounding-v3-kitchen-20260920/report.json): **15.579755 m / 186.266 s recording-inclusive**, 2,133 samples; 25 Qwen decisions, no room report, original mission deadline reached. The final head view is a corridor, not identified cooking fixtures. Increased travel and passing candidate-ID software checks do not establish either requested challenge's success. All five active-request real attempts remain failures; do not pool them into a frozen success-rate comparison.

Bounded follow-up protocol `exploration-grounding-v3-extended`: after asking about diagnostic limits, the user delegated autonomous decisions while unavailable. Ran **one additional attempt per challenge**, **300 s / 60 Qwen decisions / 400k Qwen tokens**, on the same frozen v3 code. Hypothesis: distinguish insufficient original allowance from persistent ineffective search/recognition. Other settings, original goals/scorers and safety checks remained unchanged; production and regression defaults were NOT increased. These changed-budget diagnostics remain separate from original-budget outcomes and cannot establish an original-budget pass. [Cube](../.runtime/performance/exploration-grounding-v3-extended-cube-20260920/report.json) **physically passed** at 69.078 s but **controller completion failed**, exhausting 60 decisions after 259.156 s with repeated waits and no object selection. Travel 2.535937 m; 2,438 complete unassisted samples, zero contacts/drops. [Kitchen](../.runtime/performance/exploration-grounding-v3-extended-kitchen-20260920/report.json) **failed** at its time cap: 24.080521 m / 307.610 s recording-inclusive, 3,373 complete unassisted samples, zero contacts/drops. Extra allowance did not resolve either end-to-end workflow.

Candidate `exploration-current-evidence-v4`: an [offline paired replay](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/history-replay.json) of the archived stopped-cube head frame and exact typed observed-map image reproduced the identical wait with six prior action/result pairs. Removing only those pairs produced `select_object` with the current region ID and visual evidence. No motion occurred. Hypothesis: repeated assistant claims reinforce an obsolete action despite fresh feedback. Local Ollama object/room reviews now omit prior assistant-action pairs while retaining current typed sensors, execution feedback, plan/receipts, measured episode memory and saved observations. Cloud, circuit, place, movement, legacy and pre-plan context handling remain unchanged. Trace records the effective history mode; no budgets/scorers/safety changes. One offline pair is diagnostic evidence, not measured navigation improvement; original-budget real results follow.

V4 [eight scripted guarded-worker checks](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/current-evidence-guards.xml) passed, including current-only local input, unchanged cloud history, stale/wrong-image rejection and Stop. Both original-budget real attempts failed. [Cube](../.runtime/performance/exploration-current-evidence-v4-cube-20260920/report.json): Luna returned `kind=explore` for the explicit object-approach goal; unchanged validation rejected it before any Qwen call (199 samples, 22.516 s, 0.001204 m, zero contacts/drops). [Kitchen](../.runtime/performance/exploration-current-evidence-v4-kitchen-20260920/report.json): 26 current-only Qwen reviews, **16.971366 m / 186.297 s**, 2,221 complete unassisted samples, zero contacts/drops; no kitchen receipt before the deadline. Reasons now mention some changing visible furniture, but this is not proof of adequate perception or search. Initial v4 launch hit server-not-ready before trial creation/inference and was retried only after readiness confirmation.

Candidate `exploration-task-kind-v5` adds explicit task-supervisor instructions distinguishing generic exploration from searching for a named object/room and preserving approach/entry completion. No validator relaxation, extra Luna request or plan substitution. Hypothesis: prevent the observed upstream `explore` misclassification so Qwen can exercise the current-evidence object pipeline. Kitchen search remains unresolved; the real cube result follows.

V5 [five task-contract checks](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/task-kind.xml) passed. The [real cube diagnostic](../.runtime/performance/exploration-task-kind-v5-cube-20260920/report.json) **failed**: accepted correct Luna object/arrive plan, twelve Qwen decisions, **5.250548 m / 142.563 s**, 1,900 complete unassisted samples, zero contacts/drops. Three replies said the cube was visible but chose `explore`, which cannot steer toward it. No selection/approach occurred. The prompt clarification was exercised once; reliability is not established.

Candidate `exploration-target-priority-v6` resolves the conflicting route-continuation guidance by checking the current target before continuing exploration. Visible object arrival selects its observed region; visible room fixtures trigger a report. Existing moving stop/reobserve deferral remains authoritative. No identity is inferred from reason text, no automatic target selection is introduced, and safety/budgets/scoring are unchanged. Hypothesis: prevent continued generic exploration after target recognition. The [archived moving-frame replay](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/target-priority-replay.json) changed the old `explore` into a current candidate-ID selection; this was non-actuating and not a live success. [Ten focused worker/inspection/arrival checks](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/target-priority-guards.xml) passed.

The final frozen original-budget pair **both failed**, with complete unassisted recordings and zero contacts/drops. [Cube](../.runtime/performance/exploration-target-priority-v6-cube-20260920/report.json): **7.620128 m / 142.063 s**, 1,994 samples, twelve explores with no target selection; request cap exhausted. [Kitchen](../.runtime/performance/exploration-target-priority-v6-kitchen-20260920/report.json): **15.977067 m / 186.250 s recording-inclusive**, 2,185 samples, 25 Qwen decisions; near the deadline it reported refrigerator/countertop and then stove evidence, but no room navigation/verification receipt or physical entry completion followed. This late report does not establish reliable visual identity or adequate search efficiency. More retries on this candidate were stopped. The earlier v3 kitchen recording's maximum sample gap was 2.532 s; zero dropped records does not imply uniform sampling.

Final wording safeguard `exploration-target-priority-v6a` makes target priority stage-aware: select only an unselected object, retain a selected goal for approach/verification, and retain the report/navigate/observe room sequence. [Focused final checks](../.runtime/exploration-retry-v1/final-stage-guards.xml) cover the instruction contract and existing handoff/arrival guards. No real model run has used this final wording; its navigation effect is **not measured**. The retained code is a development candidate, not a qualified improvement. Remaining work needs target-directed search and dependable current-frame recognition/receipt progression under original limits, not further automatic rerolls or unsupported success claims.

**Latest yellow-cube user trial failed (2026-09-20), `object-arrival-feedback-v1-user-trial`:** the next user-started run on port 8008, session `e4a0cfba-2bb6-485a-89d2-6155294e0d04`, **did not complete**. It used **12/12 primary requests and 72,813/100,000 tokens**, ending `limited` after **84.172 s recorded wall / 46.10 s simulated**, with **1.634336 m** travel. The [scorecard](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260920T144115Z-d9b07653/apartment/recording/scorecard.json) has 980 complete, unassisted samples, zero contacts/drops and false final physical success. The [manifest](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260920T144115Z-d9b07653/experiment.json) labels real-model evidence and reports no source changes during the run. This is a retained user development trial, not a predeclared matched benchmark.

The [trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260920T144115Z-d9b07653/apartment/trace.json) confirms the new `object_arrival` feedback reached Qwen. Seven `explore` decisions were followed by five identical `look` decisions; no target selection, approach or verification occurred. The local task failed at its existing third route rejection: `OBSERVED_PATH_BLOCKED: Inspect the route before continuing`. The last moving reply was discarded after the route ended. Four frontier alternatives were supplied at the stopped review, but the model continued inspecting instead of selecting one. This is an exploration/recovery failure before target approach, not the previous unsupported within-0.9-m claim.

The repeated `look` arguments omitted both `yaw_rad` and `pitch_rad`. The current schema makes them optional and MissionDecision defaults them to **0 / 0.2 radians**, already the measured head pose. Reviews 9-12 retained essentially the same base pose and head angles. The [terminal head image](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260920T144115Z-d9b07653/apartment/terminal.png) shows wall/floor and a corner of the blue furniture, not the yellow target. Fresh capture time alone did not provide a changed inspection view. Final termination was the request cap; tokens remained, and the 46.10 s display is simulated time, not a 46 s mission limit.

**Conclusion:** the prior arrival-feedback mitigation is active but insufficient for task completion. Its intended target-approach transition was not exercised here, and this different route/settings run cannot isolate a causal improvement or regression. Next candidate should require explicit inspection directions and detect repeated unchanged views while retaining safe waits, blocked-path rejection and observed-frontier selection. No additional runtime edit, inference, reset, restart or robot command was performed during this diagnosis; the user's scene and unsent instruction draft remain untouched. Unrelated regressions remain deferred.

**Premature yellow-cube stop (2026-09-20), `object-arrival-feedback-v1`:** the user's port-8006 screenshot matches the object-search case in [sequence 24291ee0](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v2-20260920T134915Z-24291ee0/sequence.json), session `eb88dc1c-4624-4da8-8043-bf6bb13006ce`. The independent final range was **1.188721 m**, **0.288721 m outside** the unchanged 0.9 m requirement. Target visibility and stopped/camera-steady checks were true, but inspection dwell was zero. The controller ended after **85.391 s** with **12 decisions exhausted**, using **69,922 input + 372 output = 70,294 tokens** below its 80k threshold. The subsequent reset/sequence-ended UI label is not the original termination cause.

The [recorded trace](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v2-20260920T134915Z-24291ee0/browser-session-20260920T135858Z-a9ced784/apartment/trace.json) contains seven `explore` decisions followed by five `wait` decisions. Turn 8's moving wait first caused a safe stopped re-observation. Later replies repeatedly claimed the visible cube was already within 0.9 m, although `object_goal` and `mission.target_id` were null and there was no target receipt. **No select_object, approach_object or verify_object call occurred.** Waiting did not close the remaining gap. This supports an ungrounded semantic range judgment followed by repeated waits, not an observed target-distance controller stopping at its verified setpoint. The model's internal reason for that judgment is unknown.

Hypothesis and scoped mitigation: make missing measurement/verification explicit instead of returning only `status=observed` after a wait. Object-arrival mission inputs now expose whether the current target is selected, whether the mission has an arrival receipt, and the next selection/approach/verification stage. Wait feedback explicitly says arrival is unverified and points to current-image selection or measured approach. Guidance forbids inferring target range from visibility, apparent size or generic proximity beams. Safe waits remain legal; no automatic motion, forced object identity, evaluator coordinates, distance tolerance, budget or receipt is introduced. Find-only goals and other task types keep their previous feedback. Earlier runtime/control work is preserved.

[Seven focused evidence/receipt tests](../.runtime/object-arrival-feedback-v1/focused.xml) passed, covering unselected, foreign, selected-but-unverified and receipt-verified targets, legal safety waits and unaffected find-only/room/explore goals. [Two existing compact-payload tests](../.runtime/object-arrival-feedback-v1/payload-neighbors.xml) passed; syntax/editor checks are clean. These are model-free software checks, not proof that Qwen now selects/approaches correctly. **No real-model trial or full regression series was run during implementation.** The subsequent user-started failed trial is recorded above; successful target approach remains unverified. The historical failure remains retained, and unrelated regressions remain deferred.

Activation: the original port-8006 failed sequence was read only and not restarted. After checking the separate port-8007 preview was still the same idle run, it was closed for the update. Its immediate relaunch encountered an occupied-port/teardown race; that failed launcher was closed and the updated managed preview was successfully started on **`http://127.0.0.1:8008/`**. Apartment Search was loaded with fresh isolated knowledge and no saved-map reuse, then explicitly stopped: run `d004765c-71f8-4397-aab0-d58924e28c97`, zero simulated time/model tokens, agent/busy/recording inactive. The existing preference store and prior maps/recordings were retained. No automatic trial or manual placement was performed. Port 8008 supersedes the earlier updated-preview URL below.

**Exploration entry (2026-09-20), `exploration-entry-v1`, first-stage qualification complete:** the fresh standalone kitchen failure reproduced without models: **0.065 m** travel, zero contacts, `UNREACHABLE: current footprint is not in mapped free space`. The coarse 0.1 m map rejects the start near unknown cells and a measured obstacle corner, while the existing local depth planner accepts a 0.25 m path to a coarse-map-valid pose. Hypothesis: a bounded, fully observed local entry can safely reach the mapped planning region without relaxing its inflation rules. The accompanying `exploration-memory-v1` visit-ranking experiment failed qualification and was removed, as recorded below. This stage repairs a reproduced local start failure; it does not establish reliable autonomous search or a retained memory-ranking improvement.

Retained implementation: entry is restricted to active exploration/room/object search operations, maximum **8 seconds / 0.5 m**, under the existing mission authority and sensor-checked local controller. It requires fresh paired depth, a locally planned path and an endpoint accepted by the unchanged coarse map; Stop, changed authority, stale/unknown sensors and deadline failures stop motion. Entry time/travel are charged to the model-authorized objective; local-only entry time stays inside the existing operation budget. Parking/circle paths, sensor thresholds, prompts, scoring, frontier ranking and model-visible memory fields are unchanged. Unified/hybrid labels are 0.4.3/0.3.3.

The first successful entry diagnostic travelled **2.173 m**, zero contacts, and stopped at its 60 s mission budget; final observed free area was **46.66 m2**, not whole-home coverage or room-finding success. New real-enhanced-physics tests cover partial exploration followed by deliberate Stop, Stop during entry, sensor loss, unknown-space rejection and expired deadlines. Unknown-space rejection forbids entry motion; a later independently revalidated survey may permit ordinary exploration. The initial broader gate passed 183 tests and failed two old Stop tests that mistook entry displacement for a mapped segment; those tests now wait for a real mapped segment and all four delegation cases pass. A later indentation error was corrected; the final nine focused checks pass. No model-backed improvement is claimed yet. Target-specific inspection coverage, target-directed information gain and durable cross-run search learning are not implemented in this stage.

**Completion caveat:** clean controller completions remain **3/8**, unchanged from baseline. Both Luna circuit plans preserved the requested direction. Clockwise Qwen emitted two `[0,0,0,0]` and two `[0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5]` boxes; counterclockwise alternated six looks with six `[0,0,0,0]` circle selections. No circuit translation was admitted beyond negligible settling drift. Kitchen selected a frontier, lost it after the bounded survey, then twice chose exploration; current-footprint map admission failed. It travelled only 0.067 m. This is worse geometric progress than the baseline, not a speed improvement from finishing earlier. The cause of the changed kitchen outcome is not isolated by one stochastic run.

**Sensor/preparation optimization (2026-09-20), `sensor-preparation-v1`, narrowed candidate qualified:** the user approved priority-one work on the stale-depth/preparation bottleneck. The live cockpit was verified stopped with no active agent, regression or recording before runtime edits. Hypothesis: avoiding repeated graph construction and redundant stopped RGB-D captures reduces preparation work while preserving identical sensor evidence and admissible paths. This does not assume that the observed real-run sensor timeout has a single cause.

Implemented: `ObservedRouteSearch` snapshots the clearance mask, origin and start, computes a single lazy Dijkstra search, and reconstructs the existing bounded routes for all destinations within one candidate-generation call. Reuse is scoped to that call; subsequent planning creates a new search and execution still revalidates current evidence. Parking preparation can reuse the paired snapshot only when its run/epoch/map sequence, head/base pose and <=1 s age still match; otherwise it captures again. Motion admission and final containment verification still use fresh sensing. The initial lidar-integration/live-clearing optimization was **removed** after a doorway check repeatedly failed on it and passed with the original routines restored. Production lidar/map integration, occupancy voting, obstacle inflation, unknown-space rejection, controller/scorer thresholds, motion leases and model budgets are unchanged. Unified/hybrid versions are 0.4.1/0.3.1. No model prompt, weights, image resolution, lidar coverage or call cadence changed.

Focused checks passed: 3 candidate/search tests, 6 ray/clearance tests and 18 preparation-guard/live-clearing checks (overlapping suites; do not add counts as unique coverage). Tests cover one graph search per actual candidate set, identical returned candidates on reused evidence, paired image/pose, frozen search isolation from later map mutation, and rejection of expired/future/wrong-episode/changed-pose/head frames. [Candidate tests](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/candidate-search.xml), [snapshot reuse](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/snapshot-reuse.xml), [ray equivalence](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/map-equivalence.xml), [guards](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/preparation-guards.xml).

Fixed-input computation diagnostic, 30 repetitions with exact output-equivalence assertions: 24 routes using separate searches took **152.01 ms median / 169.55 ms p95**, versus **38.49 / 45.99 ms** using one snapshot search. The removed ray-integration experiment measured 35.68 -> 18.86 ms median but is not part of the final candidate; its timings are retained only as discarded-experiment evidence. These are synthetic CPU microbenchmarks, not task-success or end-to-end inference improvement claims. [Diagnostic script](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/measure_preparation.py), [measured output](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/computation-timing.json).

The initial 17-case scripted-inference enhanced-physics parking gate passed before narrowing. [Initial parking physics and timings](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/parking-physics.xml). The broader gate returned **264 passed / 9 failed / 1 known reference-endpoint test excluded**. Eight failures also occurred with the old map routines reinstated in memory, including missing pre-existing cache/frontier/scan API behavior, saved-map revision assumptions and older navigation/clearance checks. The differing doorway case failed again on the optimized map path, then **passed after removing the two map optimizations**; exact ray-equivalence tests alone were not sufficient to validate online map behavior. This is an attribution diagnostic, not a full before/after worktree comparison or proof that every remaining failure is unrelated. [Broad gate](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/affected-gates.xml), [old-map diagnostic results](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/previous-map-gates.xml), [candidate doorway recheck](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/doorway-recheck.xml), [restored doorway pass](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/doorway-restored.xml). No full-suite-green claim is made, and unrelated failures were not repaired.

Final narrowed-source parking recheck: **7/7 passed** (four fixed starts, mid-motion Stop, injected depth loss and interrupted arrival verification), with all four successful scenes independently completed and zero contacts. Preparation medians for canonical/left/right/far were **0.305 / 0.390 / 0.328 / 0.329 s**, with maxima **0.344 / 0.468 / 0.406 / 0.531 s**. [Final parking physics and timings](../.runtime/sensor-preparation-v1/final-parking-physics.xml). **Prioritized next steps (2026-09-20), `post-hybrid-priorities-v1`, plan only:** the [user-started hybrid sequence 9b90d744](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v2-20260920T084815Z-9b90d744/sequence.json) completed at 11:00:06 +02:00. It records **3 passed / 5 failed**: original, right-offset and farther-start parking passed; left-offset parking failed at 76.1425% approach with 0.22672 m residual footprint error after stale depth and parking timeout. Both circuits ended at 0 degrees on invalid normalized image boxes. Cube search ended at 42.2882% approach / 0.97310 m remaining; kitchen at 34.3874% / 4.98060 m remaining, both on decision-budget exhaustion. Approach percentages are geometric diagnostics, not fractional task completion. All eight recordings are complete/unassisted, with zero contacts and dropped records; all 77 sequence source hashes still match. This completed result supersedes the interim statuses below, but does not establish reliability or a matched improvement over different suites.

The following order is recommended, not implemented or scheduled:

1. **Remove sensing/preparation stalls while preserving clearance.** Reproduce the left-offset stale-depth stop; profile worker queue/sensing and candidate generation separately. Reuse coherent paired sensor snapshots, bound parking-specific candidates and reuse per-revision planning work; prevent optional UI/telemetry from delaying sensing. Hypothesis: removing avoidable serialized work improves both freshness and response latency. Gate: the exact failure completes without relaxed freshness, clearance, timeout or scoring; Stop/sensor-loss tests remain fail-closed. Navigation/control changes require the unchanged complete fixed household comparison.
2. **Validate parking reliability before expanding the task scope.** Freeze the candidate and run a predefined real Qwen+Luna batch across the four fixed starts, three repetitions each, retaining every failure and using the existing task/request/token budgets. Target: all 12 independently verified completions with zero contacts, complete unassisted recordings, and recorded p50/p95 preparation/inference/observation age. This is a development gate, not a reliability estimate; claims of improvement require a matched baseline, not comparisons with scripted evidence or the incomplete prior v2 run.
3. **Repair object-grounding reliability.** Use retained camera inputs to distinguish normalized-coordinate contract problems from actual identification failures, and evaluate explicit valid candidate IDs where sensor-derived proposals are available. Gate: reject invented/degenerate selections, then complete both circuit directions with unchanged physical verification. Merely increasing calls cannot fix repeated invalid boxes.
4. **Improve shared lidar/depth map support and target-directed search.** Reuse the existing 720-ray scan; address the home-map versus rolling-depth-map boundary, localization/freshness metadata and bounded model-facing map. Inspect the cube/kitchen decision traces before choosing a search-policy change; budget exhaustion alone does not prove that more budget is the remedy. Gate: measurable approach and physical completion on the same cases, with unknown space preserved and no evaluator geometry supplied to the model.
5. **Optimize Qwen inference after the larger preparation costs are controlled.** Investigate the 10.921 s prompt-processing spike, then compare stable prompt prefixes, compact history and task-appropriate image/context size. Keep serialized calls and latency-aware scheduling. Gate: lower p95 end-to-end latency and fewer stale decisions without worse grounding or task completion, not only higher tokens/second.

Defer CPU/GPU purchases, another lidar, 3D sensing, fine-tuning and further increases in call frequency until these measurements establish a remaining hardware, perception or policy limitation. No runtime code/settings, sensor configuration, existing run or model weights changed for this prioritization, and no new tests or model calls were started.

**360-degree lidar idea review (2026-09-20), `lidar-fusion-review-v1`, evaluation only:** the user proposed continuous all-around sensing and compact map-image feedback, explicitly requesting no implementation. Hypothesis: broader, independently scheduled range coverage and clearer lidar/depth fusion could reduce viewpoint-dependent navigation uncertainty without growing model context with raw scan volume. No runtime, sensor, model, map, benchmark input or controller changed; no new scan, inference or trial was executed. Benefits and cost changes are **not measured**.

The code already contains a simulated horizontal 360-degree scan: [capture_laser](../backend/ros_navigation.py) emits 720 rays (0.5-degree spacing), range 0.03-8 m, nominally about 0.305 m above the floor, with self-hits marked unavailable. [HomeMission sampling](../backend/home_mission.py) uses it on a nominal 0.2 s active interval, subject to worker load/forced sampling, and fuses depth obstacles into its accumulated map. This is independent of the separate eight proximity rays. The simulated scan samples one physics pose, not a temporally swept spinning sensor; real spinning lidar needs scan/per-beam timestamps and motion compensation. 360-degree azimuth is not complete visibility: occlusion, self-shadowing, range limits, inter-beam gaps and obstacles outside the scan plane remain. In particular, the parking posts are only 0.25 m high, below the nominal scan plane. Lidar does not identify the painted green bay or prove floor support/drop-off safety. Depth/RGB and current full-footprint checks remain necessary.

There are two relevant map paths: the home map already incorporates lidar and depth obstacles, while ordinary parking candidate planning and clearance primarily use the rolling head-depth `spatial_map`. Adding another identical scanner would not automatically repair that boundary or the observed `SPATIAL_STALE` failure. Proposed work should clarify layer provenance, scan age, transforms and which controller consumes each layer, without treating a planar no-return as whole-body traversability or letting a lidar free ray erase a low obstacle seen by depth. Mounting/coverage improvements or multi-height sensing may help low/overhanging obstacles, but carry separate compute and hardware trade-offs; they are not implemented by this review.

Context proposal: keep scans/point clouds in a local, incremental rolling occupancy/cost map; let the worker use fresh data at its control cadence, while Qwen receives only a compact snapshot at semantic decisions. Reuse the existing [ObservedMapContext](../backend/contracts.py) and [map renderer](../backend/map_context.py): local crops are normally 80 x 80 cells, capped at 96 x 96, rendered to a fixed 512 x 552 image. The bounded input can contain robot heading/camera direction, footprint, known/unknown/occupied space, observed bay, planned versus executed path, and a small shortlist of current maneuver IDs. Full-resolution scan arrays and historical map images should not accumulate in the prompt. A fixed-size map keeps context approximately bounded independently of ray count, but image tokens and vision processing are not free and depend on the model backend. Preserve the paired head image for semantic identity; avoid adding one image per lidar sweep.

Proposed freshness contract: separately timestamp lidar, depth, RGB and accumulated geometry, transform them to the paired observation frame, and expire stale local evidence without presenting old map cells as currently clear. Current map metadata explicitly lacks per-cell ages, so adding a reliable freshness overlay requires actual provenance data, not cosmetic labels. Only typed sensor-derived AgentObservation content may reach the model, never spectator/evaluator scene geometry. A future matched comparison should hold scenes, model, call budget and safety/scoring fixed; measure completion/residual error, contacts, scans/head turns, per-layer age, worker p95 latency, stale stops, input tokens and model response time. Include low posts, rear/side obstacles, overhangs, occlusions and dynamic obstacles. Recommendation: improve the existing lidar/depth integration and scheduling before adding a duplicate sensor or increasing resolution; retain unknown-space and footprint guards throughout.

**Latency bottleneck assessment (2026-09-20), `qwen-latency-audit-v1`, read-only:** the user asked whether response time can be reduced and whether CPU is the bottleneck. The running sequence was inspected without new inference, profiling load, configuration changes or motion. At 10:56:04 +02:00, Ollama `/api/ps` reported Qwen's full 5,460,082,032-byte allocation in VRAM at 16K context. Hardware is Ryzen 7 3700X (8 cores / 16 logical processors) and RTX 5080 (16,303 MiB). Instantaneous samples showed about 20% total CPU, a Python process around one logical-core equivalent, and GPU utilization 6% with 8,142 MiB used. These uncorrelated snapshots were not sustained inference measurements and cannot prove CPU/GPU saturation or exclude waits. They do not indicate CPU model offloading or exhausted VRAM.

Completed parking traces show recurring local preparation cost: original/right/left preparation medians **0.781 / 0.985 / 2.063 s**, versus Qwen response medians **1.422 / 1.235 / 1.407 s**. Typical per-case prompt-processing medians are **0.50-0.71 s**, generation **0.20-0.31 s**, and loading **0.008-0.019 s**. A farther-start outlier took **11.672 s**, with **10.921 s prompt processing**, only **0.008 s loading** and **0.279 s generation**: its cause is not established, and keeping weights warm would not address this particular spike. The farther-start parking case has now passed, so the completed parking subset is **3 passed / 1 failed** in this still-active user run. This is not an additional trial. [Current sequence](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v2-20260920T084815Z-9b90d744/sequence.json).

The left-offset stop-centred worker records show **0.625 s** wall time for one candidate-generation call and queue waits reaching **1.015 s**. Repeated map sampling and controller updates dominate several bounded windows; the stale-depth stop occurs during a controller update. These timings include waits and unmeasured child work, not CPU profiles, and do not establish the precise cause of sensor staleness. [Candidate/queue timing](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v2-20260920T084815Z-9b90d744/browser-session-20260920T084945Z-b2f03115/park_left/recording/media/worker-timing-2.json), [stale-depth timing](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v2-20260920T084815Z-9b90d744/browser-session-20260920T084945Z-b2f03115/park_left/recording/media/worker-timing-3.json). Left-offset map images were unavailable due to localization/freshness rejection; available map-image assembly elsewhere took only 0.016-0.079 s, so image assembly is not the demonstrated dominant preparation cost.

Recommendation: first reduce duplicated sensing and map preparation for each decision using one paired sensor snapshot, without weakening final fresh-clearance validation; specialize/cap parking candidate generation and reuse per-revision planning intermediates instead of repeated generic searches; keep optional rendering/telemetry work from delaying sensing/control. Then measure stable prompt-prefix reuse, bounded history and task-appropriate image/context size with matched decision-quality checks. Investigate prompt-processing spikes separately from steady-state latency. A faster CPU could help CPU-bound map/planning work, but the present evidence favors software scheduling/preparation optimization before a hardware purchase. Smaller prompts/images or off-worker computation remain proposed changes, not measured improvements. Model calls remain serialized; no increased concurrency, discarded safety evidence or freshness-limit relaxation is recommended.

**Observed parking maneuvers and measured Qwen cadence (2026-09-19), `parking-bricks-v1`, scripted qualification complete:** the user approved the building-block and responsiveness recommendations, then delegated blocker diagnosis while unavailable. No real-model trial is started by this implementation. The live evaluator was confirmed inactive before runtime edits. Hypothesis: offset parking fails partly because a valid grid route is followed with too much turning translation near a narrow corner; precise local tracking and fresh model-selectable steps can complete the same task without training or relaxed footprint checks.

The original left-offset failure was reproduced with scripted choices and real enhanced physics. Alignment alone, entry staging and bounded rescanning did not complete it. The rejection diagnostic found a clear current pose at the first stop, then a neighbouring disallowed cell during subsequent corner tracking. The retained solution adds **parking-only precision following**: at most 0.15 m/s, translation only within 0.08 rad of the current segment, 0.02 m corner tracking and 0.015 m endpoint tolerance. Map inflation, observed-space rollout, sensor freshness, collisions and evaluator success gates are unchanged. After cancelling a completed guarded turn, settling uses the existing guarded wait instead of a zero-speed drive request. An experimental observed-entry staging generator that did not resolve the failure was removed. [Initial failure](../.runtime/parking-bricks-v1/offset-reproduction.xml), [alignment failure](../.runtime/parking-bricks-v1/alignment-diagnostic.xml), [clearance diagnostic](../.runtime/parking-bricks-v1/clearance-diagnostic.json), [first precision pass](../.runtime/parking-bricks-v1/precision-following.xml).

`park_floor` now offers current `parking_maneuver_id` choices derived only from observed bay/depth data: **inspect, align, approach, verify**. The model constructs the sequence one fresh, bounded step at a time; it cannot supply path coordinates, execute an old ID, or claim arrival from a successful approach. The target stays locked through temporary loss of visibility/rejection, disabling unrelated exploration. Target identity, unchanged stopped pose/episode, authority, original mission deadline, 60-second parking duration, eight steps, two inspections and 6 m travel remain bounded. Final verification independently checks observed full-footprint containment and rest. The legacy all-in-one call remains compatible. Candidate feedback now preserves the exact paired RGB/depth image and pose. Unified architecture is 0.4.0; hybrid task supervision is 0.3.0. No model weights, new dependency, saved map or benchmark inputs changed.

Qwen mapped-route periodic review now targets **3 s** instead of 5 s, retaining the 0.75 m event trigger and serialized inference. The effective interval is `max(3, 1.25 * (recent_max_input_s + recent_max_response_s) + 0.25)` over bounded eight-sample histories; slow responses back off instead of accumulating a request queue. Cloud review remains 15 s / 2 m. Timing records now retain input preparation, model response, their total, request-start interval, observation age and optional Ollama load/prompt-processing/generation durations and token rates. Missing or invalid provider timings are not invented. This is instrumentation and scheduling, not a measured inference-speed or real-model success improvement. Historical measured Qwen median/p95 inference was 1.52/1.89 s; sustained post-change cadence and useful-decision rate remain unmeasured.

The final combined parking artifact records independent completion and zero contacts for canonical parking (1.139 m travel), left offset (1.211 m), right offset (1.226 m), farther start (1.782 m), plus the earlier +/-0.1 m X offsets. **Parking building blocks and Qwen cadence assessment (2026-09-19), `parking-bricks-cadence-review-v1`, recommendation only:** the user paused additional robot tests to discuss fine-tuning, faster Qwen feedback and composable movement. Read-only analysis of retained traces and the current control path found that unified Qwen emits one structured capability choice at a time, not wheel commands or a waypoint sequence. `park_floor` selects an observed floor-region ID; local code selects progressively closer candidates and runs bounded approach/containment/rest checks. Qwen is not called inside that parking operation. Explicit `movement` missions already support ordered measured drive/turn steps, but they cannot be attached to a `place` parking plan. [Decision contract](../backend/mission.py), [parking and cadence](../backend/mission_supervisor.py), [measured movement steps](../backend/movement.py), [candidate selection](../backend/continuous_supervisor.py).

The user-run [partial v2 sequence](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v2-20260919T204134Z-e4aa2e4c/sequence.json) used **Luna alone, reasoning none**, not Qwen/hybrid. Canonical parking passed. Both offset traces show correct `place` plans and selection of `park_floor` / `floor-0`, followed by `PARKING_PATH_BLOCKED: OBSERVED_PATH_BLOCKED: Inspect the route before continuing`. Left-offset recovery then selected generic frontiers and finished 2.446 m outside the target footprint bounds. Right-offset diagnostics show repeated route rejection and 0.655 m residual error, but that case is **invalid**, not a valid benchmark failure: the agent's API-handler repair changed `backend/app.py` during the run, triggering source-drift invalidation after the case. Its recording is complete/unassisted with zero drops/contacts. The farther start and remaining tasks were not run. This is not a completed eight-case comparison; preserve the original statuses and do not pool its diagnostic progress into benchmark claims. Future runtime edits must wait until any user-started evaluation is inactive.

Offline timing from the prior [complete Qwen/hybrid sequence](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v1-20260919T201920Z-f7dec2e2/sequence.json), filtered to 30 Qwen `guide_mission` responses and excluding Luna task reviews: median inference **1.5155 s**, nearest-rank p95 **1.891 s**, maximum **2.219 s**. Paired-camera capture-to-response age: median **1.7735 s**, p95 **2.391 s**, maximum **2.532 s**. These are mixed-action timings from one run, including invalid decisions, not a sustained-rate or decision-quality benchmark. They exclude work before camera capture and do not separate Ollama load, prompt processing and generation durations, which the adapter currently discards. Current mapped-route review triggers are 5 s or 0.75 m for local Qwen versus 15 s or 2 m for cloud supervision; model requests are serialized. Reducing the feedback slider alone does not create intermediate reviews inside `park_floor`.

Recommendation: first retain the chosen bay across failures and expose bounded, sensor-grounded maneuver candidates for inspection, entry alignment, short approach, verified entry and final containment/rest. A model may select a short conditional sequence, but each step must be replanned/revalidated against fresh observed full-footprint clearance before execution; candidate IDs and geometry come from sensors, never evaluator coordinates or invented clear space. A blockage should request a new local view or alternate bay approach, not silently change to generic exploration. The current closer-only candidate preference is a plausible limitation for staging maneuvers, not a proven root cause of the clearance rejection. Keep the local worker's motion control independent of model latency. A **3 s review target** is a reasonable candidate for later measurement, with one in-flight call, no stale-response queue and immediate bounded event reviews; 1 Hz is not supported by the observed response times. Record full preparation/queue/HTTP/parse latency, observation age and Ollama load/prefill/decode timings before any throughput claim. Preserve safety and task budgets rather than increasing calls to hide inefficient recovery.

Fine-tuning is not yet justified as the first repair: the offset run already selected the correct goal and parking capability before local execution rejected the route. The proposed controller/skill improvements do not require trained weights, though success across varied starts remains unproven. Fine-tuning Qwen becomes a separate option if it still misselects valid observed candidates after the interface is reliable; learning motor behavior would instead require a separately evaluated action policy and demonstrations. No application code, prompt, model, cadence, budget or saved evaluation changed in this assessment; no new model calls, robot runs or training were performed.

**Path-preview error handling (2026-09-19), `regression-path-error-v1`:** the older port-8002 server's normal idle-shutdown log exposed a `NameError` when an empty trajectory reached the API exception handler. Removed that redundant exception entry because `ValueError` already covers JSON decoding errors. Missing, empty and malformed trajectory files now return the existing 404 response and recover to 200 when a valid sample becomes available; unknown case IDs remain rejected and robot state is unchanged. **43 regression checks passed**, including three real-API recovery cases. [API and regression checks](../.runtime/parking-variations-v1/path-api-tests.xml). This fixes reporting only; no controller, scoring, scenario or model behavior changed and no real-model trial was run. The prior shutdown was idle cleanup, not evidence that this exception terminated the server.

Diagnostic schema v1: arrival/search approach percent is `100 * clamp(1 - final_gap / initial_gap, 0, 1)`. Remaining percent is `100 * final_gap / initial_gap`, allowed above 100 when moving away. For zero initial gap, remaining percent is unavailable and approach is 100 only while gap remains zero. Parking/room gap is the norm of per-axis base/wheel footprint boundary violations against the fixed evaluator zone. Search gap is base-to-actual-target centre distance beyond the existing 0.9 m inspection range. Raw centre distance remains available. These are straight-line diagnostics, not obstacle-aware route completion. Circuit percent uses the unchanged evaluator's valid directed angle divided by 360; remaining angle, return error and stopped dwell are separate. A 100% approach/lap is not a pass: visibility, containment, dwell, contacts and recording integrity retain their existing gates. Different task kinds are not pooled into one autonomy score.

Metrics use the worker's read-only call path at approximately one-second wall intervals; initial/final snapshots are retained in the sequence report. Target overlays appear only in the spectator regression path, never in AgentObservation or model images. Older recordings without snapshots show progress as unrecorded, not zero. The declared and executed real run used unchanged `observable-navigation-v1`, fresh standalone instances, Qwen/none plus one Luna/low review per case, 12 primary requests/80k tokens per case, original 120/180-second budgets, no saved map, no mid-run tuning/retries. This request superseded the earlier restriction on non-parking regression tests; no training or additional model tuning was performed.

**One complete real-model sequence: 1 passed / 4 failed.** [Frozen sequence and per-case grades](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v1-20260919T201920Z-f7dec2e2/sequence.json). None was cancelled, assisted or invalid; all five recordings are complete, with 2,698 samples, zero dropped records and zero contact episodes. Total calls were 30 Qwen decisions and 5 Luna task reviews. The model deployment labels remain configuration metadata, not independent underlying-model identity proof.

| Case | Result | Endpoint diagnostic | Remaining condition | Recorded elapsed / travel |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Parking | Passed | 100% approach; footprint error 0 m; centre error 0.02150 m | All containment/rest gates satisfied | 31.500 s / 1.13001 m |
| Table clockwise | Failed | 0% valid lap; 0/360 degrees | Four rejected zero-area boxes; correction limit | 27.469 s / 0.00161 m |
| Table counterclockwise | Failed | 0% valid lap; 0/360 degrees | Six zero-area circle boxes interleaved with six looks; decision budget exhausted | 40.156 s / 0.00254 m |
| Yellow cube | Failed, near approach | 95.3902% approach; remaining gap 0.07773 m (4.6098% of initial gap) | Target centre 0.97773 m away, beyond 0.9 m; visible and stationary, but inspection dwell 0/1 s | 97.063 s / 2.43235 m |
| Kitchen | Failed | 0% approach; footprint gap 7.59619 m (100.0693% of initial gap) | `UNREACHABLE: current footprint is not in mapped free space` | 44.500 s / 0.06443 m |

Actual replies confirm Luna accepted the correct circuit direction in both cases. Clockwise Qwen boxes were twice `[0,0,0,0]`, then twice `[0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5]`; counterclockwise supplied six `[0,0,0,0]` boxes. No circuit motion was admitted. Cube search produced valid-area selections, an approach and verification request, and the controller emitted a **completed** outcome despite independent physical failure. This is a completion-contract mismatch to investigate, not permission to relax inspection range or dwell. Kitchen remains blocked by the current-footprint map guard. No additional repairs, retries or model calls were made after this sequence. These observations do not establish a matched improvement across tasks; the prior interrupted sequence is not a complete comparator and has no v1 continuous grades.

**DreamZero/action fine-tuning research, not an implementation (2026-09-19):** `dreamzero-action-learning-review-v1` reviews DreamZero, OpenVLA-OFT, SmolVLA, ACT, FAST, RTC/training-time RTC, Cosmos Policy, OpenPI and Qwen's official fine-tuning/grounding documentation. Hypothesis for a future parking-only experiment: aligned short-horizon action chunks, explicit observed goal conditioning, varied corrective data and latency-aware execution may improve transfer beyond Milo's legacy single-action primitive adapter. None of these effects was measured in this review. [Detailed sources and proposed gates](NAVIGATION_STUDY.md#dreamzero-and-action-learning-2026-09-19).

DreamZero jointly predicts future video/actions and corrects its context with actual observations. Its 7 Hz result uses two GB200 GPUs; the repository also has a 5B training path, but native Windows/16 GB operation and Milo adaptation were not verified. The paper's unseen DROID result is 49% task progress / 22.5% success, not universal autonomy. Cosmos Policy adds future-state/value prediction with published base inference memory 6.0-8.9 GB but much larger training setups. For this RTX 5080/16 GB workstation, the recommended first learned-control comparison is a small goal-conditioned SmolVLA/ACT-style parking policy against the working conventional baseline, not immediate DreamZero adoption or more updates to the old primitive dataset. Qwen semantic/tool SFT is separate from numerical action learning. Qwen's official grounding cookbook uses 0-1000 coordinates versus Milo's 0-1 contract: a proposed offline compatibility check, not an established cause of zero boxes. No application code, configuration, checkpoint, dependency, live episode or benchmark input changed; no training, inference or robot trial was run. Only research documentation changed. Other model tasks remain deferred under the parking-first scope.

**Real parking result: 3/3 passed.** Each Luna review accepted `place / green parking bay`; each Qwen emitted exactly one `park_floor` decision selecting the offered `floor-0`. The local RGB-D detector and guarded approach/containment/rest controller executed the motion; Qwen did not produce wheel trajectories. Recorded wall times were **31.688 / 25.094 / 28.781 s**, with independent first physical completion at **26.516 / 20.359 / 22.484 s**. These clocks include preparation/inference, and are not inference-only latency. Actual paths were **1.12984 / 1.12949 / 1.13152 m**, versus net displacements 1.12715 / 1.12682 / 1.12891 m. Maximum lateral deviation was **0.094 / 0.053 / 0.162 mm**, consistent with driving straight into the bay instead of touring around it. All three controller outcomes and independent physics scores are completed, all recordings complete/unassisted, **976 samples total, zero contacts and zero drops**. Final cockpit state is stopped inside the bay, with physics showing 1/1 objectives verified. **Task completion v1 implemented; qualification in progress (2026-09-19):** `task-completion-v1` repairs the confirmed full-goal clockwise false rejection and connects unified place missions to observed marked-floor parking.
