**Task-completion recommendation and corrected attribution (2026-09-19):** `task-completion-audit-v1` is a read-only runtime/retained-trace analysis, with documentation correction only. Hypothesis: some 0/5 failures are integration defects and missing task capabilities, not insufficient model speed. Offline replay of the final clockwise Luna response confirms it returned `kind=circuit`, `target=table`, `circle_direction=clockwise` correctly. `validate_plan_against_goal` rejects that response against the full goal because the explanatory sentence also contains "counterclockwise"; the same response passes against the requested first sentence. This is a validator false rejection, not a wrong Luna direction. Taking only the first sentence was a diagnostic, not an implemented parser fix. Earlier statements blaming this final clockwise failure on Luna are superseded. [Actual Luna reply](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v1-20260919T170902Z-c2f75dd5/browser-session-20260919T171115Z-49f78651/furniture_circuit/trace.json), [validator](../backend/mission.py).

Parking's retained inputs expose only the odometry-labelled Mission start as a reachable place, not the green bay. Its 12 Qwen decisions are eight `explore` and four `navigate_frontier`; the unified place contract offers named-place navigation but no marked-floor selection/approach action. The legacy continuous controller has a sensor-derived marked-floor approach capability that should be evaluated for reuse in the unified executive. More exploration requests cannot supply this missing target-binding step. Counterclockwise Qwen did supply boxes, but all four were degenerate `[0,0,0,0]`, not absent boxes. These were correctly rejected. This does not establish general Qwen incapability or a better alternative model. [Parking trace](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v1-20260919T170902Z-c2f75dd5/browser-session-20260919T170922Z-cd49cc17/park/trace.json), [counterclockwise trace](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v1-20260919T170902Z-c2f75dd5/browser-session-20260919T171145Z-090e4624/furniture_circuit/trace.json), [unified actions](../backend/mission_supervisor.py), [existing marked-floor capability](../backend/continuous_supervisor.py).

Recommendation, not implemented: first repair intent validation against complete real goals (including explanatory/negated alternatives) and bounded invalid-output correction; then complete parking's observed target -> guarded approach -> measured containment/rest chain. Reuse local capabilities with explicit search, target acquisition, navigation, verification and completion states. Keep Luna at task-contract/progress-advice level with no stale motion authority; use Qwen for current-view semantic evidence and candidate ranking, with local code owning mandatory next stages, route tracking and Stop. Qualify both circuits next, then visible-object approach before hidden-object search, then room recognition/entry; reproduce the kitchen current-footprint transition independently before model trials. Every stage needs repeatable unassisted physical completion through the production interface, negative cases, unchanged safety/scoring, and the complete fixed household comparison for control changes. Faster inference, more travel and passing component tests are not acceptance criteria for task completion. No new model calls, simulator commands, source changes or comparison run were made for this recommendation.

**Targeted frontier progression and circuit closure qualified; real hybrid task success unchanged (2026-09-19):** `targeted-progression-circuit-v1` addresses the three preserved physical failures from the preceding regression-path work. Semantic object, room and place searches now suppress generic `explore` while fresh observed frontiers exist, requiring Qwen to choose an exact supplied `frontier_id`. A zero-travel current-footprint rejection receives one bounded panoramic floor survey; the old frontier is never replayed, and execution resumes only if Qwen selects a currently supplied frontier on fresh evidence. Failed objective startup no longer emits a misleading `Mission operation finished` event. Route diagnostics now distinguish an invalid current footprint, destination and map bounds. Generic exploration remains available when no current frontier exists. No map cell is fabricated, stale coordinate reused, or clearance/freshness/authority check bypassed.

Observed furniture circuits now use a circuit-only 0.015 m terminal tolerance for the post-lap return, inside the controller's 0.02 m closure requirement, followed by a fixed 1.25-second stationary hold. The independent scorer, orbit angle/distance/closure thresholds, collision checks and motion limits are unchanged. The complete targeted physics gate passes **8/8**: nominal circuit, Stop, wrong kind, wrong direction, timeout, supplied/invented frontier handling and enhanced kitchen roaming. The kitchen case covers **3.151 m with zero sampled contacts**; the exact-frontier case performs a fresh-ID handoff after its first nearby frontier is invalidated. Qwen/hybrid/regression contracts pass **83/83**. [Targeted physics](../.runtime/qwen-recovery-20260919/targeted-physics-final.xml), [frontier recovery](../.runtime/qwen-recovery-20260919/target-frontier-recovery.xml), [Qwen/regression gate](../.runtime/qwen-recovery-20260919/qwen-regression-final.xml).

The complete final-source household comparison against `qwen-luna-task-supervision-v1-baseline-20260919` finishes **18 passed / 0 failed / 3 unchanged missing-room prerequisite blocks**. Runtime, suite, fixture and pass counts match; source remained unchanged and the original map hash remains the required `b9e33c46...`. This is scripted real-physics safety/navigation evidence, not semantic-model improvement. [Final fixed series](../.runtime/performance/targeted-progression-circuit-v1-20260919/summary.md), [comparison](../.runtime/performance/targeted-progression-circuit-v1-20260919/comparison.json), [manifest](../.runtime/performance/targeted-progression-circuit-v1-20260919/experiment.json).

Exactly one final observable Qwen+Luna five-case sequence was then run with the same 12-request/80k-primary-token limits, fresh maps, independent scorers and no retries or tuning. It remains **0/5**: parking travelled 6.075 m before Qwen exhausted decisions; the direction validator falsely rejected Luna's correct clockwise plan before Qwen or motion; counterclockwise Qwen repeatedly supplied degenerate `[0,0,0,0]` image boxes; Luna's object target exceeded the 160-character contract; and kitchen chose frontier `1:1`, surveyed when its current footprint failed, then fell back to generic exploration and again rejected the current footprint. Distances were 6.075/0.001/0.002/0.001/0.065 m. All five recordings are complete, unassisted, zero-drop and zero-contact; all five path endpoints return their full recorded sample counts (1,243/181/238/185/488), and the current source fingerprint still matches `9874b6dbe62e0425324bdc0682f81cd8aac1dc0f7f6dd9c0fe3e68ad783372bd`. Luna made exactly one non-authoritative, non-motion call per case. Three plans were accepted; the two rejected plans stopped execution, but only the overlong object target was invalid on the evidence above. Qwen used 111,454 input + 451 output tokens, versus 219,852 + 1,296 in the prior 0/5 hybrid run; lower usage reflects earlier failures and is not an efficiency gain. [Final observable sequence](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v1-20260919T170902Z-c2f75dd5/sequence.json), [visible paths](../.runtime/qwen-recovery-20260919/final-observable-paths.png), [prior hybrid sequence](../.runtime/performance/regression-observable-navigation-v1-20260919T095521Z-f51306f6/sequence.json).

The result separates software capability from model behavior. Circuit mechanics and target-frontier execution now pass deterministic physical tests, but the real models did not consistently emit valid task contracts or required evidence. The kitchen still needs a map-side current-footprint recovery that remains valid across the frontier-survey-to-generic-explore transition; that was observed only after the single frozen comparison and was not tuned or rerun. No autonomous task improvement is claimed.

**Regression path review and cross-case recommendation implemented (2026-09-19):** `regression-path-plan-contract-v1` adds an expandable Path disclosure to every observable regression case. It reads only that sequence's allowlisted `recording_directory/trajectory.jsonl`, bounds files/lines/samples/numbers, rejects paths outside the active sequence root, marks manual-placement segment breaks and contact onsets, and returns at most 1,200 displayed points. Running cases refresh once per second; completed paths persist across backend restarts by restoring the newest completed `observable-navigation-v1` summary without resuming work. The cockpit shows start/end/contact markers plus measured distance and original sample count. The restored hybrid result exposes five distinct paths: parking 7.22 m/1,573 samples, clockwise 0.00 m/179, counterclockwise 4.42 m/1,285, object search 2.22 m/1,039, and kitchen 0.06 m/480. These are privileged evaluation paths and never model inputs or motion authority.

Cross-test analysis of Qwen-only and hybrid traces found task-specific failures: Qwen-only misplanned both circuits as object/identify and misplanned parking, yellow-cube approach and kitchen entry as generic explore; parking/object search then spent nearly all decisions on generic exploration; kitchen in both modes ended at the unchanged current-footprint map guard. Hybrid corrected task semantics in four cases, but still finished 0/5: Qwen exhausted its operational budget without converting the task plan into target-specific capability calls, and one Luna clockwise plan had the wrong direction and was rejected before Qwen/motion. The ranked recommendation is therefore to enforce explicit task semantics on every production-grounded initial plan, then narrow an accepted circuit's action space to `circle` plus inspection actions. This is implemented: explicit circuit/direction, object approach, room entry, parking-arrival and return-Home language must match the typed plan; circuit plans no longer expose generic explore/frontier actions. Scoring, cases, model budgets and safety guards are unchanged. The kitchen footprint rejection remains a separate mapping/start-clearance issue.

Verification: complete Qwen/hybrid and regression contract suites pass **80/80**; regression trajectory/persistence/security tests pass **19/19**; three focused browser workflows pass for Qwen configuration, hybrid payloads and responsive per-case path viewing without robot-state changes. Focused semantic and Luna/Qwen boundary tests pass. The complete current-fixture fixed comparison against `qwen-luna-task-supervision-v1-baseline-20260919` finishes **18 passed / 0 failed / 3 unchanged missing-room blocks**, with source unchanged, original map unchanged, all 117 frozen source/build hashes matching and the same suite/fixture. This scripted no-model series validates shared navigation/safety, not semantic-model improvement. [Post-fix contracts](../.runtime/qwen-recovery-20260919/postfix-contracts.xml), [Baseline](../.runtime/performance/qwen-luna-task-supervision-v1-baseline-20260919/summary.md), [final candidate](../.runtime/performance/regression-path-plan-contract-v2-20260919/summary.md). The earlier v1 candidate preceded the final narrowed parking-language validator and is retained but not the final qualification.

The broad affected mission gate before these final repairs retained 229 passes and 10 failures. Seven stale fixture/wording assumptions were corrected first. The remaining three physical failures were then reproduced rather than hidden: the circuit controller stopped at its generic 0.04 m route tolerance before satisfying its 0.02 m closure predicate; kitchen roaming emitted an extra completion event for a failed objective start; and the supplied-frontier test inspected transient task state after cleanup while also exposing a real zero-travel current-footprint rejection. The final targeted gate above resolves those deterministic cases without changing their task criteria or safety checks. Earlier failures remain retained as evidence. [Circuit diagnostic](../.runtime/qwen-recovery-20260919/circuit-diagnostic.xml), [frontier reproduction](../.runtime/qwen-recovery-20260919/postfix-frontier.xml), [roaming reproduction](../.runtime/qwen-recovery-20260919/postfix-roaming.xml), [broad affected gate](../.runtime/qwen-recovery-20260919/path-plan-final.xml).

**High-frequency Qwen with Luna task supervision evaluated (2026-09-19):** `qwen-luna-task-supervision-v1` keeps Qwen as the only operational mission planner and the worker as the only motion authority. Local Qwen semantic checkpoints are now provider-specific at 5 seconds or 0.75 m measured mapped travel; Luna-only remains 15 seconds or 2 m. Reviews remain serialized and stopped, and no motion lease, sensor freshness, footprint clearance, objective deadline, request/token threshold or Stop guard changed. The architecture/version report records the effective cadence and identifies `qwen-luna-task-supervision@0.1.0`; the shared unified architecture advances to 0.2.0.

The explicit `Qwen + Luna task supervision` mode gives Luna one low-reasoning call at mission start. Luna receives only the original goal and a typed `review_task` schema; it receives no camera, AgentObservation, map, available actions, coordinates, route or motor command. Its output is limited to an immutable `MissionPlan` and <=500-character guidance. The plan is validated against the original goal, including circuit/direction, explicit object approach, room entry and return-Home requirements. Invalid guidance fails closed before Qwen or motion. Valid guidance is labelled non-authoritative and included in Qwen's next/current-observation decisions; Qwen still selects every available action from fresh evidence. Luna and Qwen have separate clients and counters; Luna is capped at one request/100k tokens per observable case, cannot silently fall back or complete the task, and is closed with the primary client. The UI displays separate Luna review usage and `Motion authority: None`.

**Real observable comparison:** Qwen-only `bf50f1b1-b391-4fd4-828d-66e6d6156bad` and guarded hybrid `f51306f6-35a5-4b13-abea-4c0c20d6e6c3` used the same five cases, fresh-map rule, per-case 12-request/80k-primary-token caps, recordings and independent scoring. Both finish **0/5**, so task success did not improve. Qwen-only -> hybrid elapsed/travel: parking 114.266 s/6.337 m -> 112.282 s/7.222 m; clockwise 17.282 s/0.001 m -> 16.640 s/0.001 m (Luna returned the wrong direction and was rejected before Qwen/motion); counterclockwise 16.000 s/0.001 m identify-only -> 95.141 s/4.415 m with the correct circuit contract but decision-budget exhaustion; object search 177.438 s/7.050 m -> 90.453 s/2.219 m, still decision-budget exhausted; kitchen 44.875 s/0.065 m -> 45.516 s/0.065 m, same mapped-footprint rejection. Shorter time is not improvement when both fail, and extra circuit travel is not completion.

Hybrid Luna calls took 4.735-8.063 s each but occurred only before Qwen, never in an active motion loop. Luna used 4,603 input + 688 output tokens; Qwen used 219,852 input + 1,296 output tokens versus Qwen-only 178,327 + 1,064. The hybrid therefore increased total model usage and did not establish efficiency. All five final hybrid recordings are complete, unassisted, zero-drop and zero-contact; all 74 frozen source/build hashes match. Two earlier interrupted hybrid attempts are preserved: browser disconnect after a valid non-actuating review, and a deliberately stopped sequence after exposing the pre-guard wrong clockwise plan. **Proposed Luna + Qwen experiment, not implemented or run:** begin with a frozen-observation shadow comparison in which both models receive the same allowed AgentObservation, current head image and typed observed-map image. Qwen may emit a typed, explicitly unverified semantic/plan proposal; Luna remains the only mission decision owner, and the existing worker remains the only motion authority. Measure schema validity, agreement on target/action, latency, local preparation cost, cloud requests/tokens and independent task outcomes. Only after shadow evidence should a candidate feed Qwen's bounded proposal to Luna for adjudication. Do not run two independent motion supervisors, silently fall back between models, or claim benefit from configured model names. Any navigation/control candidate still requires the complete fixed household baseline plus separately labelled real-model trials with matched settings.

**Spatial memory integration verified in focused tests (2026-09-17):** `spatial-memory-v2` continues the profile/checkpoint milestone for rooms, objects, inspections and observed pathways (UC-03/08/09/18). Hypothesis: staging observation-derived places until the evidence transaction succeeds, preserving scope through lifecycle changes, and retaining only successful transition evidence prevents false remembered destinations while keeping the existing worker navigation interface. Three regression cases first reproduced phantom places from an empty label, foreign entity association and storage failure; all now pass. Memory-enabled mission room reports use the same serialized, staged ingestion path. Evidence writes check revocation and localization before commit, then the worker rechecks publication authority. Explicit unlocalized viewing poses remain labelled wheel_odometry, not invented map coordinates. Unknown object positions render as unverified rather than crashing the map panel.

Reset and application restart retain the selected profile, including a profile initially created fresh; an explicit fresh challenge load or Start fresh knowledge creates a separate profile. Map/frame revisions survive reload, incompatible places are not navigable, and snapshots reject unpublished viewpoint references. Forks remap evidence references and retain the checkpoint's review state. New contexts revoke older contexts for that profile, teardown drains the bounded persistence executor, and temporary mission-start destinations are excluded from saved knowledge. Automatic legacy-to-current-layout import is disabled unless an internal compatibility approval is supplied; legacy map documents remain available and are not silently asserted compatible. Queries use bounded SQL materialization; same-target/same-view recent inspections lower sighting priority without certifying whole-room absence. Memory-enabled frontier failures stay task-local, while successful visits remain persistent. Path collection clears on interruption, stale depth, collision evidence or discontinuity; it records sampled transitions between observed annotation neighborhoods, not independently verified semantic room boundaries. No new perception model, motor replay, speed/lease change or map-truth input was added.

**Memory-specific evidence:** the final memory/API/mission gate passes **24 checks in 149.0 s**, including a real enhanced-camera, scripted visual object selection -> approach -> fresh verification -> Home return. Both object sightings retain one entity identity, distinct frames and image evidence; identity remains unverified. A separate real wheel-executed return between two fixture-labelled observation regions retains a multi-point pathway and success count with no sampled contacts. Scope/migration/fork/search/frame tests, queued-write cleanup, rollback during revocation, same-frame deduplication and a hidden-scene-metadata mutation check also pass. The latter changes hidden labels/positions while reusing the same paired sensor evidence; it is not exhaustive noninterference proof. **Travelled-path and TV-goal audit (2026-09-16):** `path-goal-audit-v1` is a read-only analysis of the user's screenshot and existing real-model session `f9980b38-f6ce-4697-9f4a-e714197dda52`, addressing UC-03/08/18 and object-search progress. Hypothesis: repeated travel may reflect a missing link between semantic target selection and geometric navigation, not just missing visit memory. The thick blue spectator trail is sampled browser travel across missions within an episode, clearing on reload/reset; it is not a single-mission timeline or proof of search coverage. The latest recorded mission is the custom goal **find the TV**, not the loaded preset's kitchen task. Its 180.203 wall seconds cover 85.2 simulated seconds (670.3 to 755.5), 10.146 m actual travel, 1,760 complete samples, zero drops/operator assistance/sampled contact episodes, and 68,486 reported tokens. It ended on the time budget without an object-verification receipt. The custom goal has no matching independent scorer, so the preset's physics-success flag cannot grade TV identification. [Original report](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T182044Z-0b552f1b/flat_kitchen/report.json).

The retained 77-event trace contains 17 responses: one plan, three look decisions, one object selection and twelve explore decisions, with **zero approach_object or verify_object actions**. Input event 11 reports the newly selected sighting 3.24 m away; all three local approaches are unavailable specifically because their observed routes exceed the existing 1.8 m local-goal horizon. Subsequent explore reasons repeatedly claim progress toward the television, but the worker dispatches generic HomeMission exploration without an object target. Euclidean distance to that same measured sighting grows to 6.53 m at event 41 and is 6.33 m at the final review (event 71); from event 51 onward the goal is explicitly unavailable because the sighting expired, with no new selection. Temporary increases could be required detours, but this run never transitions to object approach or verification. An observed-path blockage is recorded during the first exploration operation; a requested look stops it for new evidence before a second exploration operation. No timing-expiry diagnosis is inferred from the screenshot. [Original trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T182044Z-0b552f1b/flat_kitchen/trace.json), [dispatch](../backend/worker.py), [local object approach](../backend/object_navigation.py).

The exact recorded selection image is available as spatial frame 10002; its run, sequence and capture timestamp match input event 8's observed-map snapshot. It shows a small distant scene and does not independently establish television identity. Net observed-free area increases from 84.76 to 87.69 m2 (+2.93 m2); this is neither a whole-environment percentage nor a measure of semantic search coverage. Prior known geometry can still require new target-specific views. The report measures 22.438 s stationary during inference; total model latency can overlap travel and is not identical to this figure. [Paired selection image](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T182044Z-0b552f1b/flat_kitchen/recording/media/spatial-10002.png), [sensor provenance](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T182044Z-0b552f1b/flat_kitchen/recording/media/spatial-10002.json).

**Recommendation, not implemented:** resolve a credible last-seen target into a compatible sensor-map observation/staging destination, submit it through existing mapped navigation, then reacquire and use the existing short-range object approach. Retain fresh footprint/collision/localization/authority checks; do not extend the local horizon or replay old motor commands. Persistent region/doorway transitions and target-specific inspected viewpoints could then reduce rediscovery. Track progress to the actual selected destination separately from exploration coverage, require an explicit reason for revisiting an unchanged inspected viewpoint, and reacquire expired sightings. Improvement, recognition accuracy, shortest-path efficiency and autonomous persistent-memory learning remain unmeasured. This audit changed documentation only; no new model call, robot movement/reset/restart, test run, navigation change or replacement benchmark was performed.

**On-demand configuration drawer and visible Reset (2026-09-16):** `configuration-drawer-v1` addresses UC-18 operator setup and UC-09 control accessibility. Hypothesis: the existing connection/run/recording fields can move into a right-side drawer, with a direct command-bar trigger and acknowledged Save-and-close, without duplicating preferences or changing robot control. The configuration icon now sits beside Start and Stop; the former Settings tab opens the same drawer. Reset is a permanent icon (tooltip Reset scene, accessible name Reset episode), retaining the existing reset endpoint and guards. The separate hand-icon menu keeps manual takeover. Opening configuration from a camera/map/challenge dialog closes that dialog first and moves the single shared command bar into the drawer. The console may remain minimized. Close/Escape return focus to the command-bar trigger, and reload starts with the drawer hidden.

Save configuration waits for the existing serialized preference writes, applies explicit connection and recording drafts through their existing APIs, and collapses only after success. Ordinary fields keep their prior autosave behavior; section-specific Apply/Save remains available. Failed saves retain pending writes/drafts and display an error in the drawer. Cross-endpoint saves are sequential, not a new atomic backend transaction; a later failure does not roll back an earlier successful section. Active execution retains existing field locks; Start/Send cannot race the full save, and Stop/Reset remain available independently. Exact token thresholds use integer steps consistent with the backend, fixing the previous native min=1/step=1000 mismatch exposed by whole-form validation. No backend, model, map, sensor, safety, scoring or memory-system implementation changed.

Drawer/control geometry and screenshots pass at 1440/390/320 px; mobile and desktop images were visually inspected. The complete mission UI file then passes **15/15** (1.6 minutes), including token/power/chat guards, active settings lockout, nonblank interactive 3D, custom-folder recorded real-physics/scripted-inference mission and Stop finalization. **Configurable test-run recordings verified; activation pending (2026-09-16):** `recording-settings-v1` addresses UC-18 and the request to record test runs into a configurable folder. Hypothesis: the existing RunRecorder/session lifecycle already provides synchronized replay evidence; its fixed `.runtime/performance` destination and lack of operator controls are the missing pieces. Recording enablement and destination persist in the existing preference store, survive scene resets/server restarts, and affect new browser agent sessions and operator home-map workflows only. Active/finalizing recordings cannot be reconfigured. Runs use unique timestamp/UUID subfolders; opting out bypasses capture rather than deleting existing files. The archive retains the default root and up to 100 registered folders, uses unique stable identifiers for external roots, and preserves its size/filename/containment/reference checks. Existing explicit-root caller IDs remain compatible. No arbitrary archive file-serving endpoint, automatic MP4, manual mid-run capture, voice capture or CLI output override was added. Existing Stop/cancellation and writer-failure braking behavior is retained; invalid destinations never silently fall back.

**Ground-projected travelled path (2026-09-16):** `ground-trail-v1` addresses UC-18 and the request to project the robot's travel onto the floor. Hypothesis: the existing live world-position snapshots are sufficient for a useful operator trail without adding sensor work, inference or controller telemetry. A blue, 3-pixel Three.js line now projects sampled robot-base XY positions at 0.035 m above the flat floor. It represents past sampled travel, not a planned route or clearance certificate. The default-on Travelled path switch sits beside Sensors & areas and retains its choice in this tab. Hiding the trail does not stop collection or erase it. The history survives mission changes and spectator graphics rebuilds but resets on a new run/episode or page reload; older completed travel is not reconstructed.

Sampling uses 0.02 m minimum displacement and retains at most 4,096 segments, backed by a fixed reusable GPU buffer. Manual-placement revisions, disconnection, power-off, missing/nonfinite pose, backwards simulation time, simulation gaps >0.5 s, receipt gaps >1.5 s, same-time position jumps, and implausible displacement (>0.8 times simulated delta +0.03 m) break continuity rather than draw an unobserved bridge. These thresholds only affect visualization, not physical motion or authorization. This is sampled browser history with possible missing detail, not the complete recorder trajectory, a new map, or proof of uninterrupted movement. No backend, sensing, model inputs, motion policy, safety limits or saved-map storage changed; world coordinates remain operator-only.

**Powered-on custom instructions available (2026-09-16):** `powered-chat-v1` addresses the request to chat with the robot whenever it is on (UC-18 operator controls, with UC-09 cancellation). Hypothesis: the existing active-only composer gate, not a missing mission API, prevented custom instructions from idle. The composer now stays mounted and visible in the expanded Luna console before, during and after a run. Idle submissions share the Start button's existing mission/legacy payload construction and readiness/budget validation, using the submitted text as the goal without changing the scene. Active submissions still use the session-bound instruction endpoint, which preserves remaining budgets and invalidates prior work before replanning. The current initial mission instruction is shown before replies when absent from the backend conversation. Accepted idle text also becomes the next-run goal; rejected text is retained as a draft with its error. A successful send opens Conversation. Power-off, disconnection, concurrent submission, missing/unapplied configuration, invalid settings and unavailable mission capability block relevant submissions. Explicit local-only exploration does not silently start a paid custom mission. Plain stop/pause/halt/cancel messages bypass inference through the existing Stop endpoint, including from idle or invalid mission settings. Stop remains independently available during a pending submission.

This is a frontend entry-point change, not a new chatbot backend, prompt, model capability, motion controller or budget policy. The existing local Responses SDK/worker architecture, observed-only model inputs, sensing, collision checks, saved maps and recorded scenario criteria are unchanged. The selected scenario remains the loaded scene; a custom goal does not rewrite its evaluator or prove arbitrary instruction competence. No new navigation benchmark or real-model improvement is claimed.

**Moving sensor visualization corrected on disk; activation pending (2026-09-16):** `moving-overlay-v1` addresses UC-18 and the report that green/red areas appear only when the robot stops. Hypothesis: the display's 0.05 m / 0.08 rad pose guard invalidated an area snapshot well before the next working-mode poll (750 ms plus request time), although sensors continued updating. At 0.5 rad/s, that angle limit is crossed after 160 ms. Removing the guard while attaching old sectors to the moving robot would misrepresent previously sampled clearance, so the two visual layers are now separate: opt-in operator telemetry supplies the world pose at which corridors were sampled; areas, planning radius and stop ticks remain at that pose, while proximity rays and the displayed footprint follow the live base. The legend distinguishes area and beam status. Red rays mean a measured hit at any range, blue rays mean no return within the finite range, and stale rays are gray. Footprint dimensions remain those of the last matching zone snapshot, not a new high-rate posture sensor.

World display pose is outside AgentObservation and the model map/image path. Old servers without it retain the conservative moved-pose fallback. Source-age/localization expiry, missing/error/paused/foreign telemetry, power-off and disconnection still suppress clear/restricted area colors; geometry is never motion permission. Browser age now conservatively includes the entire spatial request duration, not just time since receipt. The existing single-flight poll waits 250 ms after a working-mode response when the overlay is shown, or four times the last request duration if larger, backing off slow responses. Hidden-overlay working cadence remains 750 ms; idle/off remain 2/5 seconds. Sensor resolution/range/capture scheduling, model cadence, motion limits, collision checks and leases are unchanged.

**Existing user-run evidence, not a candidate evaluation:** read-only analysis of session `c8b26d6d-ebd9-429a-916a-4de13aeaf1d3` finds 1,303 moving intervals among 1,530 recorded samples, using positive simulation-time delta and actual XY speed >0.015 m/s or wrapped yaw speed >0.04 rad/s. Unique source capture times match moving physical samples for **231 depth frames and 234 head frames**; laser scan count increases during 149 moving intervals. Reported Home depth age during motion has median **0.344 s**, p95 **0.516 s**, maximum **0.953 s**, with none above 1 s in these samples. This refutes sensing only at rest for this recording, but does not establish every-frame processing, absence of brief stalls, or current-run timing. The initial analysis mistakenly required asynchronous capture and delivery in the same interval and found zero; both outputs are preserved with the corrected method. [Corrected analysis](../.runtime/moving-overlay-v1/recorded-sensor-analysis-final.json), [initial analysis](../.runtime/moving-overlay-v1/recorded-sensor-analysis.json), [original trajectory](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T165909Z-f1c5223e/flat_kitchen/recording/trajectory.jsonl).

**Verification:** two backend opt-in/observed-only/no-actuation cases pass. Frontend build passes with the existing Vite bundle warning; the initial nullable cache assignment was repaired. Two scripted display checks pass (22.0 s), including translation 0.25 m and rotation 0.5 rad with fixed sampled-area origin, independent live beam origin/status, genuine stale and delayed-response suppression, legacy pose fallback, foreign-episode/error handling, switch persistence, unchanged physical snapshot/camera bytes and no display-issued motion commands. Desktop/phone screenshots and canvas-pixel checks verify nonblank rendering. [Backend gate](../.runtime/moving-overlay-v1/pose-contract.xml), [initial passing browser gate](../.runtime/moving-overlay-v1/display-first.xml), [transport-aware gate](../.runtime/moving-overlay-v1/display-timing.xml), [desktop](../.runtime/moving-overlay-v1/moving-1440.png), [phone](../.runtime/moving-overlay-v1/moving-390.png).

The existing camera-selected local route also passes with the overlay enabled and actual area-pose updates during motion: **8.312 controller seconds, 78 updates, 0.038 m remaining, 0 buffer stops, 0 replans, 0 sampled contacts and 0 model tokens**, maximum reported update gap 0.172 s. This is scripted operator destination selection with real physics, not semantic autonomy or an under-load reliability benchmark. Its first gate failed the obsolete expectation that Start is hidden; the consolidated UI correctly keeps it visible but disabled. Only that UI assertion changed, with all route/arrival/contact/buffer criteria retained. No complete household series is substituted by this display check and no navigation/performance gain is claimed. Full suites were not rerun.

It was left stopped/inactive in episode `d47ab913-834d-4c42-a442-78ab7235ee24` at 233.85 simulated seconds; no shared-page reload, backend restart, map overwrite, new mission or paid inference was performed. The built frontend and backend changes activate on the next normal restart and browser refresh. Pending restart is intentional to preserve the user's current scene. Separate kitchen verification and residual controller-timing issues remain unresolved.

**Sensor-overlay switch restored to prominence (2026-09-16):** `sensor-overlay-switch-v1` addresses UC-18 and the reported loss of the robot's sensor/area visualization after UI refresh. The existing display path is now controlled by an explicit Sensors & areas switch with a radar icon directly beside World view. Its on/off choice is retained in sessionStorage for this browser tab across refreshes; a new tab still defaults off, and unavailable browser storage falls back to page-local state. The switch changes only overlay rendering and the existing opt-in telemetry header, not sensing, localization, motion authority or the model input.

Build passes with the existing Vite bundle warning. The extended scripted-browser regression passes (21.8 s): switching on renders 48 sampled areas and changes canvas pixels; switching off removes them and restores plain telemetry requests. Both choices survive reload, the overlay works with the console minimized, and the switch/viewport fit at 1440/1024/390/320 px. Existing stale, changed-pose, error and foreign-episode guards still suppress misleading clear regions. Camera bytes and the physical snapshot remain unchanged, with no frontend motion commands. **Viewport-first cockpit consolidation (2026-09-16):** `viewport-console-v1` addresses UC-18 and the user's request to prioritize 3D observation, minimize unused panels and make token budgets easier to adjust. Hypothesis: a single-column world/readout/console hierarchy reduces simultaneous information density without hiding emergency controls or interrupting sensor telemetry. The full-width Three.js viewport now leads both setup and running states, with responsive height and portrait initial-camera framing. User-adjusted camera positions remain respected. The existing challenge dialog button remains the only challenge-selection surface; redundant workspace links/eyebrow were removed. A compact collapsible live readout sits directly below the viewport. Conversation, Trace, Telemetry and Settings share a minimizable console below it. Detailed proximity/gripper readings, motion diagnostics, policy, token breakdown and home/spatial-map controls are under Telemetry; no duplicate sensor polling was added. Head/map HUDs remain mounted and independently minimizable. The spatial-map dialog is portalled to the page body so it remains usable from a hidden/minimized console.

The goal disclosure starts minimized. The running instruction draft remains mounted across tabs; hiding or switching views issues no robot command. Existing saved Conversation/Trace/Settings preferences are retained; the new Telemetry selection and console/live-readout minimization are session-local, not new backend preferences. Next-run token settings use one shared value for the slider and exact numeric input. The slider covers 1-2,000,000 tokens with finer steps up to 100k; the numeric input preserves exact values and the existing validation range. Both remain disabled during active inference/control. The current/last run's reported usage remains distinct from the next-run setting and visible in the sticky control bar. Existing post-response threshold/overshoot semantics are unchanged.

The new layout test verifies the physical snapshot is unchanged by UI interaction. All **five sensor/HUD/accounting checks pass** (49.6 s), covering delayed/missing image independence, sampled movement-zone pixels without camera/motion changes, head/map preview minimization/expansion, focus return, interactivity, and exact token totals/disconnection behavior. The first HUD gate failed map-dialog expansion from a hidden Telemetry panel; moving the dialog out of that hidden ancestor fixed it. The initial portrait screenshot cropped the robot; aspect-aware initial framing was corrected and rechecked. **Semantic supervision/timing fixes retained; kitchen still unqualified (2026-09-16):** `semantic-room-v2` addresses UC-08/10/18 following the user's request to fix and retest the failed visible kitchen search. Hypotheses: a model plan must not disable requested supervision; repeated moving reviews must not block the physics worker for new captures/full map reconstruction; and an offered observed destination must be revalidated for current clearance rather than required to remain the ranking winner. All three bounded real-model attempts below lack kitchen verification; the last shows better sustained progress but is not task success. No success threshold, scene, map or collision/lease limit was relaxed.

**Prior visible activation:** the explicitly requested restart activated semantic-mission-v1 in episode `e15796c0-864b-41be-ad5f-32470ba0ed29`, session `0200ce48-7eb3-42c9-9d64-40cc99a59faa`. The 90-second / 6-request / 30k-threshold trial recorded 3.757331 m, +16.39 m2 observed free area, zero contacts and 850 complete samples. Its only Luna call used 2,664 input / 69 output tokens but incorrectly chose kind=explore for finding the kitchen, triggering the old local-only shortcut and bypassing semantic verification. One buffer expiry recovered; the final session-timeout banner did not establish a kitchen receipt. This is real-model classification followed by local exploration, not a token-efficiency or room-success comparison. [Report](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T143152Z-17846c5b/flat_kitchen/report.json), [trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T143152Z-17846c5b/flat_kitchen/trace.json), [visible diagnostic stream](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/visible-restart-20260916-163032/browser-watch.json).

**Repairs:** targeted explore plans are rejected with corrective room/object/place guidance; only explicit mission_local_only bypasses model supervision. Moving reviews no longer offer ordinary navigate_frontier retargeting, while inspection/Stop and objective renewal remain available. Inputs distinguish continuation from choosing the next destination. The worker now uses nonblocking asynchronous sensor results during active mission feedback and objective renewal, still rejecting stale/mismatched evidence under original limits. Frequent objective polling and active feedback use copied lightweight task/localization/coverage state instead of recomputing candidate/graph clearance; stopped evidence still requests fresh capture and full choices. Supplied destination points remain fixed and are checked for same mission/run/map, finite/fresh stopped evidence and fresh footprint-safe route before and after preparation; ranking changes alone do not invalidate them. Generic local frontier ranking and the 0.5-second trajectory lease remain unchanged.

**Failed real-model attempts:** attempt 1 (`6fd7fd79-bc67-4e53-bedc-e067eaf80c44`) had corrected room classification and selected-frontier execution, 6.366286 m / 170.453 recorder wall seconds / zero contacts / 1,150 complete samples, but exhausted 12 requests at 47,987 tokens with no kitchen receipt. Two mid-route destination requests caused stop/new-evidence round trips; two buffer expiries recovered. Attempt 2 (`99314c55-f6d6-4c9d-8563-1abdf31f328e`) used the route-boundary correction and a separately declared 300-second / 20-request / 80k budget, recorded 2.366244 m / 116.735 s / zero contacts / 618 complete samples / 40,207 tokens, and stopped after buffer-expiry retry failures and repeated FRONTIER_SELECTION_CHANGED. These settings differ, so costs/times are not matched improvement claims. A user independently started another run after attempt 2; it was not interrupted or counted as an assistant-controlled retry. [Attempt 1](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T144624Z-38d3e44e/flat_kitchen/report.json), [attempt 1 trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T144624Z-38d3e44e/flat_kitchen/trace.json), [attempt 2](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T145248Z-f0de708d/flat_kitchen/report.json), [attempt 2 trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T145248Z-f0de708d/flat_kitchen/trace.json).

**Timing evidence:** attempt 2's expired intervals include mission_feedback taking 0.750 s with a forced sample taking 0.625 s, and mission_objective polling taking 0.406 s in full state work. Other renewal calls force 0.344-0.453 s captures. This supports removing those blocking operations, not extending leases. An initial render-geometry-cache hypothesis was withdrawn after a static probe saved only about 0.8 ms (1.427 vs 0.668 ms median). The existing diagnostic script now records thread CPU versus wall times and capture/snapshot subphases. Its unchanged Home-return diagnostic passed 18.938 s / 1.254936 m / zero contacts / no retries, but did not reproduce the live failure and is not a baseline replacement. [Blocking feedback incident](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T145248Z-f0de708d/flat_kitchen/recording/media/worker-timing-3.json), [polling incident](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T145248Z-f0de708d/flat_kitchen/recording/media/worker-timing-2.json), [instrumented diagnostic](../.runtime/semantic-room-v2/capture-profile-home-r2/timing.json).

**Verification:** classification/correction and continuing supervision pass 3 checks; route-boundary/schema/SDK guards pass 4; stable destination and nonblocking paths pass 10; the final motion gate passes 16, including explicit local-only versus supervised startup and all six enhanced delayed-review Stop/expiry/sensor-loss cases. The final contract gate passes 17 after the real trial, including room evidence, exact SDK payload and selection/ownership. Initial fixture/import/quoting/indentation failures were corrected without relaxing behavior checks. Counts overlap. The complete fixed series against renewal-timing-v1-final is **18 pass / 0 fail / 3 unchanged missing-room prerequisite blocks**, 108 frozen source/build files, SHA `9af353c5815963e99cdbc7faf364e8be48adcbe9e003c4e76c4566c811066650`, all still matched after the trial; unchanged source/map. This preserves local mechanical checks; real kitchen verification and under-load no-expiry reliability remain unproved.
