**Final visible attempt 3:** episode `41fc410d-ca1e-48ed-b5ff-232bbe64b84e`, session `9244dca5-ef8d-400f-99a0-d5c1f555552c`, ran with the unchanged kitchen goal, low reasoning, 300-second mission budget, 20-request limit and 80k-token threshold. It recorded **8.958382 m actual travel**, **+41.15 m2 observed free area** (29.86 -> 71.01), **zero contact episodes**, 1,697 complete trajectory samples without drops, and 11 persisted timing incidents without omission/drop. Recorder wall time was 219.313 s; the browser listener spanned 220.559 s, and final physics time was 78.10 s. Luna made 19 requests using **78,674 input + 2,160 output = 80,834 tokens**. The last response crossed the threshold; the run ended limited, not successfully completed. The independent kitchen scenario remained `in_progress`, with zero completed objectives and no target receipt. The only room report was "possible kitchen" at confidence 0.55 with no clearly distinguishable cooking appliance. This report was tentative, not verified identity. No reported room name was promoted to evaluator truth. [Report](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T151441Z-53a42051/flat_kitchen/report.json), [trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T151441Z-53a42051/flat_kitchen/trace.json), [recorded replay](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T151441Z-53a42051/flat_kitchen/recording/replay.html), [terminal screenshot](../.runtime/semantic-room-v2/attempt3-terminal.png), [browser observations](../.runtime/semantic-room-v2/attempt3-watch.json).

The user observed smoother/faster motion during this trial. There were no watchdog stops in its first 126 seconds, and the doorway route completed where attempt 2 had repeatedly expired. One BUFFER_EXPIRED later recovered at approximately 142.849 browser seconds. Its accepted renewal was at monotonic 436544.500, expiry and stop at 436545.000, with command sensor age 0.360 s. The 0.375 s motion tick, following 0.094 s map tick and next 0.016 s sensor reception consumed nearly the full renewal interval; physics publication included 0.234 s sensing work. These nested wall times must not be added twice. No lease or speed increase was used. The single recovered expiry means the timing issue is reduced in this observed path, not eliminated or a quantified causal speedup. Different model choices, changed code and live workstation load limit comparisons. [Remaining expiry](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T151441Z-53a42051/flat_kitchen/recording/media/worker-timing-7.json).

Attempt 3's automatic manifest froze 42 backend/runtime files at SHA `acca3e58ba8cf6b2644b7c82dad1e21557d761bf6cc03e0db3ff4f8e240ee743`; source unchanged during the trial and all matched afterward. This manifest covers a different file set from the 108-file fixed series, not a conflicting fingerprint. The predeclared cap of three assistant-controlled real-model retests was reached: all failures are retained, with 169,028 total reported tokens across those three attempts (the prior 2,733-token activation trial and independently started user run are separate). No fourth paid retry, mid-run tuning or manual motion intervention was made. Original next-run settings were restored to 180 seconds / 12 requests / 100k tokens / low reasoning. The final scene is stopped/inactive; current server PID 5184 on port 8000 has the retained fixes loaded, and only that simulator listener remains. The next substantive blocker is grounded room perception/search/arrival, alongside residual worker timing, not permission to declare success after movement alone. The full user goal remains unresolved; full-suite and matched real-model reliability/cost claims are not made.

**Robot appearance refinement (2026-09-16):** `robot-appearance-v1` tests the hypothesis that rounded shells, differentiated finishes and small mechanical details make Milo easier to read in the spectator view without changing its physical embodiment or sensor images. The existing white/raspberry body now has a twin-lens visor, inset chest and service panels, chassis vents, wheel rims/spokes, arm sleeves, joint trim and gripper pads. Decorations inherit the existing visual-part transforms; their geometries/materials are disposed with the spectator scene. This is an operator-only visual treatment in all scenarios, verified on Practice Bench at 1440/390/320 px. The URDF, collision geometry, masses, joints, worker, model inputs and shared GPU camera rendering behavior are unchanged. Cosmetic lights are static materials, not new sensor/status indicators. [Appearance implementation](../frontend/src/sceneGraphics.ts), [spectator integration](../frontend/src/Spectator.tsx), [desktop](../.runtime/robot-appearance-v1/robot-1440.png), [phone](../.runtime/robot-appearance-v1/robot-320.png), [head turned](../.runtime/robot-appearance-v1/robot-head-turned.png).

**Evidence and limitations:** frontend build passes with the existing bundle-size warning. The new scripted browser check passes: nonblank WebGL across the three widths, no page overflow, unchanged geometry/observation/snapshot and camera bytes during viewing, head motion changing the spectator image, and frozen physics after Stop. Existing GPU snapshot RGB/depth determinism and episode rejection pass; mouse selection/dragging pass, and unchanged touch selection/dragging pass in isolation. The first new test used an obsolete stopped-status label, corrected to the current UI plus the actual stop latch. The old color picker mistook new small pink accents for the torso; it now picks a continuous body-paint span. A combined check still had a touch setup failure (manual control disabled before clicking); its isolated pass does not establish a fix for that inter-test failure. [Initial appearance check](../.runtime/robot-appearance-v1/browser.xml), [passing appearance check](../.runtime/robot-appearance-v1/browser-final.xml), [initial selection check](../.runtime/robot-appearance-v1/isolation-selection.xml), [combined checks](../.runtime/robot-appearance-v1/isolation-selection-final.xml), [isolated touch pass](../.runtime/robot-appearance-v1/touch-isolated.xml). No full test suite, frame-rate/GPU-memory benchmark or real-model evaluation was run; no navigation/control improvement is claimed or benchmark series substituted. The active port-8000 session was not reloaded, restarted or reset; its next frontend refresh loads the new appearance.

**Compact semantic contract implemented; final qualification incomplete (2026-09-16):** `semantic-mission-v1` addresses UC-08/10/18 after the user approved the role/input audit. Hypothesis: task-scoped semantic inputs and an executable observed-destination choice reduce irrelevant context and intent/control mismatch without weakening worker authority. Implemented current-mission task filtering, current status separate from two historical failures, compact decision/result history, mission-specific prompts and action schemas, per-request effective prompt/schema and byte-count trace fields, and preserved room evidence/receipts. The operator keeps full capability/operation diagnostics and precise stored room records. The compact viewer omits absent joints/grippers without crashing. [Contract guide](HOME_MAPPING.md#compact-semantic-mission-contract).

Luna may now choose `navigate_frontier` from up to four sensor-derived IDs ranked by distance, heading and attempts. The worker revalidates membership, map/mission/run/epoch, capture age, stopped pose, current footprint reachability and target stability, including after preparation. This selects the existing bounded single-frontier controller; it does not let model prose steer wheels, bypass unknown space or automatically prove room arrival. A moving change still stops and reacquires evidence before execution. Worker-rate odometry supplies a bounded 128-point trail at 0.025 m spacing, cut at the paired-camera time and explicitly labelled when truncated. This captures intervening turns but is not full-run motion memory. The sticky controls now show reported tokens / threshold, request counts and incomplete usage across desktop/mobile. No cadence, lease, sensor, speed, retry, mission-budget or provider settings were relaxed.

**Measured input size (offline, not real-model savings):** replaying the nine archived requests from session `67c93474-6f72-48e2-bdc5-7544d23dc6d8` through the new projection reduces current-message JSON from **198,668 to 44,288 bytes (77.71%)** overall; per-request reduction is 69.56-83.27%. Individual projected requests are 4,214-6,119 bytes in this replay. Room instructions are 2,880 characters, with the tool schema restricted to the archived action set. This compares the current-message projection on the same old observations, not a new candidate rollout or retained-history replay. It does not count image tokens, provider tokenization/caching, changing candidate availability, latency or total billed cost. No paid/model request was made. [Reproducible projection script](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/measure_payload.py), [measurement](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/payload-comparison.json).

**Focused verification:** projection/ownership guards, actual SDK image/prompt/schema identity, semantic room-record persistence, unknown frontier rejection, finite/time/pose/authority bounds and seven worker preparation-race cases pass. Scripted real physics selects a supplied frontier and moves >0.15 m with no contacts; an invented ID is rejected without a task, and the model map contains inter-review trail points. Existing delayed-review Stop/expiry/stale-sensor cases also pass. [Destination physics](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/destination-physics.xml), [final provenance checks](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/provenance-final.xml), [worker races](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/worker-selection-races.xml), [exact SDK contract](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/exact-sdk-contract.xml), [room evidence repair](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/report-provenance-repair.xml).

The initial broad backend gate had **281 pass / 3 fail**: two omitted room-report evidence assertions and one minimal no-map fixture incompatibility. These were repaired and all three focused cases pass. The subsequent mission/review/evaluation gate had **168 pass / 1 fail** in 951.11 s: the existing 25-second local-exploration case entered a guarded survey without reaching its expected checkpoint before the budget. It failed unchanged in isolated attempts, then passed after adding structured assertion output only; no runtime or acceptance change was made to turn that failure green. This remains observed timing sensitivity, not a clean all-green final suite or a resolved cause. Earlier patch-placement/import failures are preserved in their initial XML artifacts. Counts overlap and must not be pooled. [Initial broad gate](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/backend-gate.xml), [final mission gate](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/final-mission-gate.xml), [isolated failure](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/local-explore-isolated.xml), [unchanged behavior later pass](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/local-explore-reason.xml).

**Fixed baseline series:** first complete comparison against `renewal-timing-v1-final-20260916` had **18 pass / 0 fail / 3 blocked**. After the compact trace-viewer build repair, the complete final comparison has **17 pass / 1 fail / the same 3 blocked**. The failure is `return_home-r2`: checkpoint arrival verified, then three BUFFER_EXPIRED retries returning Home; 0.978032 m actual travel, final Home error 0.315117 m, no contacts, complete recording. Its accepted-renewal-to-stop gaps were 0.922 / 0.640 / 0.734 s against the unchanged 0.500 s buffer. Stop snapshots show navigation ticks 0.672 / 0.562 / 0.672 s; in the first and third, sensing.sample_spatial takes 0.422 / 0.438 s inside physics publication. These are nested wall times, not additive CPU times or proof of candidate causality. This benchmark runs no Luna mission and does not exercise semantic choices. No third series was run to replace the failure. Final source SHA **268119455b472056d8005fe625a2f78c69c4dcb0e236f66291d575312d9e0b6a**, 108 source/build files, all verified unchanged after the series; original map unchanged. No speedup or general autonomy claim. [First series](../.runtime/performance/semantic-mission-v1-20260916/summary.md), [final series](../.runtime/performance/semantic-mission-v1-final-20260916/summary.md), [comparison](../.runtime/performance/semantic-mission-v1-final-20260916/comparison.json), [failed Home case](../.runtime/performance/semantic-mission-v1-final-20260916/return_home-r2/report.json), [first expiry timing](../.runtime/performance/semantic-mission-v1-final-20260916/return_home-r2/recording/media/worker-timing-2.json).

Frontend build passes with the existing bundle-size warning. The complete mission UI file passes **10/10** in 53.0 s after fixing the trace viewer's assumption that every input includes joints/grippers. Initial 9/10 failure and its cause were not a server or model timeout. Sticky usage counters, large values, threshold overshoot, paired map images, Stop and 1440/850/390/320-pixel layouts are covered. Existing Windows connection-reset callback warnings remain. The rest of the frontend suite was not rerun; earlier unrelated failures are not claimed fixed. [Desktop usage screenshot](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/usage-1440.png), [mobile usage screenshot](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/usage-320.png), [verification summary](../.runtime/semantic-mission-v1/verification.json).

**Activation and remaining limits:** the implementation is on disk but the running backend was not restarted, no scene reset or new user mission was started, and no paid validation was performed. Latest read-only check retains episode `0a63aa4a-f82e-4653-81d8-4ca8a7db312a`, session `67c93474-6f72-48e2-bdc5-7544d23dc6d8`, stopped/inactive at 107.45 simulated seconds and 106,273 tokens. Activation remains pending because the required final regression series is not clean. Real-Luna interpretation, provider savings, continuous directional commitment and successful room arrival remain unmeasured. Candidate computation can add worker work during reviews; no under-load latency benefit is claimed. Generic frontier-selection/backtracking policy, local checkpoint pauses, session-timeout wording and the existing short-buffer scheduling issue are not fixed by input compaction. The next decision is a separately scoped worker-timing investigation or accepting this candidate's explicitly unqualified runtime status, not extending leases or masking failures.

**Luna role/input contract audit (2026-09-16):** `luna-contract-audit-v1` reviews UC-08/10/18 against the user's latest completed room-finding session `67c93474-6f72-48e2-bdc5-7544d23dc6d8`. The hypothesis is that broad operational telemetry and a narrow exploration API are poorly matched to a semantic supervisor expected to choose useful directions. This is a read-only runtime/source and finalized-trace audit, not a prompt optimization, new model trial or navigation improvement experiment. The manifest labels the user run `real_model`, source unchanged, SHA `52f700d2df906d39b7f973cf837e3df024c4ef1deccb9abebc00e9101a1f0378`. [Manifest](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T124230Z-ce0d8bb1/experiment.json), [report](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T124230Z-ce0d8bb1/flat_kitchen/report.json), [trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T124230Z-ce0d8bb1/flat_kitchen/trace.json).

**Actual role and payload:** the unified prompt delegates routine frontier selection, routes, footprint checks, collisions and short leases to the worker; Luna classifies the goal, authorizes/renews exploration, identifies fixtures/objects, requests inspection and selects supplied named places/capabilities. It explicitly forbids choosing frontier IDs and removes that field from the tool schema. Each real review sends the original goal and broad multi-task instructions/tool schema, a current head image and a 512 x 552 observed-map image (both high detail), plus JSON containing AgentObservation, mission, object goal, last execution and available actions. AgentObservation includes joints, grippers, proximity, navigation diagnostics, spatial/task/localization state and map metadata. Occupancy cells are omitted from text and supplied through the typed observed-map raster; hidden spectator geometry is not a legitimate substitute. Up to six prior decision/result pairs are considered for text history. The 8,192 setting bounds historical context using a conservative byte-based estimator; it is not a total-request token cap. Python control code and a private reasoning transcript are not sent. [Prompt and tool contract](../backend/mission_supervisor.py#L23), [request assembly](../backend/mission_supervisor.py#L280), [SDK call](../backend/agent.py#L247), [history bound](../backend/feedback.py#L28).

**Measured token/data facts:** UI and report show **106,273 reported tokens: 104,932 input / 1,341 output (98.74% input)**, nine requests against a 12-request limit and a 100,000-token threshold. The eight retained responses use 9,871-14,545 input tokens apiece; their decisions are one plan, five explore and two look calls. Report totals exceed retained response totals by 12,419 input / 216 output tokens: the ninth response crossed the threshold and BudgetedModel counted its usage but raised before returning it for decision tracing/execution. This is a post-response threshold, not a strict maximum charge. No input/image/history token attribution or cached-token pricing breakdown is retained, so those component costs are not claimed. Compact JSON character counts show the observation alone at approximately 10-21k characters and last_execution up to 10k, before instructions, schema, images and history; these are text-size measurements, not token counts. The existing token counter and threshold were scrolled into view without changing settings. [Budget enforcement](../backend/agent.py#L397), [counter placement](../frontend/src/LunaNavigationControl.tsx#L218).

**Confirmed mismatches:**
- Intent versus executable direction: an active `explore` reply renews time/travel authority, not a route preference. Natural-language "avoid revisiting" does not constrain the planner. Numbered frontier candidates can appear when stopped, but the model cannot select them; during moving reviews they are withheld. Thus this API is semantic supervision of generic exploration, not useful branch/doorway selection. [Renewal dispatch](../backend/mission_supervisor.py#L388), [worker binding](../backend/worker.py#L964), [frontier exposure](../backend/home_mission.py#L782).
- Current versus historical state: feedback event 2 includes prior session `526718dd-a861-43fe-82ff-1956a3d10f6b`'s failed task `052a6068-4634-4d34-a31e-669de62dd74e` inside observation.spatial.task while the new mission is only planning. Event 19 reports both navigation and task running, but retains "Replanning after OBSERVED_PATH_BLOCKED" and old failures. Response 20 describes the route as blocked and requests look, producing a stopped-evidence round trip. This establishes an observable status ambiguity and response, not proof that removing one field alone would change model behavior. [Prior session terminal task](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123927Z-32476fb8/flat_kitchen/trace.json), [spatial task serialization](../backend/home_mission.py#L749).
- Semantic role versus payload volume: joints/grippers, detailed renewal/event histories, long-precision coordinates and duplicate spatial state in observation.spatial/last_execution are sent even for room finding. The current path uses json.dumps rather than the existing compact_numbers/feedback_json helper. Current status and a bounded failure/progress summary are more suitable candidate inputs; full diagnostics should remain in operator recordings. Removing payload must not remove freshness, frame, identity/arrival uncertainty, allowed actions or safety evidence relevant to a semantic decision.
- Movement memory versus painted path: an observed-map image with green travelled and pink planned lines already exists. The green trail samples poses only at model reviews, after >0.1 m displacement, and joins those samples with straight lines. This run's final supplied trail has eight points. It is not a complete controller-rate trajectory and may omit intervening turns/revisits; it resets per mission. The pink route is a plan, not proof of executed motion or fresh authority. [Trail sampling](../backend/mission_supervisor.py#L280), [sensor-only map construction](../backend/worker.py#L1121), [raster drawing](../backend/map_context.py#L49).
- Cost versus review benefit: repeated explore replies authorize the same broad task, while inspections require a fresh stopped observation and another decision. Input dominates usage; lowering reasoning effort alone is unlikely to address the largest measured token category, and this run already used low effort. Review selection and summary quality should be assessed together, without bypassing objective expiry, serialized inference or worker safety.

**Proposed contract, not implemented:** retain the head image for fixture identification and an observed-only map with a bounded, accurately sampled travelled path. Send a typed semantic brief with mission/receipts, current task and target, pose/frame/age, progress since review, current safety/localization status, a clearly separated recent-failure summary, remaining budgets and a small set of fresh worker-generated branch/destination choices. Let Luna choose bounded intent/observed IDs, continue, inspect or finish; leave trajectory generation, numeric collision control and short-lease renewal local. Use task/phase-specific instructions and compact structured memory instead of repeated full execution objects. Consider event/progress-aware reviews while preserving objective deadlines; any renewal-policy changes require separate safety tests. Show used/threshold and request count beside mission controls. These changes require user approval, token-accounted payload tests and the complete fixed benchmark series for navigation/control changes; token savings and behavioral effects remain unmeasured. No cloud operation, model request, live motion, prompt change or runtime/UI code edit was made for this audit.

**User-run turnback investigation (2026-09-16):** `turnback-analysis-v1` examines UC-08/10/18 using the finalized real-model session `526718dd-a861-43fe-82ff-1956a3d10f6b`, episode `0a63aa4a-f82e-4653-81d8-4ca8a7db312a`. This is the user's later kitchen-finding run, not the preceding local-only test. The hypothesis is that local frontier selection reversed progress after an arrival, rather than Luna requesting a return or an immediate safety rejection. The immutable trace and trajectory support that distinction. The manifest labels evidence `real_model`, source SHA `52f700d2df906d39b7f973cf837e3df024c4ef1deccb9abebc00e9101a1f0378`, and source unchanged. Recorded outcome: 2.947965 m actual travel, 113.297 s recorder wall time, 842 samples, complete recording and zero contact episodes. This was not a completed kitchen mission or a matched improvement experiment. [Report](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123927Z-32476fb8/flat_kitchen/report.json), [manifest](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123927Z-32476fb8/experiment.json), [trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123927Z-32476fb8/flat_kitchen/trace.json).

At trajectory sample 594, **53.55 s simulated / 74.141 s recorder wall time**, the robot was near map pose `[0.513, 0.437, 0.336]`, facing about 19 degrees. After reaching frontier `1:0` at `[0.55, 0.45]`, the local task selected frontier `-1:0` at `[-0.45, 0.35]`: about **0.97 m away and a 166-degree turn**, back toward the previously traversed sofa side. Visited frontiers increased 2 -> 3 and segments 4 -> 5; retries stayed at 1. No new route failure or contact triggered this switch. The task retained wording about an earlier BUFFER_EXPIRED, but that was not a new rejection at the turn. A later expiry retried the same backward target, and arrival there selected another target farther back at `[-0.85, 0.35]`. The run ultimately exhausted three buffer-expiry retries and ended at the token threshold, separately from the original U-turn. [Before selection](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123927Z-32476fb8/flat_kitchen/recording/media/telemetry-120.json), [after selection](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123927Z-32476fb8/flat_kitchen/recording/media/telemetry-121.json), [trajectory](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123927Z-32476fb8/flat_kitchen/recording/trajectory.jsonl).

Trace decision 20 explicitly requests avoiding revisits and inspecting open continuations; every post-plan decision is `explore`, with no return or turn instruction. During an active mapped operation, this action renews objective authority; its natural-language reason does not become a heading or doorway constraint. In `HomeMission.tick`, arrival marks the frontier, clears its target and selects the first result of `HomeMap.frontiers`. That selector scores cells within each connected frontier group using preferred distance near 1 m, heading and cell visits, but its final inter-group ranking uses only distance plus attempt count. The near-forward continuation guard applies within an existing route, not across newly selected frontiers. This permits local backtracking despite the model's high-level instruction. [Objective renewal](../backend/mission_supervisor.py#L388), [arrival and reselection](../backend/home_mission.py#L631), [frontier ranking](../backend/home_mapping.py#L213), [within-route continuation](../backend/home_mission.py#L706).

**Limits and recommendation:** this establishes the selected backward target and controlling code, not the precise ranking of every rejected alternative. Recorded maps retain occupancy and visited bits, not full visit counts and candidate score history, so no exact frontier-ranking replay or proof of a clearance-valid straight-ahead alternative is claimed. An open-looking spectator view is not swept-footprint evidence. The next proposed policy change is consistent heading/revisit/information-gain ranking across candidates and continuity across frontier arrivals, with explicit reasons for backtracking, while retaining fresh observed-corridor and worker safety checks. Effects remain unmeasured and would require the complete fixed baseline series. The screenshot's "Kitchen identified ..." banner is the plan's target text, not an arrival receipt; the trace repeatedly says kitchen identity is not established. No runtime code, robot command, model call, server restart or new mission was performed for this investigation. A live trace read rejected the old session after the user moved on; analysis therefore used only the matching finalized recording.

**Restart and operator-observable diagnostic completed (2026-09-16):** `observed-local-test-v1` tests the hypothesis that the updated worker-timing recorder can identify a naturally occurring buffer expiry in the actual browser workload. The user explicitly requested restart and a visible test. The stopped prior episode/session was archived before its port-8000 process was replaced using the normal managed launcher. The saved-map preference was retained, but the old "Unsaved home" was a draft, not restorable saved geometry: the new test's manifest correctly records starting map mode `none`. Exactly one 60-second **local-only unified exploration** was started from the shared browser, with the movement-zone overlay and motion diagnostics visible. No paid/model inference, pose teleport, mid-run tuning or retry of the test was performed. This is an operator-observed local diagnostic, not an AP-HF1 baseline or a real-Luna/semantic-room trial. [Prior-scene archive](../.runtime/observed-test-restart-20260916-143058/state.json), [archived draft map](../.runtime/observed-test-restart-20260916-143058/home.json).

Run `0a63aa4a-f82e-4653-81d8-4ca8a7db312a`, session `0a1beb23-bf85-4d3d-bc7d-4c3c71beff42`, recorded **2.019375 m actual travel**, **16.19 m2 added observed free area** (29.86 -> 46.05), **zero contact episodes**, **514 trajectory samples**, zero dropped records and six persisted timing incidents. Final physics time was 25.60 s; recorder wall time was 59.609 s, while the browser listener spanned 60.835 s including startup/finalization. No recorded timing incidents were dropped or omitted. One read-only browser WebSocket sampled diagnostics at approximately 0.5-second intervals and retained state changes: 111 samples, 22 changes, zero local log drops. This listener observed the production scene, not another simulator. The automatic run report labels the evidence `scripted_test`, the source unchanged, and contains zero input/output tokens. [Run report](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123302Z-a79184b9/flat_kitchen/report.json), [trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123302Z-a79184b9/flat_kitchen/trace.json), [recording replay](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123302Z-a79184b9/flat_kitchen/recording/replay.html), [browser diagnostic samples](../.runtime/observed-test-restart-20260916-143058/browser-watch.json).

**Observed expiry:** the first route reached its point at about 25 seconds. The second route incurred one BUFFER_EXPIRED around browser time 39.225 s and recovered. Its last accepted renewal and controller tick were at monotonic **426741.671**, with expiry **426742.171** and watchdog stop at **426742.171**. Its command sample was **0.265 s** old; the 1 s freshness limit had not rejected that sample. The timing snapshot shows `navigation.tick` from 426741.671 to 426742.109 (**0.438 s**), followed by `map.tick` to 426742.171 (**0.062 s**). Together those sequential spans consume the complete 0.500 s buffer window. Inside the motion tick, `publication.physics` took **0.328 s**, including `map.sample` **0.250 s**, recorder home capture **0.047 s** and spatial reception/sampling **0.016 s**; the earlier collision check took **0.047 s**. These are nested wall times, not additive CPU measurements. The following map tick included another **0.047 s** map sample. This identifies the critical worker phases for this incident, not the internal laser/integration/OS contribution to the broad map sample, and it does not retroactively attribute older Luna failures. [Natural expiry timing snapshot](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T123302Z-a79184b9/flat_kitchen/recording/media/worker-timing-3.json).

The sampled running-state diagnostics showed accepted-command depth ages **0.125-0.516 s**, reported buffer time remaining **0.250-0.485 s**, and a recent completed-tick-gap maximum **0.422 s**. The final 0.500 s interval was captured by the stop snapshot, not that preceding running-state maximum. Intermediate live readouts missed the brief expiry; the continuously retained state-change record and persisted incident caught it. The overlay intermittently showed Pose changed/Stale/Unavailable, correctly describing asynchronous display evidence rather than revoking controller motion.

**Other findings and limitations:** local-only unified exploration currently uses `mapped_operation` with deliberate bounded observation-checkpoint pauses, not the semantic `MappedReviewOperation` objective-renewal path. No exploration objective was reported in the live diagnostics, and this test does not validate the new deadline-aware Luna review scheduler. It exposed checkpoint pauses even without model inference. The overall run reached its configured session-time boundary and was reported as `error` / "Inference or session timed out; robot stopped" despite zero inference; that misleading local-only budget classification remains a separate finding, not a successful full mission. The last retained buffer stop was a checkpoint with 0.453 s of lease left, distinct from the earlier expiry. No fixes were made during this observation task and no overall success or performance-improvement claim is made.

The prior 180-second mission-budget preference and normal unified profile were restored after the test. The robot remains stopped/inactive in the observed episode, with Trace > Failures open on the expiry evidence and the movement-zone toggle enabled. The new port-8000 process is PID 20552, launched with the standard 300-second unused timeout; prior PID 80028 is gone and no other simulator ports remain. All current backend changes, including worker timing and movement zones, are now loaded. The next bounded control investigation should isolate redundant/slow home-map sampling in the critical publication/navigation loop while retaining all sensor and lease checks, rather than extending the lease or attributing this local-only failure to Luna latency.

**Deadline-aware review and local timing implemented (2026-09-16):** `renewal-timing-v1` addresses UC-10/UC-18 and INV-02/04/09/10 from the [use-case specification](USE_CASE_SPECIFICATION.md). The hypothesis is that requesting semantic review with measured preparation/response headroom prevents avoidably late objective renewal, while bounded worker phase snapshots make short-buffer stalls attributable. Reviews retain the 15 s / 2 m normal checkpoints and configured feedback interval, but also trigger when objective time remaining is at most `max(10, 1.25 * (max of last eight input durations + max of last eight response durations) + 2)` seconds. The trace records the trigger and reserve; prompt wording now reflects earlier review. No mission deadline, motion lease, sensor-age threshold, speed, travel limit, retry budget or inference budget was increased. [Behavior and log guide](HOME_MAPPING.md#deadline-aware-reviews).

The latest pre-change user run (`e124aac0-885b-4be7-8511-c2084c2e2600`) contained six short-buffer expiry failures and a final 30-second exploration-objective timeout after only 0.183 m of its 4.5 m allowance. It travelled 1.581 m over 133.719 wall seconds overall and ended at 105,627 reported tokens. That user run has not been rerun on this candidate. A deterministic regression injects its 4.645 s input preparation and a recorded 12.281 s response duration: the new deadline trigger allows review at 7 s and renewal at 24.426 s before a 30 s objective expires, where a 15 s checkpoint plus the same preparation/response/0.5 s handoff would reach 32.426 s. This demonstrates the scheduling branch with controlled timing, not a natural-cadence model success rate. [Original trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260916T113443Z-ca6c973b/flat_kitchen/trace.json), [final local guards](../.runtime/renewal-timing-v1/final-local-guards.xml).

`OBJECTIVE_TIME_EXPIRED` and `OBJECTIVE_DISTANCE_LIMIT` now distinguish the cause formerly labelled "duration or travel authorization expired". Time wins if both limits are crossed together; the original authority checks and no-resurrection behavior remain. `BUFFER_EXPIRED` and local-skill `MOTION_LEASE_EXPIRED` remain separate. Timing evidence also revealed Stop racing buffer application being reported as generic EXECUTION_FAILED in two first-series cancellation cases; the worker now preserves `CANCELLED` / `external_stop` only for a MotionError cancellation with the actual Stop latch set. Other exceptions remain execution failures, and timing spans retain their type/code without copying exception messages. The first-series artifacts remain unchanged.

The new worker timing ring retains 512 completed spans/events and eight pending stop snapshots, including incomplete active spans at a stop. It measures queued operations, sensing, map work, collision checks, publication, recording capture and intentional pacing, with nested inclusive/self wall time, not CPU time. The validated worker brakes before taking an immutable stop snapshot. Each attached RunRecorder transfers at most 128 incident files through its existing pending queue and background flush; it records omission/drop counters and trajectory references. No new writer process, direct worker disk I/O or external telemetry service was added. Full buffers can evict context; abrupt shutdown can lose the unwritten tail; parent/child/queue timings overlap. This is bounded attribution evidence, not an exact whole-run profiler or crash-proof model trace journal.

**Validation:** 8 initial scheduling checks, 19 objective/scheduling checks, deterministic ring tests and a forced-expiry persistence test passed. The final affected gate passes **173 tests** (237.40 s), including six enhanced-physics delayed-review variants, normal/error/interrupted recorder finalization, worker queue fairness, Stop races, objective boundaries and the existing policy units. The persistence test verifies no expired-command physics advance and no timing file before an explicit background flush. Existing JUnit record_property and Starlette deprecation warnings remain; no tests failed. [Initial review gate](../.runtime/renewal-timing-v1/review-first.xml), [objective gate](../.runtime/renewal-timing-v1/objective-gate.xml), [timing core](../.runtime/renewal-timing-v1/timing-core.xml), [persistence gate](../.runtime/renewal-timing-v1/timing-persistence.xml), [final integration gate](../.runtime/renewal-timing-v1/final-integration-gate.xml). These counts overlap and are not pooled into a larger pass total.

Both complete frozen household comparisons against `capability-review-v1-20260916` retain **18 passes, zero failures and the same three missing-room prerequisites blocked**, with no invalid/unrun cases. Final label `renewal-timing-v1-final` froze 108 source/build files at SHA `7522a7258026ca09202dcaf95b159e7c438f5feb124ae0be084360bd10a4c9b3`; all matched disk after evaluation, `source_changed_during_run=false` and the original map stayed unchanged. The final recordings contain **28 worker-timing incidents**: 22 arrivals, three task-budget timeouts and three correctly attributed external cancellations. No naturally occurring BUFFER_EXPIRED incident occurred in that final fixed series; expiry persistence is established by the injected regression, not inferred from these arrivals. [First series](../.runtime/performance/renewal-timing-v1-20260916/summary.md), [final series](../.runtime/performance/renewal-timing-v1-final-20260916/summary.md), [matched comparison](../.runtime/performance/renewal-timing-v1-final-20260916/comparison.json), [final manifest](../.runtime/performance/renewal-timing-v1-final-20260916/experiment.json), [saved timing example](../.runtime/performance/renewal-timing-v1-final-20260916/cancel-r1/recording/media/worker-timing-1.json).

**Limitations:** these checks preserve the scripted baseline; they do not establish faster navigation, lower token cost or solved real-model stalls. Final checkpoint/Home medians were 8.344/19.968 s versus 7.625/17.906 s in the referenced baseline; sequential workstation runs and multiple changes prevent attributing that difference to timing overhead. No matched logging-on/off overhead experiment, whole-suite frontend run, paid model trial or cloud operation was performed. Earlier full-frontend failures remain unqualified. The oldest evidence cannot gain missing worker timings retroactively. The existing live process was not restarted or reset: run `3f49002d-503b-484b-8be5-3de8633750e7`, session `e124aac0-885b-4be7-8511-c2084c2e2600`, remains inactive/stopped at 45.65 simulated seconds, with only port 8000/PID 80028 listening. **A backend restart is required to activate these changes and future-run timing logs.**

**Use-case specification standardized (2026-09-16):** `use-case-specification-v1` consolidates existing task intent and acceptance references into [USE_CASE_SPECIFICATION.md](USE_CASE_SPECIFICATION.md), specification ID `milo-use-cases-v1.0`. The hypothesis is that stable references will prevent requirements, scripted feasibility and real-model qualification from being conflated across iterations. Nineteen `UC-*` entries and eleven shared `INV-*` rules cover session/map lifecycle, navigation and recovery, object/room identity, safety, delayed supervision, circuits, parking/charging, manipulation, redirection, endurance and operator diagnostics, with future extensions explicitly pending profiles. Each concrete use case records its goal, prerequisites, expected behavior, acceptance and negative cases; the endurance and extension targets retain unresolved criteria rather than invented qualification thresholds. A quick-reference index, evidence taxonomy, dated status snapshot, JSON run-record template and version/change process make the catalog reusable. README and curriculum now link to it.

Documentation checks verified unique IDs and resolved UC/INV references, all seven AP-HF1 case/budget bindings against the frozen household suite, local links, and valid run-template JSON. Existing benchmark criteria, source maps, test/scorer implementations and historical outcomes are unchanged. These checks establish document consistency, not improved behavior or user adoption. No physics/browser/model tests, cloud calls, policy changes, live robot commands or restart were needed for this documentation-only task. Future changes should reference the UC/INV IDs and update the ledger with evidence; this specification does not declare pending features implemented or erase current failures.

**Deterministic motion-policy unit tests added (2026-09-16):** `policy-unit-tests-v1` tests the hypothesis that decision-level regressions can be detected without relying on long simulator runs. The existing [navigation test file](../tests/test_navigation.py) now has a shared fake-clock fixture and **63 new parameterized unit cases** calling the real `ContinuousNavigation.update` with mocked sensor inputs, path validation and executor methods. Tests cover clear-space forward motion, exact front-beam speed reductions and irrelevant rear/side readings, heading thresholds, approach slowdown, reduced-speed/rotation fallback, Stop precedence, failed/cancelled/clearance-stopped executors, missing/stale/future depth, exact sensor-age and buffer-expiry boundaries, refresh throttling without braking, translation/rotation progress, arrival tolerance, bounded same-target replanning, exhausted retries/distance budgets, empty-buffer rejection and accepted-command sample age after observation work. Invalid terminal decisions cannot resume by merely refreshing inputs. No runtime, safety threshold, mission prompt or motion authority was changed.

The first speed/heading/path slice passed **28 cases** (3.40 s); all new units then passed **63 cases** (2.85 s). The final gate passed **82 cases** (4.02 s): the 63 new units plus 19 existing threshold, stop-attribution and physics-backed braking/replanning/rotation regressions. No failures or skips were reported. [Initial gate](../.runtime/policy-unit-tests-v1/first-gate.xml), [new unit suite](../.runtime/policy-unit-tests-v1/policy-gate.xml), [combined regression gate](../.runtime/policy-unit-tests-v1/final-gate.xml). Reproduce the fast new slice with `./.runtime/env/python.exe -m pytest -q tests/test_navigation.py -k continuous_policy`.

The new cases verify requested velocities, decisions and calls into the executor, not physical braking, map accuracy or worker scheduling under load; neighboring existing tests provide limited runtime corroboration. They neither reproduce nor fix the earlier real-Luna buffer-refresh stall, and they are not model-quality or hardware-safety evidence. Full physics/browser suites and the fixed household series were not rerun for this test-only change. No live server requests, robot commands, new model calls or restarts were made.

**Capability overview evaluated (2026-09-16):** `capability-review-v1` tests the hypothesis that local motion and basic physical skills remain usable while the full Luna-driven mission has separate reliability failures. No runtime, policy, UI, test, budget or scoring changes were made. The complete fixed household series was rerun once against `policy-clearance-v1-final-20260916`, followed by twelve doorway/localization/delayed-review cases and twenty-two mechanical skill cases. All newly executed checks passed except the three deliberately retained blocked room prerequisites; intermediate controller recoveries and earlier real-model failures remain visible below. These heterogeneous counts are not pooled into an autonomy score.

| Capability | New result | Evidence and limitation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Relocalize on saved map | 3/3 passed; 0.265 s median task time | Sensor scan matching with a saved-Home hint, excluding preparation; not arbitrary global localization. |
| Reach a nearby mapped checkpoint | 3/3 passed; 7.625 s median | Fixed 0.7 m map checkpoint, three starting offsets, independent arrival/dwell checks; not room identification. |
| Checkpoint then return Home | 3/3 passed; 17.906 s median | Both legs independently verified, excluding setup. |
| Expand a partial observed map | 3/3 passed | 2.361/2.961/3.378 m actual travel and 3.66/5.58/6.46 m2 added free area across the three 30-second trials; task-budget termination is expected, not whole-house completion. Two trials contained observed-path rejection/recovery. |
| Reject an occupied destination | 3/3 passed | Correct no-contact rejection, not proof of alternate-route finding. |
| Stop during mapped motion | 3/3 passed | Acknowledgement 0.110-0.141 s after at least 0.10 m travel; frozen ticks, cleared buffers and old-command rejection verified. |
| Navigate between named rooms | 0 executed; 3 blocked prerequisites | The frozen map still lacks two named room destinations at least 2 m apart. No replacement checkpoint was counted as a room. |
| Round the doorway corner | 3/3 arrivals, plus Stop and closed-door cases passed | Arrival task times 29.625/27.922/38.235 s, travel 2.000/1.908/2.122 m, zero contacts, unchanged 45 s limit. One arrival recovered from BUFFER_EXPIRED and another from OBSERVED_PATH_BLOCKED; success was not uninterrupted motion. Stop froze after 0.151 m; a closed doorway was rejected with zero travel. |
| Continue while a supervisor reply is delayed | 6/6 boundary cases passed | Continue, Stop, objective expiry, stale timestamp, actual sensor loss and path blockage use enhanced physics with scripted replies and a phase-aligned trigger. Invalidated replies cannot restart motion. Not natural-cadence, five-minute Luna qualification. |
| Correct a seeded localization offset | 1/1 passed | Fresh scan against a frozen map with injected XY/yaw drift; bounded seeded search, not continuous pose correction. |
| Park, pick/place, sort and recharge | 6/6 passed | Parking (1), pick/place variants (3), both-cube sorting (1), and charge round trip (1) use scripted wheel/arm commands and known task geometry. Mechanical feasibility, not autonomous object selection or manipulation. |
| Circle furniture | 10/10 passed | Four standalone objects in both directions plus shared-apartment table in both directions. The test supplies the center, starts on the permitted annulus and sets tangent heading; it does not test model detection or approach selection. |
| Brake for a crossing pedestrian | 6/6 passed | Three commanded speeds and two clearance-stop modes; no pedestrian contact, followed by scripted continuation. Not real-model yielding/parking competence. |

The same code already passed the previous baseline, so this is another observation, not an improvement claim. Control-boundary tests took 360.44 s including setup/teardown; mechanical checks took 272.74 s. Twelve pytest JUnit-family warnings concerned `record_property` compatibility; the metric properties were present and read successfully.

**Where it still fails or lacks evidence:** the separately preserved real-Luna session `39eaedde-dfd4-4ee5-910b-7bb94ec753d1` recorded only 0.355058 m of objective-accounted travel over six exploration operations, each ending in exhausted BUFFER_EXPIRED retries. It ended at the token threshold after 106,186 reported tokens, with no mission receipts. This was not rerun and its metric is not interchangeable with the benchmark's evaluator trajectory. Today's recovered doorway expiry is additional evidence that intermittent refresh gaps remain possible even in a passing run. The exact blocking worker operation under the original saved-map/model/publication workload is still unmeasured. Reliable room recognition/arrival, arbitrary object selection/manipulation, continuous drift correction, complete house coverage, five-minute multi-start real-Luna missions and hardware safety remain unqualified. The next discriminating control experiment is a same-saved-map replay with the original publication/recording workload and scripted Luna latency while profiling worker gaps, not longer leases or further unbounded model trials. No new paid inference, cloud request, model training, live restart/reset or code repair was performed. The operator run remained `3f49002d-503b-484b-8be5-3de8633750e7`, inactive at 20.95 simulated seconds; only port 8000/PID 80028 remains. Movement-zone activation still awaits the separately requested future backend restart.

**Motion-policy review and movement zones implemented (2026-09-16):** `policy-clearance-v1` reviews the hypothesis that incorrect distance calibration or unjustified clearance restrictions explain the user's repeated low-motion runs. The reviewed path is `ContinuousNavigation.update` -> observed-map `path_valid` -> `NavigationRuntime.check_clearance`, with separate objective/lease/freshness guards. Physical calibration tests confirm sensor-mount distances, heading-invariant collision footprints and actual folded-arm footprint reduction in their fixtures. The beam stopping formula is unchanged and now shared by the guard and preview: minimum 0.18 m, about 0.302 m at 0.35 m/s and 0.522 m at 0.50 m/s. Circular map inflation and the near-obstacle speed cap are conservative by design; no test established that a safety margin should be reduced. Every previously inspected stopped operation had an explicit expiry cause. **This is not a fix or assurance for the unresolved saved-map Luna renewal stalls.** [Policy/zone definitions](MECHANICS.md#movement-zones), [27 safety/calibration checks](../.runtime/policy-clearance-v1/safety-gate.xml).

One existing instrumented **30-second fresh-map sensor-only exploration diagnostic**, using enhanced rendering and no model calls, moved **2.511733 m**, added **16.14 m2** observed free area, recorded **zero contact episodes**, visited three frontiers and ended at the task budget with complete recording and unchanged original map. It did not reproduce the real-Luna run's buffer expiry: four segments, no retry failure reported. Inclusive instrumented maxima included home sampling 0.105 s, continuous update 0.121 s, whole-body clearance 0.042 s and spatial sampling 0.308 s. These overlap, include instrumentation overhead, and are not exclusive CPU totals or evidence that any one helper caused the earlier stalled run. The fixture creates a fresh map and has a different model/publication workload; it is a diagnostic, not a baseline replacement or an improvement claim. [Timing and source snapshot](../.runtime/policy-clearance-v1/review/timing.json), [recorded diagnostic outcome](../.runtime/policy-clearance-v1/review/fresh-exploration/report.json).

The operator-only **Movement zones** toggle adds sixteen sampled headings at 0.25/0.50/1.00 m robot-center travel radii, the real collision-footprint outline, the map-radius/margin circle, measured sensor rays and direction-specific beam-stop ticks. Colors distinguish sampled map-clear, restricted, unknown and unavailable space; hover reasons and a separate readings table explain each zone. Current or idle-reference velocity is labelled. Geometry-clear does not mean permission to move: the controller's stop reason remains separate. Sampling uses the existing home/rolling-map admission masks and observed live obstacles, never evaluator geometry. It does not reproduce the exact whole-body/dynamic collision shield or prove every point between samples. Disabled/paused/disconnected sensing, stale depth or localization, episode mismatch and moved poses remove old clear indications. A footprint-only preview is available with sensing off. Computation is requested only by an explicit header on the existing sensor poll and is absent from normal control responses. No threshold, retry, authorization, prompt, target or map/scorer input was loosened.

**User-visible sensor and expiry diagnostics implemented (2026-09-16):** `cockpit-diagnostics-v1` tests whether exposing existing measured timing alongside model activity helps distinguish controller starvation, refused renewal, stale sensing, objective closure and final budget termination. The compact cockpit now shows the retained buffer stop, owner, last renewal outcome, command-sensor age, reported motion state and exploration state without requiring raw JSON. Expandable details expose clock-labelled live sensor receipts, per-ray proximity, buffer/objective timestamps and renewal history, last-tick-to-stop delay and stop-relative-to-expiry. A copy icon exports the diagnostic evidence without spectator geometry or connection configuration. Live sensing reuses the existing SpatialSensing poll; no extra home-map or worker polling was introduced. Historical input and policy activities expose their recorded route failures by segment/frontier, localization sampling and map-age meanings. The new Failures filter uses recorded controller evidence, not model narration or ordinary operator Stop. [Troubleshooting field guide](HOME_MAPPING.md#cockpit-troubleshooting).

The user's preserved real-Luna run `3f49002d-503b-484b-8be5-3de8633750e7`, session `39eaedde-dfd4-4ee5-910b-7bb94ec753d1`, corroborates the reported poor motion. Six completed exploration-operation records sum to **0.355058 m of objective-accounted travel**, not a full independent physics trajectory measure; every operation reports exhausted bounded retries with BUFFER_EXPIRED. The last task retained frontier `2:1` across three segments. Each last renewal was accepted with a **0.500 s** buffer window; last-tick-to-stop intervals were **0.594, 0.640 and 0.750 s**, with accepted-command depth ages **0.500, 0.828 and 0.437 s**. The final segment stopped **0.250 s after its expiry timestamp**. Its recorded recent maximum active-tick gap is only **0.266 s**; that statistic does not include every final stall interval. This establishes a missed local refresh/expiry pattern, not a rejected renewal or proven sensor outage, and does not identify which operation occupied the worker. The final task outcome was the Luna token threshold, distinct from the preceding buffer failures. No claim is made that motion continued during the deadline overrun. **Compact cockpit implemented (2026-09-16):** `compact-cockpit-v1` tests the hypothesis that thumbnail-first sensing and progressive disclosure reduce visual crowding while retaining model input/output evidence and safety controls. Instructions can collapse without losing the goal draft; run settings can collapse during a session as well as before one. A read-only active-policy disclosure uses reported controller, backend, mission/objective, local motion and skill state, including raw diagnostic state on demand. It distinguishes active local-only navigation, a previous run and disconnected last-received data; a loaded model alone is not called an active policy.

All retained activity categories remain available with compact title/time/direction/result previews. Structured model replies preview their reported action/reason, not private reasoning. Each entry opens independently to expose camera images, sensor readings, arguments and payloads. Opening an entry pauses Follow latest; filters and full-feed JSON copying remain available. The existing bounded trace retention is unchanged, and the UI does not reconstruct discarded events. No backend, motion, inference, observation contract, saved-map or safety-authority behavior changed. A household navigation comparison was therefore not run; navigation performance and real-model competence are not measured by this UI change.

**Expiry diagnostics and observation provenance implemented (2026-09-16):** `expiry-provenance-v1` tests whether explicit authorization history separates missing/refused renewal, controller delay, stale sensing and real path rejection without extending any motion authority. Persistent exploration objectives and short motion buffers now retain separate IDs/scopes/issuers/renewal owners, clock-labelled issue/renewal/expiry timestamps, bounded acceptance/rejection history, linked task IDs, last accepted-command sensor age and stop-layer provenance. No lease, sensor age, speed, clearance, retry or mission deadline was relaxed. [Field definitions and limitations](HOME_MAPPING.md#authorization-and-observation-provenance).

The combined affected gate passes **113 tests** (265.39 s), including all six real enhanced-physics delayed-review cases: continue, Stop, objective expiry, stale timestamp, actual sensor-update loss and blocked path. The same operation moves about 0.43..0.46 m while a scripted response is pending; continued review renews the same task, all invalidated cases reject late motion, and sampled contacts remain zero. A test-only checkpoint aligns fault injection with actual motion; these are not natural-cadence five-minute Luna trials. One retained first trace test expected a last-command sensor for an unissued rejected route; its assertion now correctly requires null. The repeat **passed in 40.156 task seconds**, travelled **2.032 m**, ended about 0.031 m from the requested world destination, and recorded **zero sampled contacts**. This is one scripted-destination diagnostic, not a replacement full household series, real-Luna validation or reliable causal estimate. Sequential runs, prior batch versus isolated execution and other unmeasured workstation activity remain confounds.

Post-run checks confirm only port 8000 remains, with the user's run `b612cd00-47ef-4368-a331-6af28207acf9` still inactive at 26.9 simulated seconds; it was not reset or moved. No additional paid/local-model trial, architecture change or repeated successful-run selection occurred. **Unused simulator cleanup and lifecycle verified (2026-09-16):** `server-lifecycle-v1` addresses accumulated preview servers, not robot motion policy. The last browser disconnect already stopped motion but left the backend, camera renderer and map-processing helpers alive indefinitely. Eight old workspace-owned previews on ports 8019..8026 were verified stopped/inactive with the last message Operator disconnected; their public state, traces where present, head images and unsaved map snapshots were archived, then their exact process trees were terminated after rechecking PID creation time, run/session identity and unchanged simulated time. All eight listeners and descendant processes are gone. Port 8000 / PID 9020 / run `b612cd00-47ef-4368-a331-6af28207acf9` remained untouched at 26.9 simulated seconds, stopped and inactive. [Cleanup audit](../.runtime/unused-previews-20260916-090325/cleanup.json), [guarded script](../scripts/stop-unused-previews.ps1).

The normal launcher now uses an activity-aware Uvicorn entry point. After five unused minutes it preserves the current state/trace, unsaved map document and head image in a separate recovery directory, then requests graceful server shutdown through the existing lifespan cleanup. Connected HTTP/WebSocket work, recent HTTP activity, controllers, commands, scene transitions, power transitions and unfinished recordings prevent automatic exit. Requests/work arriving during preservation cancel the pending shutdown; archive failure postpones it. The process owns no robot motion and changes no sensor, lease or collision checks. A finished recording does not keep an unused server alive. Explicit timeout zero opts out; direct raw Uvicorn launches and already-running old processes do not gain this behavior automatically. `start.ps1` now reuses an existing same-workspace server on the requested port instead of suggesting another preview.

All **25 lifecycle tests pass**, including actual enhanced-renderer server startup, a connected WebSocket beyond the test timeout, recent-HTTP grace, snapshot preservation, and verified physics-thread, renderer-process and map-process cleanup after automatic exit. Unit tests cover active-work owners, invalid timeouts, opt-out, cancelled requests, preservation races/failure, completed recordings and explicit server Stop. An additional real `start.ps1 -SkipBuild -Port 8002 -IdleTimeoutSeconds 2` check completed startup, preserved a snapshot and exited cleanly without model calls or motion; no port-8002 listener or server process remained. Running the normal launcher against current port 8000 returned successfully without a duplicate. [Final lifecycle tests](../.runtime/server-lifecycle-v1/final.xml), [launcher recovery snapshot](../.runtime/idle-sessions/20260916T071009692604Z-d6593675-0d99-4aa7-b874-0064821bcea9/snapshot.json).

This is local lifecycle/software verification, not a navigation benchmark or a demonstrated CPU/GPU performance gain. A later system-wide GPU snapshot showed 4,153 MiB used / 30% utilization; unlike-load snapshots do not establish a causal speedup. No paid inference, saved-map mutation or current-user-scene restart occurred. The retained current port-8000 process still uses its old direct-Uvicorn lifecycle; its next normal launcher restart enables automatic shutdown. Full robot/browser suites and household benchmarks were not rerun for this server-entry-point-only change; earlier doorway timing failures remain unresolved.

**Compute-isolation plan proposed, not implemented (2026-09-16):** `map-compute-isolation-v1` evaluates whether the remaining synchronous home-map work materially delays control on the current doorway candidate. Existing RGB-D rendering and rolling-map processing are already asynchronous. Historical pre-cache timings are inclusive and log only calls over 10 ms; later corner deadline failures occurred without recorded buffer-expiry events. Those observations justify a bounded measurement/prototype phase, not a promise that offloading the mapper will solve the maneuver. [Explanation, architecture boundary, effort and go/no-go plan](HOME_MAPPING.md#proposed-compute-isolation).

The plan measures current exclusive costs and full refresh intervals first, tries smaller forced-wait/cache fixes, then conditionally shadows one pure clearance calculation off the physics worker. It preserves sole worker actuation, current-obstacle invalidation, original sensor/trajectory timestamps and all safety limits. Proposed qualification includes three repetitions of all three existing corner starts under matched nominal/loaded conditions, unchanged 45-second completion criteria, Stop/closed-door and concurrency fault tests, a refresh-headroom target, and the complete existing household baseline. The larger integration proceeds only if the current profile and transfer-inclusive shadow results justify it. This entry is planning only: no new runtime code, model call, simulation, measured improvement or live-server change. The incomplete doorway timing result below remains unresolved.

Hardware advisory for the prepared RTX 5080: the current machine still reports RTX 3080 10 GiB, Ryzen 7 3700X and 63.9 GiB RAM. A stopped-robot snapshot reported 4,769 MiB VRAM used / 33% GPU utilization, 50% system CPU utilization (maximum logical processor 82%), 20.9 GiB RAM free and nine simulator listeners; the local model was unloaded. This is read-only system context, not an active-run profile or proof of CPU/GPU saturation. CPU-side serial work and contention are stronger current hypotheses than VRAM exhaustion, but the plan requires matched active-workload measurements before recommending hardware or architecture changes. No RTX 5080 speedup, local inference gain or elimination of remote Luna latency has been measured. No process was stopped for this assessment.

**Doorway motion candidate: local defects repaired, timing qualification incomplete (2026-09-16):** `doorway-motion-v1` investigates the user's stopped five-room-flat run `b612cd00-47ef-4368-a331-6af28207acf9`, session `a99e0bc2-38f4-49f6-9fd4-701f4bae929b`. All 12 model observations included the observed map; six exploration operations ended with buffer-expiry retry exhaustion. The final operation travelled only 0.000841 m. A clear forward beam did not establish full-footprint clearance. State, observed map and trace were preserved read-only; the live episode was not reset, moved or restarted. [Original state](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/original-state.json), [observed map](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/original-map.json), [trace](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/original-trace.json).

Hypothesis 1: command-queue traffic starved navigation because the worker advanced physics only when its queue was empty. Two real-physics reproductions retained zero ticks throughout queued reads. The worker now services active built-in navigation between requests on a bounded 50 ms cadence through the existing executor; Stop and original leases remain authoritative. Both queue-pressure/Stop checks pass. The first post-fix fixture incorrectly demanded that a deliberately non-renewed buffer remain running after its lease; final assertions require measured progress followed by valid expiry/braking instead. [Original failure](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/queue-red.xml), [intermediate checks](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/queue-green.xml), [lease-fixture check](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/queue-verified.xml), [final queue checks](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/queue-final.xml). One slow operation can still delay control; this is not a hard-real-time guarantee or proof that queue starvation caused every live expiry.

Hypothesis 2: timing-only failures were incorrectly blacklisting geometric destinations. BUFFER_EXPIRED, BUFFER_EMPTY and MOTION_LEASE_EXPIRED now retain the frontier target for fresh replanning, while consuming the same original bounded retry allowance. Observed obstruction still rejects the frontier; stale sensors, Stop and task deadlines remain terminal. Eight classification/authority cases pass. [Checks](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/retry-classification.xml).

Hypothesis 3: fixed-distance shortcut sampling skipped occupied corner cells. A failing offset's straight shortcut crossed cells `[207,206]`, `[208,207]`, `[209,208]` between coarse samples, leaving the follower to choose zero linear speed until NO_PROGRESS. New grid-intersection checks cover every touched cell, including boundaries, for shortcut selection and rolling continuation; four-connected route generation and all clearance margins remain unchanged. Bidirectional regression reproduces the coarse-sampling false acceptance and rejects the segment. [Velocity/path diagnostic](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/corner-shortcut-events.json), [failed maneuver](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/corner-shortcut-diagnostic.xml), [14 shortcut/retry checks](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/shortcut-guards.xml).

The isolated doorway fixture reconstructs the corner and original wheel-odometry map frame at three declared offsets/headings. It provides one destination beyond the door, not a prescribed route or model input; all geometry is built from fresh simulated sensors. An earlier reconstruction used a newly rotated map frame and rejected the starting footprint (0.447 m observed clearance against radius 0.397 m plus 0.1 m grid margin); that setup was corrected, not its threshold. All three original-frame passage cases passed in one run after the shortcut repair, and separate mid-motion Stop/closed-door checks passed with no contacts. These are scripted-destination physics results, not autonomous doorway selection or a general success rate. [Admission diagnostic](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/corner-admission.xml), [frame correction results](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/corner-original-frame.xml), [three passages](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/corner-clearance-verified.xml), [Stop and closed door](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/corner-safety.xml).

Repeated timing qualification is **not green**. The complete worker/map/async gate retained 150 passes / two failures out of 152: one 45-second doorway timeout with BUFFER_EXPIRED and one short exploration with insufficient travel. Instrumentation then measured repeated full-grid clearance costs (420 calls, 8.58 s inclusive; other synchronous work also exceeded portions of the 0.5 s refresh budget). The existing sensor-invalidated clearance cache is now reused for shortcuts/continuations with explicit radius invalidation, and trajectory checks are vectorized against the same 32 sample positions. Eighteen equivalence/safety checks pass. The subsequent six-case motion gate had four passes / two doorway timeouts; no route-failure records occurred in those two timeouts, but that does not prove general timing resolution. The short exploration passed. No budgets or success criteria were extended. [Shared gate](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/shared-gate.xml), [timing events](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/corner-timing-events.json), [instrumented outcome](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/corner-timing.xml), [cache/equivalence gate](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/clearance-cache-verified.xml), [retained final motion failures](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/final-motion-gate.xml).

An eight-connected diagonal-route experiment passed geometry checks but worsened two corner cases to blocked/no-progress failures. It was withdrawn, not promoted or used to replace earlier failures. The final retained code passes 17 focused queue/clearance/retry checks. No reverse-maneuver search or new learned/local planning engine was added; those remain deferred pending repeatable execution. [Withdrawn experiment geometry](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/diagonal-route-guards.xml), [withdrawn motion results](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/diagonal-motion-gate.xml), [retained-fix gate](../.runtime/doorway-motion-v1/retained-fixes.xml).

**Async-control diagnosis verified; real-Luna milestone pending (2026-09-15):** `mission-review-callback-v1` qualifies the focused software repair to `mission-review-v1`. Hypothesis: persistent, bounded exploration authorization permits fresh local planning during serialized Luna review, but revocation from a physics callback must end the current executor tick immediately. Affected workflows are unified exploration/room search, objective expiry, buffered navigation cancellation, Home return and separate hidden bathroom evaluation. The requested repeated five-minute real-Luna milestone is still not qualified.

The deterministic reproduction found `NavigationRuntime.tick` indexing an empty deque after `sim.on_tick` revoked its motion buffer. Both cancellation and failure variants raised `IndexError`. The executor now returns if the callback changed the buffer revision or cleared it, preserving the terminal reason and rejecting old tickets without extending any lease. Both reproductions plus seven existing lease/clearance checks pass. [Failing reproduction](../.runtime/mission-review-v1/async/callback-red.xml), [nine repaired executor checks](../.runtime/mission-review-v1/async/callback-green.xml). No safety threshold, speed, collision margin, mission deadline, production review interval, local frontier policy or benchmark input was changed in this diagnosis.

The final combined gate records **117 passes / one failure out of 118**: all **45 async-review and 33 evaluator checks pass**, including five enhanced-physics delayed-response cases. Pending-review translation was 0.151..0.420 m, with one inference at a time and zero sampled contacts. Continue renewed the same task/operation; Stop froze physics; expiry, stale depth and blocked path ended authorization without late renewal. A moving inspection request was discarded and required fresh stopped evidence. The test-only review checkpoint is synchronized after measured translation so fault injection does not depend on whether the kitchen controller happens to be turning at 15 seconds; expiry is injected only after motion is observed. Natural production-cadence continuity is not established by those synchronized cases. [Final combined gate and metrics](../.runtime/mission-review-v1/async/final-mission-gate.xml).

The one retained combined-gate failure is the existing 25-second one-call local-exploration case: a recovery survey did not finish an operation before the budget. It passes unchanged on isolation alongside all nine executor checks; no time limit or assertion was relaxed. The complete 118-case gate was not rerun green, so short-budget scheduling remains a residual risk. [Unchanged short-budget and executor recheck](../.runtime/mission-review-v1/async/short-budget-and-executor.xml). The final combined run also passes the cabinet-to-Home redirect and other unified object/return interruption cases.

**Local roaming under Luna supervision candidate (2026-09-15):** `local-supervision-v1` tests the hypothesis that the worker should choose fresh reachable routes and continue across frontiers while Luna handles intent and visual identity at bounded checkpoints. The retained real-model run `d3ef198f-1e74-4793-8056-2e9c4562edd6` / session `bebcd336-9d55-41fd-bfd8-54a89c658647` used high reasoning, made 12 calls totaling about 88.16 s of reported inference latency and 81,879 input/output tokens, and ended limited after 32.6 simulated seconds. Five exploration selections were rejected as stale or unreachable. The model received paired camera/sensor observations and observed-map images; supplying a frontier ID forced single-frontier execution and frequent stopped reviews. This is existing real-model failure evidence, not a new paid evaluation. [Original run](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/original-run.json).

Unified `explore` now delegates to the existing local multi-frontier worker and omits frontier IDs from the model tool schema. Older replies containing that optional field remain parseable, but the worker selects fresh destinations instead. Review occurs after completion, unresolved blockage, or at most 15 s / 2 m, compared with 8 s / 1 m; it still stops during inference. This is reduced waypoint supervision, not asynchronous inference while driving or unrestricted roaming. A single guarded full-circle survey and local retry may recover missing observed clearance; it is reactive, not mandatory before every exploration. Stop, task authority, motion leases, original mission deadlines and all worker clearance thresholds remain enforced. Unknown space is not treated as free.

Tests exposed two local causes beyond model reasoning. Frontier selection chose already-arrived points near the base and suppressed an entire connected region after exhausting only one target; it now skips the arrival band and exhausted individual targets and ranks reachable representatives for roughly one-metre forward progress. Empty frontier sets complete through the existing worker path after validating the current footprint. Separately, footprint calculation used world-axis AABB corners, so a fixed posture grew from radius 0.39764 to 0.40213 m after only 0.08 rad of yaw, crossing a 0.5 m map-clearance threshold. It now uses robot-frame collision shapes/mesh vertices with margins, independent of global heading. An initial duplicate primitive-margin expansion caused a room-return regression; removing that duplicate padding while retaining mesh padding resolved it. No map cell, scorer, benchmark fixture or clearance threshold was relaxed. [Footprint reproduction](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/footprint-reproduced.xml), [margin/return checks](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/footprint-margin-fixed.xml).

The first delegated-motion tests failed 2/2 without meaningful travel; frontier repair passed both, but kitchen testing then exposed self-occluded rear sensing and heading-dependent clearance. The isolated diagnostic preserves unknown/occupied cells, radius, route failures and actual pose. Mandatory scanning was rejected after a short-budget regression; reactive scanning restored that test. All four park/kitchen motion and mid-motion Stop cases later passed. A separate enhanced-camera kitchen test crossed three local exploration intervals, travelled 3.339 m, made five scripted supervisor calls and had zero sampled contacts or rejected capabilities in 78.71 s. It does not establish bathroom arrival or real-Luna supervision quality. [Initial failures](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/first.xml), [kitchen failures](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/scenes.xml), [local diagnostic](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/kitchen-diagnostic.json), [ranked diagnostic](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/kitchen-ranked-diagnostic.json), [four roaming checks](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/roaming-footprint.xml), [sustained roaming](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/sustained.xml).

The broad four-suite run retained 384 passes / 19 failures out of 403. Fifteen are legacy test-contract errors (missing mock fields, removed API arguments or mock callback signature), one is the previously recorded cabinet/Home parking-margin failure, and three motion cases were isolated. The crossing failure also occurs with the previous footprint; the circle case passes on current-code isolation. The reproducible room-return regression was fixed by correct primitive margin accounting; all seven footprint/room cases then passed. Earlier 92-case results retained 88 passes / four failures; two related budget/no-frontier issues were repaired, and the delayed-capture fixture was made explicitly active to avoid testing active cadence while idle. Failures are preserved; no whole-suite-green claim. [Broad results](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/broad.xml), [four-suite results](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/full-final.xml), [previous-footprint diagnostic](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/previous-footprint-motion.xml), [current isolation](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/current-footprint-motion.xml), [active capture](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/capture-active.xml), [empty-map Stop](../.runtime/local-supervision-v1/empty-map-stop.xml).
