## Nav2 Primary Integration

Follow-up evaluation: the task pilot and local comparison above supersede any implication that the initial two-goal integration establishes a performance improvement. The hypothesis that Nav2's tracker reduces short-goal arrival time remains unproven. Local probes kept 0.7 m / 1.2 m targets, two repeats per backend, reversed order on repeat two, enhanced rendering and a common 0.04 m position criterion. They were operator-selected scripted goals with 60-second local deadlines, not the 180-second real-model task pilot. Different speed caps, additional Nav2 laser and final-heading validation are disclosed; no software or safety threshold was adjusted to produce a win.

The real-model attempt ran from 21:17:31.989 to 21:18:57.059 +02:00; Built-in parking's retained recording lasted 35.656 s before access failure and has zero model tokens. The local attempt ran from 21:21:54 to 21:26:14 +02:00 and is marked `source_changed_during_run=true`. At inspection, differences included the app, worker, continuous supervisor, contracts and home-mission source; these were not edited for this comparison. No conclusion attributes a failure to a particular concurrent change. After preserving all eight results, the runner's post-scan readiness and final-drift handling were corrected and checked without another motion trial. Readiness passed; source freeze failed again. Further evaluation is blocked, not complete qualification. The original port-8013 run `9fa6785a-9335-4101-9001-7144285d3703` remains inactive, not busy and stopped; all task-owned pilot containers were removed.

Design: `nav2-supervised-v1-integration`, architecture `nav2-supervised` 0.1.0. Hypothesis: a proven Nav2 planner/controller can replace local destination tracking while semantic selection and motion authorization remain local. The original controller is explicitly selectable as backup; API default remains built-in for compatibility, and the UI prefers Nav2 when launched with `-Nav2`. No automatic switch occurs after Stop or failure. Affected scenarios: bounded local destinations in continuous supervision; parking was only an integration fixture. Apartment search, target recognition, task completion, dynamic obstacle navigation and real-model behavior are **not measured** for Nav2.

Nav2 uses odometry-frame rolling costmaps, regulated pure pursuit, velocity smoothing and collision monitoring. Camera/depth and synthetic laser contain no object identities or privileged geometry; the additional laser means a future built-in comparison must match or disclose sensor differences. Existing candidate generation, scans, turn and marked-floor approach remain. Composed routes, furniture circuits and rolling continuation require explicit built-in selection. No loop closure/global localization is enabled.

Timing changes: independent odometry drives the ROS clock; camera transforms remain paired to capture time; four callback groups separate sensing, odometry, commands and lifecycle/actions. Worker ROS ticks use a 20 Hz cadence instead of advancing physics before every queued request. Stop bypasses pacing and is rechecked at queue wakeup. Request-side terminal state is published even if a watchdog raises. NumPy depth serialization preserves finite values/None and removes a measured Python per-pixel conversion cost (about 33 ms per 160x120 frame in one development profile); end-to-end speed gain is **not measured**. Speed, freshness, lease, contact/tilt/floor and arrival limits were not relaxed.

Final real-container evidence: [report and source manifest](../.runtime/nav2-current-frozen-20260914/report.json), 2026-09-14 19:05:16.610-19:05:37.617 UTC, run `07870ee9-4b4d-42a7-895a-24569a04a222`, epoch 2, enhanced renderer, standalone `park`. All measured backend/ROS/URDF/project/launcher/harness files stayed unchanged during this run. Operator-selected 0.7 m and 0.35 m relative goals arrived after 0.661 m and 0.311 m actual travel; command sequences 98 and 185 are bridge-global counters, not independent per-goal counts. There were 33 and 27 samples with no sampled contacts. Third goal cancelled after movement; simulation stayed at 8.85 s during the Stop checks, old velocities rejected HTTP 409 both stopped and resumed. Local arrival does not establish the parking objective or mission success. This is scripted/operator-selected integration evidence, not real-model, matched performance or reliability evidence.

Runtime: Docker Linux engine 29.6.2; image `milo-nav2:jazzy`, ID `sha256:c1302aa82bbaa8cbdbef1e7539b39de1c3393728f0ed2d1ae66000c3bb6fed3e`; installed navigation2 `1.3.13-1noble.20260905.072714`, RPP `1.3.13-1noble.20260903.031928`, slam_toolbox `2.8.5-1noble.20260905.070219` (installed, not launched). Existing image was not rebuilt; current ROS code is read-only bind mounted. A Dockerfile hash does not prove an existing image was built from that recipe. Nav2 variant source hashes include ROS code/YAML/Dockerfile and launcher; runtime image identity is recorded separately here.

Development failures are retained, not excluded from an aggregate score: initial readiness/middleware, TF/clock, camera cadence and watchdog attempts failed. [Wall-clock attempt](../.runtime/nav2-wall-clock-smoke-20260914/report.json) stopped after 11 commands with no useful travel. [Depth conversion attempt](../.runtime/nav2-vector-depth-smoke-20260914/report.json) exhausted 60 s after 540 commands, 0.026 m travel. [Bench diagnostic](../.runtime/nav2-runtime-detail-smoke-20260914/report.json) had whole-body clearance stops, 0.015 m travel and deadline exhaustion. [Parking paired-clock attempt](../.runtime/nav2-park-paired-smoke-20260914/report.json) travelled 0.053 m but exhausted 60 s. [Independent odometry alone](../.runtime/nav2-independent-odom-smoke-20260914/report.json) travelled 0.241 m then hit the watchdog. [Paced-worker development run](../.runtime/nav2-paced-smoke-20260914/report.json) first reached two goals. [Earlier frozen run](../.runtime/nav2-frozen-integration-20260914/report.json) also passed arrivals/Stop, before the subsequent cancellation-acknowledgement repair. These changing-code runs are not pooled or called benchmark trials.

An external change to the API source removed required routes after passing checks. The [next frozen attempt](../.runtime/nav2-frozen-final-20260914/report.json) failed on `/api/ros/status` HTTP 404 before any goal. Only Nav2 routes and required selector metadata were reapplied to the current file; unrelated current app changes were preserved. The final run above used that repaired source, not the earlier API snapshot. An intermediate metadata test caught a missing required inert preview goal; corrected before final API verification.

The only subsequent production change adds the already-supported `none` reasoning value to architecture-preview query validation. The [final API options/interruption gate](../.runtime/nav2-api-options-final-20260914.xml) passed four cases in 12.26 s. No motion implementation changed; the final preview was restarted on a fresh stopped parking episode without repeating motion. Its API hash differs from the last internally frozen motion report. Live selector inspection confirmed Built-in and Nav2 values, restored Nav2, and observed an inactive/stopped controller; no inference started.

Software evidence: [38 scoped tests](../.runtime/nav2-scoped-verified-20260914.xml) passed in 83.15 s; [two scripted supervisor dispatch cases](../.runtime/nav2-supervisor-dispatch-20260914.xml) passed in 23.64 s, with blocked outcomes and no mission claim; [final four API contract cases](../.runtime/nav2-api-contract-final-20260914.xml) passed in 7.82 s, covering endpoints and Stop/takeover/reset/disconnect. [Depth/spatial gate](../.runtime/nav2-depth-conversion-20260914.xml) passed 16 cases. [Paced-worker gate](../.runtime/nav2-paced-worker-verified-20260914.xml) passed seven cases, including odometry bursts not forcing one physics tick per call. Launcher parse/WhatIf and mounted YAML parse/frame checks pass. Production frontend build passes with its bundle-size warning; primary/backup selector test passes with scripted readiness.

Full regression limitations: [backend JUnit](../.runtime/nav2-final-backend-20260914.xml) records 686 passed / 10 failed, 2,487.422 s, tiny baseline except explicitly enhanced fixtures. Two ROS Stop/disconnect acknowledgement races were fixed and revalidated in all four interruption variants. Eight non-Nav2 failures were left unchanged: adaptive no-progress; crossing-yield Stop; pedestrian floor parking; kitchen moving-review continuity; one incomplete redirect mock; two obsolete generic-route schema cases; and one continuous-depth mock missing `skill_components`. The [broader focused rerun](../.runtime/nav2-focused-final-20260914.xml) passed 76 and reproduced pedestrian parking failure. No before/after full-suite equivalence or full-green claim is made.

Full Playwright gate: 46 passed, one skipped, three archive failures in 7.0 minutes; all three failed again in isolation (focus refresh and two selected-trial-details checks). No archive production code was changed for this migration. Navigation, camera/pixel, responsive layout, Stop and primary/backup tests passed. Scripted inference only; no Foundry requests or real-model validation. Windows disconnect callback and dependency deprecation warnings are retained. The preview at http://127.0.0.1:8013/ is intentionally running and stopped after integration; secondary task-owned diagnostics were removed, older user servers preserved.

## Motion Skill Composer

User approved the recommended opt-in four-component prototype. Hypothesis: locally compiling several meaningful motion components under one authority avoids intermediate endpoint braking and reduces model decisions while retaining useful viewing directions. Existing FastAPI/PyBullet/OpenAI Responses architecture is preserved. No hosted agent or cloud resources were created.

The typed `MotionSkillPlan` accepts 1-3 components: cruise length, curve length/angle, approach to a CURRENT observed candidate, and optional terminal inspection yaw/pitch/dwell. Approach must be the last moving component; inspection must be last. Cruise/curve geometry is integrated from a smooth quintic heading profile with zero endpoint curvature; approach uses a quintic Hermite curve with matched heading and zero endpoint second derivative. SciPy supplies interpolation/integration. The compiler rejects nonfinite data, unknown candidates, sharp approaches, excessive curvature and total paths above 1.8 m. Curves change heading over a length; they are not fixed-radius arcs. No new dependency was needed.

The worker validates the paired episode, Stop revision, task revision, unchanged pose, 15 s selection age, fresh depth, footprint and the full path before starting one continuous route. It rechecks authority after refresh. All existing online proximity/braking, observed-floor, no-progress and timeout checks remain. Plans share one motion buffer across internal joins and use a curvature-derived speed ceiling below the existing 0.5 m/s cap. They end within the existing bounded motion lease (no later than 20 s from the observation); generic replanning is disabled inside the plan to avoid silently changing a component's geometry. Unexpected obstruction returns blocked for fresh review. Neither target constraints nor independent scorers are weakened.

Each moving component chooses forward/left/right head orientation at pitch 0.2. This is an observation orientation policy, not an object detector or a guarantee of search coverage. After arrival, a terminal inspection sets the head and holds it for 0.5-2 simulated seconds. At least one second enables subsequent visual confirmation; fresh feedback is still required. Its result explicitly says `mission_success_verified=false`. Marked-floor parking retains its existing approach/containment workflow. Disabled sessions do not advertise the new action. The cockpit toggle and evaluator `--skill-composer` flag record distinct architecture/model-configuration provenance; no generic AI-waypoint integration was restored.

This first increment deliberately stops at plan boundaries: no mid-plan LLM review, automatic target detection, compatible moving suffix replacement, cross-plan velocity blending or jerk-limited timing is implemented. Existing acceleration limits remain. It is not the complete event-driven architecture discussed in planning. New multi-skill travel cannot itself solve target recognition or semantic search. Live instruction/Stop still cancels pending control instead of preserving a suffix.

Recording reliability changes use unique same-directory temporary files and up to five `PermissionError` replacement attempts with 0.30 s total scheduled backoff; prior JSON remains intact until atomic replacement, temporary files are cleaned and permanent failures propagate. This addresses a verified failure mode, not an identified external locking process. The first full browser run passed 46, skipped one and failed two archive-loading workflows. Active two-second refresh cancelled unfinished catalogue loads; skipping automatic refresh while loading made both workflows pass in isolation. A new UI Stop assertion initially mixed immediate motion stop with recording finalization; it now separately verifies the stop latch, frozen physics and bounded finalization. All failed runs remain historical evidence, not model trials.

Current software evidence: [four real-depth scripted integration cases](../.runtime/motion-skills-controller-20260914.xml) passed in 59.16 s (normal execution, late model reply, Stop during motion, Stop during inspection); [22 recording/provenance checks](../.runtime/motion-skills-recording-20260914.xml) passed. The final [157-check backend regression gate](../.runtime/motion-skills-regressions-20260914.xml) passed in 291.31 s with two dependency deprecation warnings; it covers composer, existing braking/authority, sensor waits/subgoals, recording/provenance and agent tests, not the entire physics suite. The single cruise-to-curve physics fixture held join speed above 0.15 m/s, respected existing acceleration limits, arrived within 0.05 m and had no buffer stop/contact. Production build passed with the bundle warning; final full browser suite passed 48, skipped one in 5.6 minutes, with a nonfatal Windows disconnect callback warning. These remain real-physics/scripted-inference checks, not real-model success or broad smoothness qualification.

Completed frozen pilot: two Shared Apartment V1 object-search trials per setting, composer off then on, same source/build, exact task, Luna/high, enhanced, history/adaptive on, ordinary handoff off, two image slots, 8,192 context tokens and 180 s wall-time-only budget. Camera configured 320 x 240; actual feedback includes both 320 x 240 and 160 x 120 in both settings, with different counts. No retuning or retries. Full source `210974192970609271ef88adb6968ece18053647bcf0e375718677f320ca92a1`, implementation `40b0cf52fe75`, 84 files unchanged. [Manifests, reports, traces, raw logs and reproducible checks](../.runtime/performance/motion-skills-20260914-review/summary.md).

Results: baseline-00 skill deadline error at 68.953 s / 5.612 m; baseline-01 unverified completion at 100.563 s / 5.678 m. Candidate-00 skill deadline error at 72.437 s / 2.959 m; candidate-01 schema failure at 152.203 s / 8.916 m. Every trial has zero independently completed objectives. Recordings contain 637/887/663/1,568 samples, complete/unassisted/zero-drop/zero sampled contacts. Baseline reported 165,395 tokens across 13 responses; candidate 263,596 across 19, with differing early termination. No fixed-work efficiency or success gain is established.

Candidate-00 selected two approach/inspect compositions: first 1.500 m plan arrived with 0.039 m remaining and completed one-second inspection, explicitly not mission success. Second 1.583 m plan followed a 14.328 s model reply and blocked after 5.250 s of execution with 0.676 m remaining. Worker deadline is at most capture+20 s, so the generic "60-second deadline" message does not describe the effective lease. Candidate-01 selected no composition and failed on response 77 (`reason` length 559 > 500). Neither candidate selected cruise or curve. Preserve all failures; recommend bounded output-contract recovery and conservative lease-aware admission/sizing, not extending freshness or session budgets. These follow-up changes are **not implemented**.

Stationary inference seconds were 15.344/34.687/24.843/18.235; recorded stationary intervals include intentional arrival/preparation holds. Zero buffer-stop counters do not mean uninterrupted motion. Numerical moving-speed, acceleration/jerk and minimum-clearance comparisons remain unmeasured. Ordered four-trial shared-workstation evidence cannot establish causality, reliability, model identity or generalization. Cross-plan blending, target-triggered review and target/range/visibility qualification remain unmet. Preview http://127.0.0.1:8008/ is idle; cancelled port-8007 sweep remains stopped.

## Live Common Capability Sweep

User requested a real-time observable test covering common target capabilities. Hypothesis: a fixed, visible sequence of existing independently scored scenarios can expose which current behaviors complete and where model/controller failures remain. This is a live evaluation workflow, not a new navigation design or repair of the preceding schema/subgoal failures.

Status: **cancelled after four attempted stages**, 2026-09-14 13:22:17 to 13:29:36 +02:00. The readiness prompt returned that the user was unavailable and requested autonomous execution, so the prepared sequence was started once. Kitchen control later reported `Operator disconnected`; the harness cancelled all remaining stages without retrying. The [local-only harness](../.runtime/live-common-capabilities-20260914/serve.py) wraps the existing FastAPI app without production edits and records its own hash plus the full existing source/build snapshot in [suite status](../.runtime/live-common-capabilities-20260914/suite.json). It sets the existing NavigationEvaluationBudget to 180 wall seconds per trial with no request ceiling. No cloud resources, hosted agent or new frontend build were created.

Fixed sequence: standalone parking, standalone table/clockwise circuit, Shared Apartment V1 yellow-cube search/inspection, Shared Apartment V1 kitchen search/entry, standalone pedestrian crossing, Shared Apartment V1 survey/return/recharge. Each uses its exact preset goal and fresh episode; these are separate scored tasks, not a combined mission retaining memory across resets. Luna/high, observed-continuous 0.3.0, enhanced rendering, adaptive navigation, history, two image slots, 8,192 context tokens, 0.25 s feedback, folded arms and ordinary moving handoff disabled. The six control periods are capped at 18 minutes total, excluding scene setup/finalization.

Stop, takeover, manual commands, a new instruction or episode changes cancel remaining stages. Existing worker/controller disconnect and motion-invalidation safeguards remain authoritative. Before starting and between stages, source/hash checks reject changed files; individual browser recordings retain their normal provenance and independent scoring. The harness never selects wheel actions or injects hidden scorer/scene data into model inputs. Its sequence controller only loads standard challenges, starts ordinary agent control, waits for termination and links the resulting recordings. An ordinary failed trial remains in the results and does not trigger a retry. Operator interruption ends the sequence.

Local no-model checks passed for six-stage sequencing, stopping after the first stage, disconnected-operator rejection, source-drift rejection and time-only budget. The harness has no reported editor diagnostics. The first browser navigation arrived before server startup and was retried after Uvicorn confirmed readiness; no model session was involved. Before Start, the shared browser at `http://127.0.0.1:8007/` showed a visible 3D canvas and loaded authoritative head-camera image. The API exposes `observed-continuous@0.3.0+8c0f464ca88e`. Existing server processes are preserved.

After Start, internal Test cockpit navigation restored the shared tab from its archive view without cancelling control. Live verification showed parking stage 1/6, model active/thinking, simulated 8.00 s, camera and exchange trace present at `http://127.0.0.1:8007/`. No operator steering or goal changes were made.

## Sensor Dwell Repeat

User requested another model test and remaining capability gaps. Hypothesis: repeating the latest fixed recharge task without changes can reveal whether explicit subgoal transitions and the new dwell action are selected, or expose another failure before that stage. Exactly one new real-model trial was run; no source/prompt/scorer/build changes, operator assistance or retry. The original 180 s wall-time budget and 90% charge objective remain unchanged.

[Manifest](../.runtime/performance/sensor-dwell-repeat-20260914-recharge/experiment.json): experiment `d85c674f-d2bc-4433-b813-f0b53270b2b6`, design `sensor-dwell-v1`, variant `observed-continuous@0.3.0+8c0f464ca88e/8b0bbab361bb`, full source `e78cd5587192ce57787728be480abf952328af34edd05a2aca95365370fe9121`, completed 2026-09-14 13:09:20 +02:00. Source/task/model/configuration/runtime match the first 0.3.0 probe exactly; no source drift. Luna/high, enhanced rendering, adaptive/history enabled, ordinary handoff disabled, two images, 8,192 context tokens and 0.25 s feedback.

Independent result: **failed, survey only completed**. Elapsed control 118.734 s, travel 3.131 m, six completed model responses but only five accepted decisions, 85,466 input / 5,144 output tokens. Last paired battery 25.034%, low, not charging; simulated 34.70 s. Complete/unassisted recording, 1,115 samples, zero drops/contact episodes/manual placements/false terminal completion claims. Response event 32 contains a 552-character `reason`; GuideContinuous permits 500, so validation ended the session before its requested `approach_target` action. The tool supplies its generated schema with `strict: false`; local validation enforces the bound without a correction path. The failure is safely rejected model output plus missing controller correction, not a 180 s timeout or demonstrated motion-safety failure.

The model acknowledged the sensor-change-2 battery drop of about 60.101 points over one simulated second but retained `floor-0` and insisted on verifying survey containment before returning. No `advance_subgoal` or `wait_until` was selected. Across the two same-build trials, neither full recharge passed and neither new action was selected; the first did return while retaining an obsolete survey target. This supports unresolved task-stage/target consistency and tool-selection gaps, not a general reliability estimate or evidence that the wait implementation itself failed. Recorded stationary inference was 64.234 s (about 54% of this run), median/p95 inference 8.852/19.766 s; the budget was not the terminal cause.

The [capability review](../.runtime/performance/sensor-dwell-repeat-20260914-recharge/review.md) separates current evidence from earlier 0.1.0 object-framing/inspection and kitchen-path recovery failures. Those tasks were not retested on 0.3.0. Fixed-layout parking/table success does not establish arbitrary task control; current generic-route integration is unavailable, and following/figure-eight qualification, held-out layouts and manipulation remain unmeasured. Next recommendations are bounded non-motion correction for invalid decisions, consistent explicit subgoal/target binding, then actual dwell selection and separate object/recovery evaluations. No fixes are included in this task.

RTX 3080 whole-device snapshots: 3,253 MiB/12% before and 3,217 MiB/1% after, not controlled workload. Underlying model identity and cloud seed remain unverified. Existing browser episodes were not reset or steered. No software suite/build was rerun for this evaluation-only task; previous scoped checks and unresolved full-suite failures retain their separate labels. Raw failed output, trace, final camera, metrics and recording remain linked in the review. Shared archive now shows both same-design failures with the repeat selected; its complete 1,115-point recorded-scene trajectory and final PNG endpoints were verified. Artifact links, key claims and frozen production hashes pass validation.

## Sensor Dwell Candidate

Design `sensor-dwell-v1`, authorized 2026-09-14 after the subgoal-progress probe returned to the charger but timed out during inspection/inference. Hypothesis: a model-selected sensor-conditioned stationary dwell can complete the requested wait without invoking inference for every half-second of physics. Target architecture is observed-continuous 0.3.0. The model receives no hidden task/scorer state, and no recharge-specific success or automatic homing routine is added. Kitchen path recovery and object approach/framing remain unchanged.

`wait_until` accepts a typed condition over `battery.charge_pct`, `battery.charging`, `battery.low` or `proximity.front_m`, a model-selected threshold/comparison, an optional guard of the same form and a 0.5-60 s cap. Unknown sensors, invalid thresholds, nonfinite numbers, invalid boolean comparisons and missing/occluded readings are rejected. The model chooses the requested target; 90% is unchanged in the recharge task and is not hard-coded into this action. A matching condition returns `condition_met` with measured value and frame ID, never mission success. A separate fresh decision and the independent scorer retain completion authority.

Execution uses existing worker-validated stationary wait commands of at most 0.5 simulated seconds. Before each slice, the worker refreshes observed depth under its original episode/Stop/controller guard, and the supervisor checks fresh paired observations, contact, unchanged pose, inactive motion controllers, parking margin, any selected floor containment/surface and the optional sensor guard. It stops on missing/stale data, changed pose, lost guard or safety rejection. Wall and simulated time limits are checked between bounded operations; sensing/worker latency can add termination overhead. The original session timeout is never extended. Depth refresh after inference/dwell is required; old-map age limits are not relaxed.

Initial [condition/guard tests](../.runtime/sensor-dwell-20260914-focused.xml) exposed two stale-map failures, confirming that ordinary waits do not refresh stationary depth by themselves. Guarded depth refresh corrected that path; [11 scripted-sensor checks](../.runtime/sensor-dwell-20260914-focused-refresh.xml) then passed. The later real-charging fixture correctly rejected zero-clearance unseen floor under its starting footprint; a full scan alone still left that cell unknown. The final fixture explicitly folds arms and executes a short scripted out-and-back path to observe that floor before rotating and waiting. [Three real-depth/charging/refresh-Stop checks](../.runtime/sensor-dwell-20260914-depth-final.xml) passed in 68.00 s. The fixture seeds battery state for mechanics validation and is not autonomous recharge evidence. Earlier failures are retained; no clearance or freshness threshold was weakened.

Final [36-check scoped regression gate](../.runtime/sensor-dwell-20260914-regressions.xml) passed in 222.47 s, including typed conditions, missing/stale/occluded readings, guard failure, selected-floor/surface rejection, contact/pose changes, Stop during observation/refresh, wall/session deadlines, actual refreshed-depth charging, controller integration, prior stationary wait/visual dwell, subgoal transitions, history freshness and version metadata. Two dependency deprecation warnings remain. Edited production/test files have no reported editor errors. This is scripted/mechanics evidence, not real-model task success. The known earlier pedestrian/full-suite failures were not repaired or claimed green; no frontend code changed or new build was required.

Completed measured probe: one fresh Shared Apartment V1 recharge attempt, Luna/high, enhanced rendering, adaptive/history enabled, ordinary moving handoff disabled, two images, 8,192 context tokens, 0.25 s feedback setting and the same 180 s wall-time-only budget. [Manifest](../.runtime/performance/sensor-dwell-20260914-recharge/experiment.json): experiment `b3791c03-bdb9-4921-bce2-cfe30bd3fdbe`, variant `observed-continuous@0.3.0+8c0f464ca88e/8b0bbab361bb`, full source `e78cd5587192ce57787728be480abf952328af34edd05a2aca95365370fe9121`, finished 2026-09-14 12:57:49 +02:00. No source drift, tuning or retries. Task hash, model configuration and runtime match subgoal-progress-v1; exactly continuous_supervisor.py, worker.py and experiment_variants.py differ in the covered production source.

Independent survey and return passed, but 90% charge did not: elapsed 180.360 s, travel 6.237 m, nine reported turns / eight completed responses, 134,882 input / 5,525 output tokens. Complete/unassisted/zero-drop/zero-contact recording; no false terminal completion claim. The model selected neither `wait_until` nor `advance_subgoal`. Its written memory switched to returning at turn 5, but retained `floor-0` still referred to the survey zone. At turn 8 the robot was charging at 21.692% with 0.75 m observed clearance; `inspect_arrival` produced `not_contained` at trace event 44 against that old target. The next feedback was unchanged at simulated 47.75 s before inference timed out. The new dwell action's real-model effect remains **not measured**; this trial does not show improvement.

Preflight found ports 8000/8002/8003/8004/8005 idle at zero simulated time; their episodes were preserved. RTX 3080 snapshot: 3,185 MiB used, 6,869 MiB free, 6% utilization; manifest before/after 3,165 MiB/6% and 3,168 MiB/16%. Sensing recorded two expired results, zero processing timeouts and a 19.391 s worker-lifetime processing-age maximum including preparation/rejected frames. These are not proof of stale motion authorization or controlled workload. One trial per version is descriptive evidence, not reliability or a causal estimate. Other-challenge performance remains **not measured**.

The [complete review](../.runtime/performance/sensor-dwell-20260914-review/summary.md) preserves failure details and recommends a separately versioned contract keeping subgoal transitions and retained targets consistent, without automatically clearing targets to evade safety rejection. No further implementation or model attempts followed this result. Archive PNG/trajectory endpoints and Not passed status verified: 1,612 samples, 807 displayed route points, recorded initial scene. Shared archive filtered to sensor-dwell-v1. Experimental preview `http://127.0.0.1:8006/`, idle episode `851d92c2-5189-4526-b565-4412814da6b0`, exposes the measured 0.3.0 revision. Version 0.2.0 remains available on port 8005; neither is claimed to be a generally qualified controller.

## Subgoal Progress Candidate

Design `subgoal-progress-v1`, authorized 2026-09-14 after the five-task baseline. Hypothesis: measured sensor transitions and explicit subgoal state let the model reconcile completed intermediate actions with stale memory, while a bounded verification loop prevents spending the whole trial on unchanged containment checks. The first discriminating task is Shared Apartment V1 recharge; object inspection and room/parking verification share the affected interface. Kitchen path-rejection recovery is deliberately unchanged.

`TaskProgress` consumes only AgentObservation. It retains up to four sensor-change records for battery changes of at least five percentage points or low/charging boolean transitions, with paired before/after frame IDs and simulated elapsed time. This is general measured evidence, not a hard-coded survey trigger or task-success flag. New episodes clear all records. Existing measured navigation routes and sightings remain intact. Minor-version bump `observed-continuous@0.2.0` identifies the added model tool capability; other controller families keep their semantic versions, though their shared source fingerprints also change.

The model can call `advance_subgoal` with the next user-requested objective and exactly one retained sensor-event ID or current paired frame reference. The transition is explicitly a **model assessment, not independently verified success**. It clears only the prior floor target, destination surface and inspection flags; no wheel motion, scene change or completion is authorized. A subsequent fresh decision must choose a currently validated route. Reused evidence, missing/stale frame references, an unchanged subgoal label and more than eight transitions are rejected. Historical-image decisions and moving decisions remain subject to the original fresh/stopped-feedback checks, and Stop/reset/disconnect retain authority over delayed replies.

Repeated nonterminal verification at an unchanged pose/view stops after three attempts. Merely receiving a new frame or advancing the clock does not reset the count. Actual translation of at least 0.15 m, base-heading change of 0.25 rad or head-angle change of 0.2 rad allows a new inspection; a supported planning-state transition also clears its old inspection state. The controller reports **unachievable**, not completed, on exhaustion. Existing containment, parking margin, surface match, visual dwell, speed, clearance and freshness checks are not weakened. Task transitions may still be wrong semantically and will be judged by the independent scorer, never by a model success claim.

Initial [five focused checks](../.runtime/subgoal-progress-20260914-focused.xml) passed in 24.35 s: sensor-delta retention, epoch clearing, stale/reused evidence rejection, changed-view handling, real-physics repeated-inspection termination, non-motion target release and Stop rejecting a late transition. Responses are scripted and containment rejection is deliberately injected; this is not model competence evidence. The first integration run reached its assertions but failed on an unimported test-only NumPy name; a standard-library assertion repair passed on rerun.

The [broader slice](../.runtime/subgoal-progress-20260914-regressions.xml) produced 24 passes and the known pedestrian-crossing no-progress failure. Its exact case/message also appears in the pre-candidate comprehensive report; no assertion was weakened or mechanics repaired under this scope. The [final scoped gate](../.runtime/subgoal-progress-20260914-final-regressions.xml), explicitly excluding that known failure, passed 24 checks in 90.60 s. It includes ordinary parking, stationary visual dwell, historical-image freshness, Stop, memory/episode isolation, evidence/transition bounds and version API checks. Two dependency deprecation warnings remain; edited files have no reported editor errors. The full suite is not claimed green.

An [offline replay](../.runtime/subgoal-progress-20260914-replay.json) feeds the baseline's captured AgentObservation values into TaskProgress, without inference or motion. It retains the measured -60.101 percentage-point battery transition from frames `0-251.png` to `0-256.png` and reaches inspection exhaustion at turn 11, the third unchanged rejection. This is a counterfactual tracker check, not an assertion that a real model would choose a successful return. The independent scorer's state is not supplied to TaskProgress.

Completed real-model probe: one independent `recharge` trial in Shared Apartment V1, fresh evaluator process, Luna/high, enhanced rendering, adaptive exploration and history enabled, ordinary handoff off, two images, 8,192 context tokens, 0.25 s feedback and 180 s time-only budget. [Manifest](../.runtime/performance/subgoal-progress-20260914-recharge/experiment.json), experiment `7b60030c-cbc4-4c13-be97-c9c4e6a6632a`, source `85e1c262f465370d564179177ade7df91c4c1283e323a4696c339afecdae4711`, variant `observed-continuous@0.2.0+ed2a7ca47bf8/8b0bbab361bb`, finished 2026-09-14 12:28:56 +02:00, no drift or retries. Baseline task hash, runtime and all model/evaluation settings match; exactly three covered backend files differ. Shared workstation/cloud variability and one trial per version prohibit a reliability or causal improvement claim.

The [independent report](../.runtime/performance/subgoal-progress-20260914-recharge/shared-apartment-v1-recharge/report.json) verifies survey and return, but not 90% charging. The model advanced its subgoal at turn 5 using sensor-change-2, then selected three fresh routes back to the original pad. At turn 9 battery was 20.342% and charging; existing room-style arrival inspection consumed 22.5 wall seconds. Last paired feedback showed 54.044%, charging, at simulated 65.55 s, before the next inference timed out. No explicit charging wait was dispatched. Elapsed 180.281 s, travel 6.526 m, 10 reported turns / nine completed responses, 152,410 input / 5,453 output tokens. Complete/unassisted/zero-drop/zero-contact recording; no false completion claim. The repeated-inspection cutoff was not exercised by this real trial because the model left the survey loop; its evidence remains scripted/offline.

The [full comparison](../.runtime/performance/subgoal-progress-20260914-review/summary.md) preserves both failures and remaining uncertainty. Next hypothesis is generic bounded stationary dwell for observed conditions, with safety checks retained, not a longer timeout or lower charge target. Object/kitchen real-model effects remain **not measured**. No further candidate edits or model attempts were made after this probe. Candidate sensing recorded one expired result and zero processing timeouts; its 11.5 s worker-lifetime processing-age maximum includes preparation/rejected results. Whole-device GPU snapshots were 3,136 MiB / 16% before and 2,918 MiB / 5% after.

The archive correctly shows Not passed, the new variant and its recorded scene/1,946-sample trajectory/final PNG. Fresh preview `http://127.0.0.1:8005/`, PID 38352, idle episode `8939bb22-8ff1-4e1c-8fa5-9d100825b8b4`, exposes the measured version without starting inference. Original ports 8000/8002/8003/8004 and episodes remain idle/unchanged. No frontend code changed; its prior build is reused, while old backend processes retain their previously imported controller until restarted.

## Current Cross-Challenge Baseline

Design/evaluation label `cross-challenge-baseline-v1`, authorized 2026-09-14. Hypothesis: a frozen five-task diagnostic of the current runnable observed-continuous architecture will identify whether the next change should target sensing readiness, search progress, destination recognition/completion, or execution recovery. This is a real-model evaluation, not a software-suite run. No code, prompt, model, scene or scorer changes are included; generic AI-route control remains unavailable and is not being restored for this baseline.

Status: **completed**, 2026-09-14 12:04:42 +02:00. Fixed order: standalone `park`, standalone `furniture_circuit` (table/clockwise), then Shared Apartment V1 `apartment`, `flat_kitchen`, `recharge`. One independent reset/start per task; no manual placement, privileged steering, repeated attempts or tuning between cases. Settings: Luna, high reasoning, `luna_continuous`, enhanced rendering, adaptive exploration, camera history enabled, ordinary moving handoff disabled, two image slots, 8,192 context tokens, 0.25 s feedback setting and a 180 s wall-time-only control budget per trial. Actual control periods totalled 559.578 s plus initialization/cleanup. Configured deployment identity is recorded but underlying model identity remains unverified.

The existing evaluator ran two sequential fresh output batches: [standalone manifest](../.runtime/performance/cross-challenge-baseline-20260914-standalone/experiment.json) and [apartment manifest](../.runtime/performance/cross-challenge-baseline-20260914-apartment/experiment.json). Both retained variant `observed-continuous@0.1.0+47fc5f0cc16e/8b0bbab361bb`, full source/build fingerprint `7eef8c62f2b79c713a2303968af9f4d085380dd4b25b5195f4df33bddd387acd`, and no source drift. Every attempted outcome, input/response trace, initial scene, trajectory, final camera and independent score remains in the archive, including failures. Both evaluator processes completed; no retries or infrastructure replacements were required.

Preflight public-state checks found ports 8000/8002/8003/8004 idle, not busy, and at zero simulated seconds. Their episodes and processes are preserved, including the open port-8004 cockpit episode `e6a95734-9088-4615-a46e-b3507c108c5f`. RTX 3080: 10,240 MiB total, 3,026 MiB used, 7,028 MiB free, 11% instantaneous utilization. Trials run sequentially, but preserved renderers and other workstation activity prevent a controlled latency/throughput claim. Per-batch workload snapshots are whole-device instants, not continuous per-process attribution.

The [complete report](../.runtime/performance/cross-challenge-baseline-20260914-review/summary.md) records outcomes, tokens/latency, sensing limits, artifact links and failure hypotheses. Parking/table passed at 95.500/59.156 s; object search timed out after 180.219 s and 8.588 m; kitchen stopped on path rejection after 37.797 s and 0.500 m; recharge timed out at 180.250 s and 3.904 m with survey completed but no return. All five are complete, unassisted, zero-drop, zero sampled contacts and without false terminal completion claims. Total reported input/output tokens: 525,628 / 21,378; monetary cost uncalculated.

Priority evidence: recharge feedback showed battery falling from 86.452% to 26.351% with `low=true`, consistent with the specified survey energy cost, while later model memory continued to deny survey completion. Four unchanged `not_contained` checks and an expired intermediate floor target followed; no return occurred. Object search repeatedly reframed/approached a suspected target without executing the required wait/visual inspection. Kitchen lost rolling motion authority and its replacement explore path was rejected. Recommended next candidate: generic sensor-grounded subgoal progress and bounded repeated-inspection recovery, with kitchen rejection recovery kept separate. Scorer state must remain evaluator-only and safety thresholds must not be weakened. No candidate was implemented in this evaluation task.

Live validation confirmed both batches/five rows, all five final PNG/trajectory endpoints, correct pass/failure labels and unchanged idle episodes on ports 8000/8002/8003/8004. The shared archive is filtered to the baseline label. No software suite or build was rerun for this evaluation-only task; earlier full-suite failures remain. Historical circle successes and unsuccessful breadth/exploration pilots are context, not a matched comparison to this build. Model/architecture improvement and generalization remain **not measured** until a separately versioned candidate is evaluated under matched settings.

## Integrated Model Test Workspace

Design `integrated-model-test-workspace-v1`, 2026-09-14. Hypothesis: operators can inspect results during a model/robot trial without cancelling it, and explicit architecture/model provenance makes subsequent comparisons traceable. Here, **test normally means a model-driven robot trial**. Software suites and scripted inference are implementation checks, retained with separate evidence labels. This change affects browser trials across the supported control modes and adds version metadata to supervised CLI evaluations; it does not change navigation planning or raise safety limits.

Cockpit and archive remain mounted once opened. Internal links and browser Back/Forward change the visible view without closing its live socket, resetting the episode, or discarding control settings. Direct archive entry is read-only and opens no control socket until the cockpit is visited. Explicit Stop remains available in the archive. Actual reload, tab closure/disconnect, takeover, reset and Stop retain their motion-invalidation behavior. Hidden spectator rendering pauses. New browser sessions publish a provisional report every 0.5 s; the archive refreshes every 2 s during connected active control and every 15 s otherwise. **Show current test** locates the session. Finalization updates the score, final image and recorded route without a manual refresh or operator Stop. Running records never count as verified passes; a running heartbeat older than 15 s is labelled **Interrupted recording**.

Final reports retain initial/final tasks, assistance and recording completeness. Custom/revised tasks cannot inherit unrelated scenario success; the CLI benchmark summary now also respects an explicit `verification_eligible=false`. Progress rows separate architecture/model revision, task hash, environment, assistance, renderer, budget and camera history. Failed attempts remain in counts; completion medians use verified passes only. Scripted evidence, legacy records without versions and source-drift batches are excluded from this versioned real-model progress table, not deleted from the archive. This is a descriptive grouping, not an automatic causal ranking or matched benchmark.

### Version Rules

- [Architecture registry](../backend/experiment_variants.py): each supported controller family starts at semantic version `0.1.0` when this tracking scheme is introduced. This is a new provenance baseline, not a retroactive version assigned to earlier experiments. The historical AI-waypoint registry entry does not imply that the current API supports it.
- Architecture identity is `family@major.minor.patch+revision`, for example `observed-continuous@0.1.0+47fc5f0cc16e` for the final preview. The 12-character revision is derived from the full SHA256 mapping of backend Python sources, robot URDF and project metadata. Any covered source change updates the revision even when the human-managed semantic version is unchanged.
- Bump patch for a compatible correction within the design, minor for an added/changed control strategy or observation/tool capability, and major for incompatible control/observation contracts. Every intentional design change also receives a ledger label, hypothesis and evidence entry here. Never change archived labels or reports to make a newer result appear comparable.
- Model/configuration identity has a separate revision over provider, profile, deployment and relevant settings: reasoning, image/context/feedback settings, effective turn/time budget, handoff/adaptive controls, camera-history mode and applicable local-policy configuration. Changing the task does not change model identity; the task fingerprint is separate. No credentials enter these records. The combined variant ID joins architecture and model revisions.
- Legacy rows remain explicitly unversioned. A deployment name is not proof of underlying model identity. Endpoint revisions, cloud randomness and local checkpoint weight hashes are not pinned by this model ID; checkpoint names/paths are recorded where applicable. Underlying identity remains marked unverified.

Implementation hashes describe covered files on disk at session start, with end-of-session drift checks. They do not prove that a long-running Python process imported those exact bytes after source edits. Restart the backend after changes; use labelled frozen-code evaluator runs with matched tasks/settings and recorded runtime workload before claiming improvement. Interactive sessions are not controlled benchmarks. The new architecture fingerprint does not cover frontend renderer bundles/textures or dependency binaries; existing CLI design/build snapshots provide additional provenance. GUI and CLI representations of local checkpoint settings may differ, so their IDs are not an automatic equivalence guarantee.

### Reference Paths

The cockpit world view offers **Reference path** where a geometric scenario definition exists: a straight start-to-target line for `park`/`local_park`, or the requested-direction mid-annulus circle for furniture circuits. The circle includes the initial approach point. Unsupported/custom active tasks show no reference. The optional burgundy dotted line is labelled `scenario-reference-v1` and can be toggled without changing physics or camera bytes; matching session recordings retain the reference definition.

This is an operator reference, **not a proven optimal, shortest or collision-free path**. It is not obstacle-aware and does not authorize motion, including in shared layouts. Reference geometry is never added to AgentObservation, paired camera images, model tools or scenario public inputs. It does not implement following or figure-eight verification.

### Verification

The final production typecheck/Vite build passed with the existing large-bundle warning. [28 backend checks](../.runtime/integrated-tests-20260914-final-backend.xml) passed in 73.25 s, covering provenance, actual recording lifecycle, archive normalization, operator-reference boundaries and CLI metadata under scripted inference. Two dependency deprecation warnings remain. [Seven final browser workflows](../.runtime/integrated-tests-20260914-release-browser.xml) passed in about 1.1 min using real physics and scripted inference: same-tab navigation/filtering, live archive and explicit Stop, automatic bounded finalization with final route refresh, mocked architecture/task/assistance grouping, dotted references, empty/unavailable archive, and ordinary kitchen Start. These are not new real-model capability trials. Edited production files have no reported editor errors.

Desktop/mobile screenshots and pixel checks verify nonblank spectator rendering and toggled reference pixels at 1440/390/320 px, with unchanged model camera bytes and no page overflow. Preserved captures: [desktop reference](../.runtime/integrated-tests-20260914-release-dotted-reference-1440.png), [mobile reference](../.runtime/integrated-tests-20260914-release-dotted-reference-320.png), [desktop version groups](../.runtime/integrated-tests-20260914-release-variant-progress-1440.png), [mobile version groups](../.runtime/integrated-tests-20260914-release-variant-progress-320.png). Version-group screenshots use explicitly mocked metadata; they are not model trial evidence.

Failures were retained. One intermediate bounded-trial check exposed a recording interruption while the archive read a report: [failed browser run](../.runtime/integrated-tests-20260914-final-browser.xml), [recording trace](../.runtime/performance/browser-session-20260914T093414Z-bf0d5c73/bench/trace.json). A local Windows repro confirmed `PermissionError: WinError 5` when replacing a JSON file with an active read handle. Report publication and archive reads now share a process-local lock; final manifest/summary/report writes are atomic, and writer errors are retained in final reports. The lock contract and complete browser lifecycle then passed, including a second final run after navigation styling. External processes holding file handles are outside this process-local coordination.

The earlier [comprehensive regression](../.runtime/comprehensive-20260914/summary.md) finished with 582 backend passes / nine failures, a passing build, and 38 browser passes / six failures / one skip. Source changed during that run. Later focused fixes include ordinary Start's unsupported flag and the custom-task benchmark eligibility gate, but the complete suites were not rerun and are not claimed green. No new real-model performance, success-rate, recognition or operator-efficiency gain was measured for this workspace change.

Fresh preview: `http://127.0.0.1:8004/`, backend PID 50092, idle episode `e6a95734-9088-4615-a46e-b3507c108c5f`, zero simulated seconds and no automatic inference. Existing servers were preserved. Read-only live verification found 155 archived batches, including 79 labelled real-model and 15 with new version metadata; these counts describe archive contents, not verified passes. Old server processes serve the new frontend but lack the new API code, so use the fresh preview.

## Generic AI Routes

Historical design/evidence section. As of the integrated-workspace change above, the current backend contract lacks this integration; the following implementation statements describe the earlier experiment, not current availability.

Design `generic-ai-routes-v1`, 2026-09-14. Hypothesis: replace task-specific controller choices with a generic AI-generated rolling waypoint route and versioned task updates, while retaining deterministic local safety. The user specified arbitrary AI-supervised tasks, smooth motion and on-the-fly new commands, with circle/person-follow/figure-eight examples; see [acceptance requirements](TASK_CURRICULUM.md#user-requirements). User was unavailable for a design clarification and delegated autonomous implementation decisions. The conservative choice retains legacy controllers and exposes an explicit **AI-generated routes (experimental)** setting.

The model receives current paired AgentObservation/head RGB, labelled camera history and depth-derived free-floor samples in wheel-odometry coordinates. It returns `guide_route` with route, continue, look, wait or finish, never wheel commands or code. There are no circle/follow/figure-eight branches in the generic supervisor. SciPy cubic interpolation retains exact endpoints and a compatible current heading. Route length is 0.3-6 m, 1-24 waypoints; the worker validates all densely sampled points against fresh observed support/footprint, including spline overshoot. Moving replacements trim only a progress-constrained prefix, preserve runtime/velocity and reject incompatible/stale/revoked plans. The path-progress arrival check handles loops ending near their start. Existing speed, acceleration, proximity, stale-depth, Stop and two-second motor-buffer limits remain; each AI plan expires 20 s after capture, replies older than 15 s are rejected. Long unknown-space paths are rejected, never extrapolated blindly.

Live generic commands update the task revision, goal and instruction memory inside the same session without changing the time/turn budget or token totals. A new command cancels/discards the pending response and waits for its termination before another model call, preserving serialized inference. The worker rejects all superseded task revisions. An existing validated route may finish its remaining bounded travel while the new task is reviewed. Stop/cancel/takeover/reset/disconnect still stop motion and invalidate outstanding work. Non-motion actions stop first; current task revision is rechecked before task completion. This is not a guarantee that arbitrary changes are physically compatible without braking.

Evidence so far: [13 focused contracts/integration checks](../.runtime/generic-ai-routes-20260914-contracts.xml) passed in 38.08 s, including two recorded enhanced-physics live-update cases. [Task update](../.runtime/performance/scripted-physics-20260914T074611Z-050165ba/experiment.json) and [Stop race](../.runtime/performance/scripted-physics-20260914T074628Z-b974a4c8/experiment.json) used a declared open-floor fixture and scripted generic waypoint responses, not model recognition. Both recordings complete, zero dropped samples/contacts and no source drift during each test. The task update retained session, original deadline, stop revision and route identity with nonzero velocity while discarding an old deliberately delayed reply; Stop prevented the replacement from executing. Separate loop-progress and custom-goal scoring checks passed. Initial spline endpoint comparison exposed floating-point drift; endpoints were pinned exactly and the same geometry tests passed. Final frontend build and three focused UI/legacy/navigation tests passed in 17.0 s, with generic setting submission mocked, no model calls.

Completed development evaluation: nine real-model attempts, three tasks per internally frozen design. All were recorded with zero drops, no operator assistance, zero sampled contact episodes and no source drift within each attempt. Each had a 180 s control budget and enhanced rendering. Original table/clockwise used its independent lap scorer; custom figure-eight/follow goals were **unscored exploratory attempts** and cannot inherit circle/crossing success. None demonstrated full example-task completion. [All nine outcomes with source IDs and artifact directories](../.runtime/performance/generic-ai-routes-20260914-summary.json).

| Design / Reasoning | Table Circle | Figure Eight | Person Following |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| v1 / high | 46.141 s, incomplete model response, no useful translation | 180.157 s timeout, no useful translation | 180.250 s timeout; stopped-map freshness rejection, no useful translation |
| v2 refresh / high | 105.282 s, expired plan then incomplete model response, no useful translation | 180.328 s timeout, ambiguous target/route | 180.328 s timeout, ambiguous/lost target |
| v3 grounding / medium | 180.250 s timeout, 2.129 m, 0.219 m/s translating mean, 168.188 s stationary; 15/360 degrees | 16.390 s, model reported limitation, no useful translation | 37.250 s, model reported lost/ambiguous target, no useful translation |

Sources: v1 `8fa0dafe44729cc0aef744d64cdee7a7f4f46899654da8a4d2f8155f47b41588`, v2 `b781b4781e0373020b91c3e46a863c14610364482f6df77aa48efee5809e87f4`, v3 `1ba955d327db4b7940e018cb02a182c70a825ca73f4dd51d284f1439bdbcb170`. V3 manifests: [circle](../.runtime/performance/generic-ai-routes-v3-20260914-circle/experiment.json), [figure eight](../.runtime/performance/generic-ai-routes-v3-20260914-figure-eight/experiment.json), [follow](../.runtime/performance/generic-ai-routes-v3-20260914-follow/experiment.json). Final v3 attempt finished 2026-09-14 10:23:26 +02:00. V1/v2 high reasoning sometimes took >15 s or exhausted the model's 4,096 output-token cap; v3 intentionally changed reasoning to medium and is not a matched causal comparison. Prompts/source also changed. Concurrent port-8000/8002 sessions were preserved; preflight RTX 3080 used 2,199/10,240 MiB at 2%, not continuous workload measurement.

V2 repairs a demonstrated integration defect: stopped inference allowed the map to expire, so route installation now obtains new depth before validating, then rechecks plan age/authority. No freshness threshold was relaxed. V3 adds generic `measure` of a model-selected CURRENT image region using paired depth: visible surface median/bounds only, not object identity/center/complete shape/tracking or clearance. It also adds explicit `report_limit` and command-aware 0.5 s wait slices. No circle/follow/figure-eight motion routine was added. The pedestrian scene is a crossing fixture, not a qualified following benchmark; this work does not add a fast identity-preserving visual tracker or independently verify a figure-eight's two lobes/crossover. These remain implementation/qualification gaps, not successful capabilities.

After v3, traces showing confusion about historical versus current imagery prompted explicit labels before IMAGE 1 (current) and IMAGE 2 (history); pixels are unchanged. This clarification has unit/integration evidence only; its real-model effect is **not measured**. Browser-session provenance now records original/final goals, route mode and command revisions; revised/custom tasks cannot inherit an unrelated original scenario score. [Final generic checks](../.runtime/generic-ai-routes-20260914-final.xml): 21 passed in 45.97 s, including recorded live-update physics. [Recording/provenance/archive checks](../.runtime/generic-ai-routes-20260914-provenance.xml): 28 passed in 52.06 s. Earlier [focused regressions](../.runtime/generic-ai-routes-20260914-regressions.xml): 26 passed in 41.45 s. Full suites were not rerun; earlier unrelated renderer limitations remain.

Development tooling issue: a stale editor buffer overwrote earlier navigation test additions during a patch. The exact pre-overwrite local-history snapshot was restored (SHA256 `8781e35d797d632565e31891fdb9d92dc270e684f0bd3be801078c6c1866714e`), then all affected tests were rerun. New focused measurement/wait/provenance tests live in `tests/test_route_supervisor.py`. No controller or experiment evidence was lost. The initial measurement fixture used an unsupported 12 px image height; the final 24x16 fixture passes. No failed trial or earlier diagnostic was silently promoted to success.

Recommendation: retain the generic mode as opt-in. Reliable target tracking/association, shorter visual planning latency and independent task evaluators are required before claiming these requirements fully satisfied. The existing five successful legacy table laps must not be presented as generic AI-planning evidence.

Final preview: `http://127.0.0.1:8003/`, new idle episode `634617a3-796c-41be-8d0f-557b8feb9ed0`; existing ports 8000/8002 were preserved. The setting is visible under Run settings and remains off by default. Live archive audit loaded all nine attempts, their initial scenes, trajectories and PNGs; none is presented as a verified pass. Actual setting checks at 1440/320 px showed correct selected state, fitting labels and no page overflow; [desktop capture](../.runtime/generic-ai-control-20260914-settings-1440.png), [mobile capture](../.runtime/generic-ai-control-20260914-settings-320.png). Hidden integrated-browser stability waits timed out; DOM activation verified the state, while the isolated automated UI test had already passed real checkbox interaction. No extra model run or robot movement was initiated during preview checks. Current-image labels and revised-session provenance changed after the last real-model batch, so the final working tree is not falsely identified as that batch's frozen source.

## Compact Challenge Menu

Design `compact-challenge-menu-v1`, 2026-09-14. Hypothesis: a compact **Challenges** launcher beside the active challenge name preserves cockpit space while keeping scene switching accessible. It opens a labelled native dialog with environment/scenario controls, circuit options, existing scene preview and goal details. The dialog is rendered outside the cockpit layout through a portal, starts closed, and closes only after a successful load. Reopening preserves current/draft choices. Errors remain in the open dialog for retry; Escape and Close restore launcher focus. During loading, Close/Escape are disabled until the request completes; the dialog retains an accessible **Stop** button. Loading still uses the original stop-and-new-episode API, without changing controller/physics behaviour.

Final production build passed with the existing large-chunk warning. The [focused menu test](../.runtime/challenge-menu-20260914-final.xml) passed in 18.9 s including fixture startup, then [six affected workflows](../.runtime/challenge-menu-20260914-workflows.xml) passed in 72.61 s: all predefined scenarios/reset, shared apartment, preview browsing/retry, circuit options/reset, setup journey and auto-close/reopen/focus/error handling. Existing tests now explicitly open the dialog before selection and close it before cockpit interaction; physics assertions were retained. An initial check matched the hidden dialog heading inside the run bar; rendering the dialog through a portal removed that layout ambiguity and the same check passed. No new robot model evaluations were run.

Stable Playwright checks cover 1440/768/390/320 px. Additional live-build inspection used an isolated browser with a mocked live socket and zero non-GET API requests: no preview remained inside main layout, the closed launcher measured 36 px high, previews decoded at 480 px and the dialog/Stop control fit. [Desktop cockpit](../.runtime/challenge-menu-20260914-cockpit-1440.png), [mobile menu](../.runtime/challenge-menu-20260914-open-320.png). The integrated browser resized after screenshots (open-dialog checks observed 1158/258 px rather than the requested 1440/320); stable viewport evidence comes from the automated test. Existing Node-type and CSS line-clamp editor warnings remain; build and component diagnostics passed. Current app servers serve the new build without restart; open cockpit pages need refresh.

This requested UI/test change occurred while the comprehensive backend suite was running. Its source-drift evidence includes the menu and test changes; focused results here are separate from the [completed full-suite verdict](../.runtime/comprehensive-20260914/summary.md). No backend files or navigation tests were changed for the menu.

## Same-Tab Archive Navigation

Historical design `archive-same-tab-v1`, 2026-09-14, superseded by [the integrated workspace](#integrated-model-test-workspace), which preserves control during internal navigation. This version superseded the original popup behaviour below. The user explicitly requested no new window for Test archive. At this stage navigation used a plain same-tab anchor with no `target` or `window.open`; Test cockpit also returned in the same tab. The active view did not reload on an ordinary click. Browser-controlled modifier-click behaviour was not overridden.

Leaving the cockpit closes its live socket and triggers the existing disconnect-stop safeguard. The archive itself creates no live-control socket or motion request. Returning to the cockpit does not reset the episode or resume motion automatically. This intentional change replaces the earlier connection-preserving popup design; safety limits and backend control code are unchanged.

Verification: production typecheck/Vite build passed with the existing large-chunk warning. [Two focused browser tests](../.runtime/archive-same-tab-20260914-final.xml) passed in 28.0 s, exercising same-tab entry/return, no navigation-created page, active-page links, archive filtering/replay/download/error states and 1440/768/390/320 px layouts. The first test attempt counted an extra page across the entire archive workflow; the final test scopes no-popup checks immediately around each navigation instead of conflating them with other archive controls. Existing editor Node-type diagnostics remain separate from the successful build/browser run. No model calls or controller changes were made.

The requested UI/test changes were made while the earlier comprehensive backend suite was running. Its runner records source drift, and its later frontend stages use the updated files. Do not describe the combined comprehensive run as a single frozen-source baseline; these focused navigation results are separate evidence.

## Test View Navigation

Historical design `test-view-navigation-v1`, 2026-09-14; its popup behaviour is superseded by [same-tab navigation](#same-tab-archive-navigation). Hypothesis: shared navigation immediately below both headers makes the live test cockpit and read-only archive easy to find. The icon/text links use `aria-current=page`, keyboard focus styling and stable responsive dimensions. Originally, opening **Test archive** retained the isolated popup/no-opener behavior, keeping the original cockpit connected. **Test cockpit** navigated the archive window to live controls without resetting the episode. Clicking the current view did not reload it. Existing cockpit section links and the fixed Stop controls were unchanged.

Evidence is scripted browser/UI validation, not model inference. Final production build passed, retaining the existing large-chunk warning. Three focused Playwright tests passed in 23.2 s: return navigation without episode/time changes, archive popup isolation/filter/replay workflow, and unavailable/empty archive states. A first navigation sizing assertion found 103 px content-width links; setting a 132 px desktop minimum fixed it, and the same test set passed. View links were checked at 1440/768/390/320 px. Live checks at 1440/390/320 px confirmed visible, hit-testable links, correct active page, no page overflow and unobstructed Stop; screenshots [archive desktop](../.runtime/view-navigation-20260914-archive-1440.png) and [cockpit mobile](../.runtime/view-navigation-20260914-cockpit-320.png). The desktop screenshot captures archive loading; the focused archive test verifies loaded content. Temporary live cockpit checks triggered the existing disconnect-stop when closed; no motion or reset was requested. Full backend/frontend suites were not rerun. No new robot evaluation or performance improvement is claimed.

The new build is served by the existing app on port 8002 without restarting its backend. Historical frozen evaluation artifacts remain unchanged; the current source/build fingerprint now differs because of this UI-only change.

## Rolling Circuit Experiment

Design `rolling-circuit-v1`, 2026-09-14. Hypothesis: renewing arcs before endpoint braking preserves speed without raising speed limits. User approved a recorded comparison to the five-run `circuit-freshness-v1` baseline. No model prompt, object recognition, physical scene or scorer threshold changed. The worker alone validates each new <=1.8 m arc using current depth-map support and robot footprint. It preserves motion runtime, velocity and buffer; it never extends an expired lease or resumes after Stop. A checked path renews its distance authorization and <=60 s local deadline, bounded by the original 180 s mission and cumulative circumference +2 m allowance retained across recovery stops. Unknown continuation retains the stopping endpoint; stale sensing brakes. Ordinary navigation handoffs and parking behavior are unchanged.

Evidence: [27 safety/reporting checks](../.runtime/rolling-circuit-20260914-safety.xml) passed; [four final recorded physics checks](../.runtime/rolling-circuit-20260914-final-physics.xml) passed in 169.75 s; final frontend build and two archive browser tests passed in 21.3 s. Development failures remain visible. The first stale-injection batch had 3 passes / 1 safe timeout: recovery caused precise pursuit of a nearby radial waypoint and repeated turns, ending at 209 degrees. [Failed recorded trial](../.runtime/performance/scripted-physics-20260914T060027Z-53e6a84d/experiment.json). Circuit-only 0.35 m lookahead corrected that failure; [targeted recovery](../.runtime/rolling-circuit-20260914-recovery.xml) passed, then all four final variants passed. Scripted image boxes are explicit; actual depth/map/physics/control recordings include scene, trajectory, camera, scorecard and failure status. They appear as `rolling-circuit-v1-scripted` in Test results and are not pooled with real-model trials.

New motion metrics are interval-weighted from recorded samples: translation >0.015 m/s; stationary additionally <=0.04 rad/s; mean translation speed weighted by simulated time. Stationary wall time includes preparation, inference and final dwell. Route-arrival transitions include legitimate stops, so they are not labelled unnecessary. Old reports are not overwritten; historical metrics can be recomputed into a separate comparison artifact. The results build also completed a missing media-refresh state declaration left by prior archive work; automatic catalog refresh does not reset the selected replay.

Completed frozen batch: [manifest](../.runtime/performance/rolling-circuit-20260914/experiment.json), [five trial rows](../.runtime/performance/rolling-circuit-20260914/performance.json), finished 2026-09-14 08:18:36 +02:00. Source `84cb00ae966b60c4af3fa71e2c86dbb5ab6c4b6b018c01c31169d62c1795a369`, no source/build drift. Five sequential real-Luna standalone table/clockwise trials used enhanced rendering, high reasoning, adaptive navigation, camera history enabled, ordinary moving handoff disabled and a 180 s control budget. All five passed, with zero dropped samples, assistance, sampled contacts, false terminal completion claims or terminal errors. Completion times: **64.297, 59.218, 58.469, 58.250, 59.859 s**. Each used one model decision; totals 41,417 input / 1,740 output tokens. No circuit sensing recoveries or processing timeouts. Repeat 00 had one expired sensing result and an 11.031 s worker-lifetime processing-age maximum including preparation; remaining maxima 0.250-0.281 s. Rejected stale results are not evidence of motion authorized by stale sensing.

The [offline comparison](../.runtime/performance/rolling-circuit-comparison-20260914/summary.md) preserves automatic mismatch warnings and recomputes motion metrics from both original trajectories, without rewriting historical reports. Medians, baseline -> candidate: completion 100.219 -> 59.218 wall s; moving speed 0.184 -> 0.387 m/s; translating wall time 72.406 -> 33.016 s; stationary wall time 24.391 -> 23.030 s; route-arrival transitions 3 -> 2; distance 11.702 -> 11.715 m. Arrival counters are sampled transitions, not every braking event. The observed benefit is predominantly less slow translation. The 0.5 m/s command cap, 0.2 m/s obstacle cap, 0.3 m/s2 acceleration and 0.5 rad/s angular cap remain unchanged; sampled physical velocity can briefly overshoot a command cap.

Challenge hashes differ exactly because of the added `environment=standalone` field: removing only that field reproduces the historical hash. All five paired recorded initial scenes have identical 26-object nonrobot geometry/transforms and identical robot starting positions. Source differences include earlier apartment/recording work as well as the controller experiment. Preflight had no simulator listeners on ports 8000-8026; RTX 3080 10,240 MiB, 1,238 MiB used / 8,816 MiB free, 4% utilization. The manifest recorded 1,235 MiB used before/after, versus historical baseline 2,624 MiB used and four idle apps. These are instant whole-device snapshots, not a controlled continuous load. Model seed/underlying identity are unverified; do not attribute the entire difference solely to this change. Other objects, counterclockwise and shared-apartment real-model effects are **not measured**. Full backend/frontend suites were not rerun; the previously documented observatory layout failure is not claimed fixed.

Live archive verification on port 8000 found all five verified trials, all five trajectory/final-PNG endpoints and exact 26-object recorded initial scenes. The failed scripted recovery attempt remains visible as **Test failed**, evidence `scripted_test`. Isolated 1440/390 px browser checks had no horizontal page overflow, nonblank textured WebGL (479/298 sampled colors), changing playback pixels/time and 320 px decoded final cameras; zero live sockets, non-GET requests or page errors. [Desktop capture](../.runtime/performance/rolling-circuit-comparison-20260914/archive-1440.png), [mobile capture](../.runtime/performance/rolling-circuit-comparison-20260914/archive-390.png). These are UI checks, not extra model trials. Captures live beside the comparison so later Playwright runs do not clear them. Some earlier screenshot links under `frontend/test-results` now point to cleaned test output; their original numerical evidence remains above, but those historical images are unavailable.

## Navigation Breadth Pilot

Authorized 2026-09-14: try other test scenarios after the rolling-circuit experiment. Design label `rolling-circuit-v1-breadth-pilot`; no controller, prompt, physics, scorer or frontend changes. Hypothesis: current observed-map navigation can complete individual parking, search, room-arrival and charger-return tasks within the existing safeguards. This is a four-case exploratory capability check, one attempt per task, not a repeatability gate or before/after improvement experiment.

Fixed order: standalone `park`, then Shared Apartment V1 `apartment`, `flat_kitchen`, `recharge`. Each task starts in its prescribed independent scene; no manual relocation, privileged steering or combined-mission memory. Settings: real Luna, continuous local control, enhanced rendering, high reasoning, adaptive exploration, camera history enabled, ordinary moving handoff disabled, 180 wall seconds per trial. At most 12 minutes of control budgets plus initialization/cleanup. Outputs are fresh directories `.runtime/performance/navigation-breadth-20260914-standalone` and `.runtime/performance/navigation-breadth-20260914-apartment`; every attempted outcome, trace, trajectory, initial scene and final image is retained and exposed in Test results. Source/build must stay frozen across both batches; no tuning between scenarios.

Status: **completed**, 2026-09-14 08:50:06 +02:00. Both manifests use source `84cb00ae966b60c4af3fa71e2c86dbb5ab6c4b6b018c01c31169d62c1795a369`, identical to the preceding circle batch, with no source drift. All four planned attempts produced reports, traces, final cameras, initial scenes, trajectories and scorecards; no dropped samples, operator assistance or sampled contact episodes. No controller changes, tuning or retries were made. No new unit/build/browser suite run was needed for this evaluation-only task; prior full-suite limitations remain.

| Task / Environment | Verified / Attempts | Outcome | Elapsed Wall S | Travel M | Moving M/S | Stationary Wall S | Input / Output Tokens |
| --- | --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Park / standalone | 0 / 1 | Required depth state unavailable; no route started | 27.766 | 0.001 | Not moving | 27.766 | 7,429 / 894 |
| Object search / shared apartment | 0 / 1 | Time limit; target not inspected | 180.188 | 10.570 | 0.209 | 90.547 | 110,244 / 4,503 |
| Kitchen / shared apartment | 0 / 1 | False completion claim; physical arrival failed | 174.609 | 6.551 | 0.188 | 76.688 | 101,242 / 4,290 |
| Recharge / shared apartment | 0 / 1 | Time limit; survey complete, return/charge incomplete | 180.187 | 4.179 | 0.161 | 133.688 | 254,253 / 8,908 |

These are elapsed times to unsuccessful termination, **not completion times or speed gains**. Moving speed uses simulated-time weighting while translating; stationary time includes preparation, inference and final dwell. Total reported usage: 473,168 input / 18,595 output tokens; underlying model identity and monetary cost are unverified. Do not pool these different tasks into a general success-rate estimate.

Artifacts: [standalone manifest](../.runtime/performance/navigation-breadth-20260914-standalone/experiment.json), [standalone metrics](../.runtime/performance/navigation-breadth-20260914-standalone/performance.json), [apartment manifest](../.runtime/performance/navigation-breadth-20260914-apartment/experiment.json), [apartment metrics](../.runtime/performance/navigation-breadth-20260914-apartment/performance.json). Per-case reports: [parking](../.runtime/performance/navigation-breadth-20260914-standalone/park/report.json), [object search](../.runtime/performance/navigation-breadth-20260914-apartment/shared-apartment-v1-apartment/report.json), [kitchen](../.runtime/performance/navigation-breadth-20260914-apartment/shared-apartment-v1-flat_kitchen/report.json), [recharge](../.runtime/performance/navigation-breadth-20260914-apartment/shared-apartment-v1-recharge/report.json). Search `navigation-breadth-20260914` with **All scenarios / Real model** in Test results; the kitchen row explicitly shows **False completion claim**. All failures remain visible.

Failure interpretation: parking selected a measured destination after a 15.312 s model response, but `start_continuous` rejected the start with `SPATIAL_REQUIRED`. The trace cannot distinguish disabled sensing, absent map or a missing retained frame; a targeted repro should establish the cause before changing freshness logic. Object search completed seven local routes but repeatedly described floor/table legs and never satisfied target inspection.
