Kitchen took four local routes, then two arrival-inspection requests and a final claim based on blue/orange fixture descriptions; the physics scorer remained in progress. Clearance/surface matching did not establish correct-room arrival. Recharge completed five local routes and the physical survey; turns 7-13 repeatedly requested arrival inspection, while return and charging remained incomplete. It spent 115.454 wall seconds in recorded inference-wait activity, including an unfinished request. These traces support investigating task progress/arrival handling and semantic verification; they do not isolate a single causal defect or prove extra time would solve the tasks. Keep actual geometry/scorer information evaluator-only in any follow-up.

Preflight preserved the idle port-8000 preview (run `944794f6-4c9b-497c-b46c-6ff949479679`, zero simulated seconds); no other app listeners on 8000-8026. RTX 3080 10,240 MiB: 1,430 MiB used / 8,624 MiB free, 6% instantaneous utilization. After snapshots: standalone 1,406 MiB used / 8,648 MiB free at 8%; apartment 1,442 MiB used / 8,612 MiB free at 2%. Worker-lifetime expired sensing counts were 1/1/0/3 in trial order, including preparation; no processing timeouts. These snapshots are not continuous/per-process workload measurements. One attempt per static task is exploratory, not qualification; shared-apartment circles, other objects/directions, seeded/held-out layouts and combined missions remain unmeasured. Do not pool with scripted apartment mechanics or standalone circle repetitions. Cloud randomness, model identity and continuous workstation load remain uncontrolled.

## Rolling Exploration Experiment

Authorized 2026-09-14 after the breadth pilot: keep search moving while its supervisor updates the path. Hypothesis: an observed search direction, renewed locally while Luna processes paired images, removes intermediate endpoint braking and inference waits without removing safety stops. The old opt-in handoff retains its original two-update / 1.8 m budget; enabling it alone cannot sustain motion through 6-15 s model latency.

Design `rolling-exploration-v1` introduces `explore` for room/object search, distinct from `navigate` and `approach_target`. A current observed candidate supplies direction, never hidden target coordinates. Every <=1.8 m extension is checked against fresh depth-map support, current footprint and existing swept-motion/clearance gates. Smooth 1.4 m paths are considered at most every 0.25 s, before endpoint braking. Runtime, velocity and buffers are retained. Unknown continuation retains the stopping endpoint. Speed/acceleration/freshness/scorer limits are unchanged.

Luna reviews serially while moving. `explore` redirects a compatible observed direction; `continue` retains it. Each review grants at most 20 s and 6 m from its captured state, with a 15 s reply age limit and an original 180 s exploration ceiling. Only matching live run/epoch/Stop/intent identity can renew; local path revisions do not invalidate the semantic review. Local extensions cannot extend the supervisor lease. Expired review, stale sensing, changed posture, buffer expiry, Stop, takeover, reset and disconnect remain authoritative. Non-search decisions end renewal and wait at the existing endpoint for fresh stopped feedback before inspection or precise navigation. Late replies cannot resurrect motion.

An explicit still-valid selected sensor no longer depends on remaining in the eight-frame cache; the worker still checks run/epoch, age and unchanged pose, then validates against a newly sampled map. Raw pixel requests without a retained frame remain rejected. This addresses the locally reproduced cache boundary, not a proven diagnosis of the earlier parking failure. Trajectories record rolling mode, handoffs and supervisor review count. No evaluator/spectator information enters policy inputs.

Scripted evidence: [five recorded physics checks](../.runtime/rolling-exploration-20260914-physics.xml) passed in 103.57 s, using actual enhanced depth/wheels and an explicit open-floor fixture with furniture removed. Decisions select observed candidates through a scripted supervisor. [Slow review](../.runtime/performance/scripted-physics-20260914T070109Z-991d0e6f/experiment.json): 3.295 m, seven renewals, no arrivals and 99 states above 0.35 m/s during inference. [Inspection](../.runtime/performance/scripted-physics-20260914T070222Z-0b62769d/experiment.json): 4.193 m, six renewals and one intentional arrival. [Stop](../.runtime/performance/scripted-physics-20260914T070134Z-437f7ed0/experiment.json), [stale depth](../.runtime/performance/scripted-physics-20260914T070150Z-3859d14a/experiment.json) and [review expiry](../.runtime/performance/scripted-physics-20260914T070206Z-a3785da1/experiment.json) stopped correctly. All five recordings are complete, zero-drop, zero-contact and frozen internally. They are not furniture-task passes or real-model autonomy evidence.

[Review/sensor checks](../.runtime/rolling-exploration-20260914-review.xml): 14 passed; earlier 21 intent/worker/review checks passed. [Regression slice](../.runtime/rolling-exploration-20260914-regressions.xml): 64 passed, one unrelated legacy training-data recorder failed its camera-size contract under enhanced rendering. That unchanged recorder passed with the supported [tiny renderer](../.runtime/rolling-exploration-20260914-legacy-recording-tiny.xml); an intermediate recheck used invalid renderer name `cpu`. The initial slow-review harness had an unawaited-coroutine warning, fixed and [rerun cleanly](../.runtime/rolling-exploration-20260914-slow-final.xml). All attempts are retained. Full suites and general reliability are not claimed green.

Completed real validation: one Shared Apartment V1 object-search and one kitchen-navigation trial, 180 s budgets, enhanced rendering, high reasoning, adaptive exploration, history enabled and ordinary handoff disabled. [Manifest](../.runtime/performance/rolling-exploration-20260914/experiment.json), [trial metrics](../.runtime/performance/rolling-exploration-20260914/performance.json), [matched-case comparison](../.runtime/performance/rolling-exploration-comparison-20260914/summary.md). Experiment `fdd9ae71-e873-4ad5-b1c3-b6ab9330938b`, source `950746441fe601d4ad3229f3d7323cbefc841836064732994c5c1ab72a5c1a06`, finished 2026-09-14 09:13:32 +02:00, no drift. Both challenge hashes and evaluation settings match their breadth-pilot cases; code/prompt intentionally differs. No tuning/retries during this batch.

| Case | Result | Elapsed Recording S | Travel M | Mean Moving M/S | Stationary S | Path Renewals / Accepted Reviews |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Object search | Blocked-path error, no target inspection | 67.282 | 4.129 | 0.335 | 19.453 | 5 / 1 |
| Kitchen search | 180 s time limit, kitchen not reached | 180.203 | 19.035 | 0.330 | 17.672 | 27 / 11 |

Both recordings are complete, zero-drop, unassisted, zero sampled contacts and no false completion claims. The original object-search report measured evaluation elapsed 67.266 s before final capture; trajectory elapsed is 67.282 s. Its final ordinary destination proposal was rejected with `OBSERVED_PATH_BLOCKED` after rolling travel ended for a stopped review. This is retained as an unsuccessful run, not bypassed. Kitchen had four sampled route arrivals, the same as its earlier trial; smoother motion does not mean all stops disappeared. Stationary share fell from 43.9% to 9.8%, moving speed rose from 0.188 to 0.330 m/s, and distance rose from 6.551 to 19.035 m. More travel did not find the kitchen. Reported tokens rose from 105,532 to 287,529 for that case. Object-search totals cover unequal durations and are not directly comparable as savings.

Recorded translation during the `thinking` phase was 44.062 s for object search and 126.874 s for kitchen, versus zero in each earlier trial. This phase can include draining a route after a reply, so it is not an exact inference-execution overlap measure. Positive sampled handoff-count increments give the renewal totals; legacy trajectories lacked that field. Scorer, speed, obstacle and Stop limits were unchanged. Follower updates are not hard-real-time; the reported maximum moving update gap in the object trial reached 0.391 s. Per-worker sensing expiry counts were 1 and 3 with no processing timeouts; lifetime maxima include preparation and rejected frames, not proof of stale motion authorization.

Preflight preserved the idle port-8000 instance at zero simulation time; RTX 3080 10,240 MiB had 1,563 MiB used / 8,491 MiB free at 10%. Manifest whole-device snapshots were 1,529 MiB used at 42% before and 1,728 MiB used at 5% after. Load was not continuously controlled. The same static cases were tried once per design, not counterbalanced or held out; cloud seed and underlying model identity remain unverified. No task-success or causal improvement claim. Generalized exploration, correct semantic arrival and recovery from blocked destination proposals remain unfinished.

Live verification: updated backend on port 8002 exposes `explore`, while its new episode remains idle at zero simulated time. Port 8000 and its original episode were preserved. Both candidate recordings load with 123-object recorded initial scenes, 646/2,031 source samples and final PNGs; failures remain labelled **Not passed**. Isolated desktop/mobile checks at 1440/390 px verified changed playback pixels/time, 1,153/690 sampled WebGL colors, loaded textures, 320 px final images and no horizontal page overflow, live sockets, non-GET requests or page errors. [Desktop](../.runtime/performance/rolling-exploration-comparison-20260914/archive-1440.png), [mobile](../.runtime/performance/rolling-exploration-comparison-20260914/archive-390.png). The test context's catalog request was cancelled on close; no app error or extra robot trial resulted. No frontend source changed or rebuild was needed; backend diagnostics and touched-file whitespace checks are clean.

## Shared Apartment Validation

The [curriculum](TASK_CURRICULUM.md#shared-apartment-v1) fixes one static apartment for table circling, yellow-object search, kitchen arrival and recharge. Initial physical checks exposed a real scene defect: the charging beacon intersected the robot's circuit footprint, failing both directions while the other three routes passed (2 failed, 3 passed in 117.23 s). Moving that beacon to the other side of its unchanged pad made all five pass; speed and scorer tolerances were not loosened. These are privileged scripted steering tests. Circle tests set the initial annulus pose; search/recharge tests drive from their task starts. Passing them establishes mechanical feasibility, not autonomous recognition, route planning or statistical reliability.

The browser check covers four selections, reset/reload persistence, head-camera assets, nonblank canvas pixels, actual movement, explicit resume after reload's disconnect stop, final Stop and 1440/768/390/320-pixel layouts. An initial preview-packaging failure was resolved by rebuilding after asset generation. Screenshot inspection then exposed close-up framing inherited from standalone recharge; all shared variants now use large-facility framing. Final [preview asset](../frontend/public/scenario-previews/shared_apartment_v1.webp), [desktop world](../frontend/test-results/shared-apartment-world-1440.png), [mobile world](../frontend/test-results/shared-apartment-world-320.png) and [mobile setup](../frontend/test-results/shared-apartment-setup-320.png) are from the actual scene. Final focused browser run: shared apartment, all standalone scenarios, and two archive workflows, **4 passed**. Final build retains the existing chunk-size warning; editor diagnostics clean. Full backend/browser suites were not rerun; the previously documented observatory layout failure is not claimed resolved.

The initial mechanics/UI validation did not include apartment real-model trials. The subsequent [breadth pilot](#navigation-breadth-pilot) tested three apartment tasks and recorded no verified completions. No improvement claim is made relative to the standalone circuit. Initial scenes are static in saved replay; dynamic actor playback, generalized search, person tracking and manipulation remain deferred. CPU-only physics checks and enhanced browser checks stay separate from real-model evidence.

## Single-Scenario Benchmark

The user authorized the [household task curriculum](TASK_CURRICULUM.md) on 2026-09-13. Begin with the five-run circle qualification below, then build shared-apartment tasks separately; approval does not turn planned capabilities into measured results. Preserve this standalone circuit as the regression anchor.

Initial focus: **Circle the Furniture, table, clockwise**, using the existing default start and unchanged physics scorer. It exercises current-image object selection, sustained closed-loop curved motion, partially observed floor, rescanning, stale-depth recovery, lap closure and stopping. It does not qualify doorway traversal, room search, payload transport or manipulation. Those experiments stay deferred until this benchmark is stable.

Use `/?view=test-results&scenario=furniture_circuit` to open its saved-run overview. The scenario filter scopes the selected trials and verified-pass denominator; All scenarios still exposes the rest of the archive. Older changing-code runs remain visible but must not be pooled into a benchmark success rate.

Completed qualification: five sequential real-Luna repetitions of this one task, under frozen source `5badcc913e07547df2fd8f7c6703f4f80ba155d9485d53ed2d5b709f8114d196` and `circuit-freshness-v1`. Enhanced rendering, high reasoning, adaptive navigation, history enabled, moving handoff disabled, arm preparation and 180-second control budgets were matched. No tuning between repetitions; manifest confirms no source drift. A causal improvement claim still needs a matched frozen baseline; the older shared-workstation pilot is diagnostic context only.

Artifacts: [manifest](../.runtime/performance/circuit-qualification-20260913/experiment.json), [per-trial metrics](../.runtime/performance/circuit-qualification-20260913/performance.json), and per-repeat reports, traces and recordings beneath that directory. All **5/5 passed**, unassisted and physics-verified, with complete recordings, zero sampled contact episodes, no false completion claims and no terminal errors. Completion seconds by repeat: **104.375, 97.969, 98.250, 100.328, 100.219** (median 100.219); travel 11.700-11.704 m. One model decision per trial; 41,347 input and 1,879 output tokens total, individual inference latencies 7.109-12.828 s. No circuit sensing recoveries or processing timeouts occurred. Repeat 00 recorded one expired sensing result and an 11.781 s worker-lifetime maximum processing age, including preparation; remaining maxima 0.266-0.328 s. This does not imply motion used an 11.781 s old result.

Batch finished 2026-09-13 22:25:08 +02:00. The initial fixed-task development gate passed; broader autonomy and reliability are not established. Underlying model identity remains unverified beyond the configured Luna deployment. Four idle app instances on ports 8015/8022/8023/8024 were preserved. Whole-device GPU snapshots: RTX 3080 10 GB, used/free 2,624/7,430 MiB at 10% before and 2,705/7,349 MiB at 3% after; not continuous or per-process workload measurement. An auxiliary editor-side wait lacked psutil and had no effect on the repository-runtime evaluation. Source freeze is released after verification; subsequent apartment changes use a separate design label.

Report verified completion, completion wall time, actual travel, sampled contact episodes, recording completeness/drops, terminal failures, false completion claims, model tokens/latency, sensing age and recovery counts. Inspect the recorded route for incomplete arcs, drift, unnecessary loops and stationary intervals. Require full scorer completion, no operator assistance, no sampled contacts and a complete recording for an accepted benchmark trial. Retain the separate Stop and injected-staleness regressions as safety gates; five passes would still be a small development qualification, not general reliability.

### Sensing Evidence

`GET /api/spatial` now includes worker-lifetime `timing`: maximum capture latency, maximum post-capture latency, accepted/expired completed results and blocking-wait timeout counts. `maximum_processing_age_s` includes completed results rejected at installation. Post-capture time includes queueing, processing and worker scheduling, not just CPU execution. Existing one-second map freshness and motion/mission deadlines are unchanged. Stop revisions are rechecked after capture and planning before starting an arc.

The scripted four-case physics test measured ordinary capture maxima of about 94 ms in the two Stop cases; injected publication withholding intentionally increased post-capture age. These checks are not recorded real-model benchmark runs and are not imported into the results catalog. They do not establish zero contacts throughout a recorded run: the tests assert final contact state and successful full-lap scorer conditions, but do not create full trajectory recordings.

Final focused sensing/control regressions: **21 passed in 76.52 s**, covering expired publication, bounded circuit recovery, braking and Stop/takeover/reset/disconnect ownership ([JUnit](../.runtime/sensing-recovery-20260913-safety.xml)). The full backend suite was not run. Two existing Starlette/AnyIO deprecation warnings remain.

### Route Archive

The lazy trajectory endpoint reads only the selected allowlisted `recording/trajectory.jsonl`, off the event loop. Coordinates use recorded physics world XY, separate from controller odometry. It preserves first/last points, shows a uniform display sample of at most 1,501 points, retains up to 200 contact-onset markers, and breaks path segments at recorded manual relocations. Counts and display sampling are labelled. Missing, corrupt, oversized or nonmonotonic recordings are unavailable; no synthetic path is inferred from the final image. Limits: 64 MB / 50,000 source samples per route. The final camera remains explicitly a final image while the route is scrubbed.

Live archive verification on port 8023 loaded six historical furniture batches. The selected current-only pilot recording contained 2,064 samples, displayed as 1,033 points, with its final 320-pixel camera image. Playback advanced from 0.016 to 0.766 s and end scrubbing reached the exact 178.21900000001187-second final sample. This check exposed fixed-step range rounding; the scrubber now permits fractional endpoints, covered by the existing browser test with a 20.003-second fixture. These are archive-playback checks, not new robot trials.

At 1440/768/390/320 px, measured map widths were 614.1/468.8/343.2/272.8 px, without horizontal overflow or camera overlap. [Desktop capture](../.runtime/route-overview-20260913-1440.png) and [mobile capture](../.runtime/route-overview-20260913-320.png) show the actual historical route. Browser capture framing is tool-dependent; layout assertions are the responsive evidence. The isolated results page opened zero live sockets, made zero non-GET requests and raised no page errors. The preview robot remained idle at zero simulated seconds; port 8022 was not restarted.

Final frontend build passed, retaining the existing large-chunk warning. The two focused results browser tests passed in 14.9 s after the fractional-endpoint fix. The preceding full browser run had **40 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed**: the existing observatory test expected the Robot goal field above y=450 but measured y=575. That failure reproduced in isolation; the main layout and its assertion were left unchanged. Full-browser validation is therefore **not green**, and the full suite was not rerun after the endpoint-only fix. No new real-model qualification repetitions were executed.

### Environment Overlay

The saved-route viewer now reuses the simulator's Three.js geometry/material helpers for top-down and angled 3D environments. The route, start/end/contact markers and selected-time marker share physics world coordinates with the furniture. Orbit controls provide rotation, pan and zoom; object picking displays the saved/generated name. Route framing keeps the relevant furniture prominent, while whole-room framing fits the projected 3D bounds. The plain XY view remains available, and the final camera/metrics retain their previous meaning.

New `RunRecorder` captures save `recording/scene.json` with initial geometry and link/body transforms. Scene capture is observational: the test verifies unchanged simulation time and no geometry in policy observations. It does add one initial snapshot operation; its latency impact is **not measured**. The archive filters robot geometry out of the environment, validates shape/pose vectors and scene size (8 MB / 2,000 entries), allows only local texture URLs and never exposes arbitrary metadata. Malformed scene data does not invalidate an otherwise valid route.

Legacy recordings have no scene snapshot. Their overlay is generated from the current structured challenge definition, without starting or controlling a simulator, and labelled **Reconstructed current environment; historical layout unverified**. These are not frozen historical reconstructions. Recorded initial scenes also remain static during playback; moving props, people and manipulation effects are not reconstructed over time. Textures are resolved from the current app rather than archived bitmap versions. Geometry is evaluator/operator-only and never becomes a model input.

Evidence: the 11-test backend slice covers scene capture, bounded recording, recorded transforms, robot/private-field exclusion, unsafe texture rejection, legacy reconstruction, corrupt scenes and existing route/path boundaries. The final two browser tests cover nonblank WebGL pixels, object picking, orbit dragging, playback/scrub endpoints, view/frame controls, isolation and fixed 1440/768/390/320 px layouts. A test initially retained an outdated path-only expectation after batch selection; that assertion was corrected and the same tests passed. Pylance syntax and editor diagnostics are clean. Build retains the existing large-chunk warning.

Actual archive verification on port 8024 loaded 26 environment objects and all four local textures. Top/3D canvases had 194-592 sampled colors and 1,572-4,492 route-colored pixels, no overflow or overlap with the camera; playback advanced from 0.016 to 1.188 seconds. The integrated browser changed its viewport after captures (actual widths 284-1,687 px), so the stable-width responsive evidence comes from Playwright's isolated test runner. Earlier forced-click captures failed to change modes; final captures explicitly verified selected controls. [Top capture](../.runtime/environment-overlay-20260913-1440-top.png), [3D capture](../.runtime/environment-overlay-20260913-1440-3d.png), [mobile 3D capture](../.runtime/environment-overlay-20260913-390-3d.png). Zero non-GET requests, live sockets or page errors were observed in the isolated results page. The pre-existing full-suite observatory y-position assertion remains outside this change; the full suites were not rerun. No new real-model benchmark was executed.

## Automatic Run Records

Use `scripts.evaluate_supervised` with a meaningful `--design` label and a fresh output directory. It now saves:

- `experiment.json`: experiment UUID/time, design/source/build/texture hashes (including simulation, camera and visual history), challenge/goal definitions by hash, runtime package/hardware identity, model deployment, reasoning, budgets, camera settings and history condition. An end-of-run check marks source changes during the experiment.
- Per-case `report.json` and existing recordings/traces: actual renderer, physics outcome, contacts, distance, completion time, model latency, tokens, images and historical-image counts.
- `performance.json`: one row for **every planned case**, including missing/interrupted reports. Success requires physical completion, a complete unassisted recording and a measured completion time. Unknown values remain null.
- `.runtime/performance/index.jsonl`: one appended experiment reference with planned/reported counts, verified successes and evidence type. Evaluations must run serially. Override with `--performance-index` for isolated tests or separate collections.

The index and raw artifacts are local and not automatically backed up or committed. Keep the tracked ledger updated with important results and archive raw output directories before cleaning `.runtime`. Browser controller sessions now save replayable recordings with explicit evidence and intervention labels; they are **not automatically benchmark runs**. The scenario-test recorder similarly exposes scripted physics attempts, including failures. Raw pytest/Playwright logs are not robot replays. A killed process may not reach final index writing; its prewritten experiment manifest and per-case files remain the source of truth.

The shared **Test cockpit / Test archive** navigation highlights the current view. **Test archive** opens `/?view=test-results` in a separate window/tab, preserving the cockpit connection; **Test cockpit** returns the archive window to `/` without resetting the episode. The archive reads a normalized, allowlisted catalog through `GET /api/test-results`, showing three batches initially with Show more/View all, search, scenario/evidence filters, trial details, final camera images and JSON download. Older challenge-list manifests are supported; file modification time is labelled when no recorded timestamp exists. Legacy physics-success flags without a complete unassisted recording and measured completion time are not promoted to verified passes. This is a browsing view, not a pooled success-rate benchmark or automatic before/after comparison.

`real_model`, `scripted_test`, and `scripted_reference` results must stay separate. Configured deployment names do not verify the underlying model version. The index is an inventory, not a global leaderboard; do not pool different scenarios, budgets, rendering paths or changing code indiscriminately.

## Matched Pilot

Status: **completed, authorized 2026-09-13**. Four challenges above, one trial per condition (eight trials), 180 wall seconds per trial including controller preparation/inference but excluding scene initialization. All planned attempts are retained. This is exploratory, not enough to establish reliability; repeats remain unrun. [Complete results and trace analysis](../.runtime/performance/history-ab-20260913/summary.md).

Run order was Park A/B, Apartment B/A, Kitchen A/B, Furniture B/A, where A is current-only and B is history-enabled. Outputs use fresh directories under `.runtime/performance/history-ab-20260913`. Both used enhanced rendering, high reasoning, adaptive exploration and no moving handoff. All eight source fingerprints match `bbd4ae39b16b309bd46289333b59daa6ee355f28bc163137d2c6f53ee1b9ead0`, with no source drift; all four comparisons report no configuration, challenge or runtime mismatches. Preflight found 21 idle/stopped simulator servers, including three with resident local models; existing sessions were preserved. RTX 3080 10,240 MiB: 8,968 MiB used, 1,086 MiB free, 7% GPU utilization at preflight; 8,911 used / 1,143 free / 7% afterward. Load throughout the run was not measured. These are shared-workstation development trials, not an isolated hardware benchmark.

Compare `--camera-history disabled` with `enabled` in the same frozen code, model deployment/reasoning, enhanced renderer, challenge definitions/starting poses, turn/time budgets, arm preparation, safety checks and machine conditions. The disabled condition sends the current image only and denies original inspection. It still performs the same local history processing, so this measures the **input-history effect**, not capture cost. It is not an exact reconstruction of the older raw+annotated+panorama design.

Counterbalance order by running A/B for one challenge and B/A for the next; reverse for repeats. Do not edit source or tune prompts between paired trials. Keep failed/time-limited/error attempts. No assisted relocation, private-map teacher, relaxed freshness limits or changed scorer. Record concurrent workload and any deployment changes; current runtime metadata does not measure full machine-load history.

Commands below show one matched pair; use fresh output paths. They **make real model calls**. Set `MILO_RENDERER=enhanced` for both child processes or pass the equivalent environment through your runner.

```powershell
./.runtime/env/python.exe -m scripts.evaluate_supervised --mode luna_continuous --challenges park --time-limit-s 180 --reasoning high --design current-only-ablation-v1 --camera-history disabled --output .runtime/performance/park-a
./.runtime/env/python.exe -m scripts.evaluate_supervised --mode luna_continuous --challenges park --time-limit-s 180 --reasoning high --design motion-history-v1 --camera-history enabled --output .runtime/performance/park-b
```

Offline comparison (no model calls):

```powershell
./.runtime/env/python.exe -m scripts.evaluate_supervised --compare .runtime/performance/park-a .runtime/performance/park-b --output .runtime/performance/park-comparison
```

This writes JSON and Markdown per-challenge tables and flags mismatched settings, source, runtime, renderer, scenarios/repeats, evidence or missing records. Older artifacts without version-2 provenance require explicitly documented retrospective analysis. There is no automatic winner. Review changed files before attribution, even when only intended files differ.

## Decision Rules

Primary: physics-verified success rate per challenge with every planned attempt visible. Also record false completion claims and contact episodes. Compare successful completion times only alongside success rates; an early failure is not a speed improvement. Inspect paired outcomes and distributions, not just an aggregate average.

Secondary: travel distance, input/output tokens, images sent, inference latency, rescans/recovery, and stale-sensor stops. Dollar cost needs verified model pricing; tokens alone are not dollars. Similar frame counts do not imply similar image-token charges.

To claim improved brief-object recognition, use annotated fixed recordings or explicit target-visibility events and score missed sightings and false identifications separately. Task success alone does not isolate perception. Only promote a change after repeatable gains with no unacceptable safety/retention regressions; otherwise mark mixed, regressed or inconclusive and retain the baseline.

## Consumer cockpit disclosure — 2026-09-20

- **Design label:** `consumer-cockpit-disclosure-v1`.
- **Hypothesis:** keeping settings in an on-demand left drawer, separating scenario loading from Start/Stop/Reset, and showing model activity beside the world makes routine use easier while retaining detailed diagnostics.
- **Affected scenarios:** all cockpit scenarios, including the existing maze work. Controller requests, physics, model input boundaries, and scoring are unchanged.
- **Changes:** one labeled run-control group; a separate Load scenario entry; Robot settings in a left drawer with power/manual access; a visible model activity card with total requests, reported tokens and latest response latency; drill-down to model exchanges and detailed telemetry. Trace requests pause while hidden and catch up when opened. Raw telemetry starts collapsed. Settings retain their values on close and return keyboard focus to their opener; the shared Stop and Reset controls remain available in dialogs.
- **Evidence type:** scripted browser UI and real-physics fixture checks, without Foundry calls. Real-model navigation performance and consumer usability effects are **not measured**.
- **Validation:** production build and whitespace checks pass; the existing large-chunk warning remains. Ten targeted browser checks have passed across the final runs: [nine passing checks and the retained initial manual-control failure](../.runtime/consumer-cockpit-disclosure-v1/browser-checks.xml), followed by the [passing manual-control rerun](../.runtime/consumer-cockpit-disclosure-v1/manual-check.xml). The manual fixture now explicitly re-enables control after a disconnect stop. The checks cover 1440/1024/850/768/390/320 px layouts (depending on test), no horizontal overflow, model totals including task supervision, lazy trace loading and catch-up, keyboard focus, settings saves/errors, power cycling, dialog Stop/Reset access, manual commands and maze loading/reset.
- **Artifacts:** [desktop UI with scripted activity](../.runtime/consumer-cockpit-disclosure-v1/cockpit-1440.png), [320 px UI with scripted activity](../.runtime/consumer-cockpit-disclosure-v1/cockpit-320.png), [left settings drawer](../.runtime/consumer-cockpit-disclosure-v1/settings-1440.png). Screenshots show scripted test data, not real-model runs.
- **Test environment and limits:** preliminary runs hit shared-map-store setup/save timeouts and outdated selectors/default-controller assumptions. Final checks use a fresh `MILO_HOME_MAP_STORE` per run and an explicit scripted Luna profile where required; the hybrid-default test is preserved. An older shared-apartment browser check still expects a permanently visible scenario selector; it was not migrated or rerun in this focused validation. The complete browser/backend suites and real-model benchmarks were not run. No measured navigation, reliability, cost or usability improvement is claimed.
